# Changelog

This is the **umbrella changelog** for the Tadah! framework — the curated, cross-module view that users browsing the umbrella repository read first. Each entry is prefixed with the originating module:

- `[core]` — Tadah.CORE (blueprint / config / registry)
- `[mlip]` — Tadah.MLIP module (training / predict / data-balance orchestration)
- `[models]` — Tadah.MODELS (descriptors / cutoffs / basis functions / regressors)
- `[md]` — Tadah.MD (lightweight MD drivers)
- `[hpo]` — Tadah.HPO (hyper-parameter optimisation)
- `[lammps]` — Tadah.LAMMPS (`pair_tadah` LAMMPS plugin)
- `[umbrella]` — this repository (`tadah` binary, docs site, examples)

For the full per-module commit-level log, see each module's own `CHANGELOG.md`.

Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/);
versioning follows [SemVer](https://semver.org/) at the umbrella level.

## [Unreleased]

### Changed
- [umbrella] Restore the Nested Fitting overview diagram and place pinned-weight
  search details in the parameter reference. Use manual-style headings and
  affirmative descriptions throughout the chapter. Promote the block/key
  directory and add an Optimizer section that shows the parser's subblock
  hierarchy. Wrap reference-table prose and parameter names to fit the reading
  column at desktop and mobile widths. Consolidate generated annealing
  defaults, values and descriptions into a two-column reference layout.
- [umbrella] Reorganise Nested Fitting into short syntax, block-key and option
  references, with a runnable first example. Preserve old topic links and keep
  the previous workflow and diagnostic text in a temporary review file. Check
  the reference against the parser and drivers, correcting block placement,
  loss defaults and formulas, coordinate scaling, initialisation, budgets,
  gradients and the current limitations of parallel multistart and PA jitter.
- [hpo] Correct shared annealing help descriptions for plateau tests and
  automatic-jitter calibration. Schema, defaults and numerical behaviour are
  unchanged.

## [1.6.0] — unreleased

A clean-up release for wider testing: one version everywhere, documentation that matches the code, and fixes in every module.

**BREAKING for task files and for command lines with several commands:** a run of `tadah` is one command. Task files are removed: `tadah --task f` and `tadah <command> --task f` are refused as an unknown option, and a `TASK` line in a configuration file, in a file it includes or in a potential as an unknown key at its line; a command line with two commands (`tadah train -c config.train predict -d db.tadah -p pot.tadah`) is refused before either runs. Each `TASK` section of a task file becomes a command of its own (see *Removed*).

**BREAKING for every fit: error scales and priorities replace the weights `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT`.** A fit minimises the sum over its observations of priority × (residual / error scale)², plus the `LAMBDA` penalty: the energy of each structure per atom (`ENORM ATOM`, the default; `ENORM SQRT` per square root of the atoms) with `ESIGMA` in meV/atom (default 1), each force component with `FSIGMA a [b]` in eV/Å (default 0.1 0) and the nine entries of each stress tensor, an off-diagonal one counted twice, with `SSIGMA a [b]` in GPa (default 0.5 0); a priority is a structure's `eweight`, `fweight` or `sweight` times the `WDBFILE` value of its dataset, applied in the fit; `ENERGY false` fits forces or stresses only. The sum is not averaged, and a row that is zero in every column (to rounding) is no observation. `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT`, `SWEIGHT`, `WDBFILE_AUTO` and `EWEIGHT_TEMP` are retired: a potential that has them still loads, with one notice per load that lists them (one LAMMPS warning per `pair_coeff`); a potential that `tadah train` or `tadah hpo` of 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 wrote has `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT` lines, and from 1.3.0 a `WDBFILE_AUTO` line (the published potentials have the first three). Every fit (`tadah train`, `tadah hpo`, the C++ API) refuses a configuration that has one, naming each line and its conversion: `FSIGMA` = 1/sqrt(`FWEIGHT`) eV/Å; `SSIGMA` = 160.2/sqrt(`SWEIGHT`) GPa (exact for the diagonal entries); `ESIGMA` = 1000/(N sqrt(`EWEIGHT`)) meV/atom for cells of N atoms; a weight of 0 is `ENERGY`, `FORCE` or `STRESS false`; an `FWEIGHT` (`SWEIGHT`) line with `FORCE false` (`STRESS false`) converts by its deletion, which the refusal marks; `WDBFILE_AUTO a` multiplies each `WDBFILE` value (1 without `WDBFILE`) by n_d^-a, and `WDBFILE_AUTO 0` converts by its deletion; a retrain does not read a potential's `WDBFILE` line, so the `WDBFILE` values of a potential go on the command line (`--wdbfile`) or into a file that includes it with `CONFIG`; `EWEIGHT_TEMP` has no replacement; under M_KRR implementation 2, whose solve of 1.5.0 read none of these keys, every line converts by its deletion, with `ESIGMA 1000` (or `LAMBDA` × 10⁶ with `ESIGMA 1`). A configuration that set none of them trains without a message to another fit (the defaults were `ESIGMA` 1000/N meV/atom, `FSIGMA` 1 eV/Å and `SSIGMA` 160.2 GPa): such a configuration needs the conversion, and its `LAMBDA` a new check, as it weighs the coefficients against the new sum. Every potential a fit writes has `ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA`, `ENORM` and `ENERGY` lines, which Tadah! 1.5.0 refuses. The stress metrics of `tadah predict -A` and `tadah hpo` are the RMS and MAE of the nine entries in GPa: `PC_SRMSE_GPA` replaces `PC_SRMSE`, which is refused with the conversion, and each row of `tadah predict -A` names its unit (`Stress RMSE (GPa)`, `Energy RMSE (meV/atom)`). `tadah data balance` shifts energies only (`--rescale`, `--threshold`, `--force` and `--stress` are refused with the reason). The documentation page *What a Fit Minimises* gives the rule and every conversion (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for DM_REAM potentials:** the density form `RHOFORM` is mandatory; add `RHOFORM SIGNED` to a DM_REAM potential or configuration written before 1.6.0 to use it unchanged. The embedding is zero-referenced, F(0) = 0 (`EMBF0 ZERO`, the new default): add `EMBF0 FREE` to a DM_REAM potential written by tadah 1.5.0 or earlier to reproduce its energies; every DM_REAM potential carries an `EMBF0` line, which tadah 1.5.0 refuses (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for multi-species BIAS potentials:** with `BIAS true` every atom has one intercept column per `ATOM` species on every path. A potential trained with `BIAS true` and two or more species by an earlier version has one intercept column shared by the species (its LAMMPS energies and forces were wrong with `pair_style tadah` 1.3.0-beta.1 and later, which used one column per species; with 1.2.0 and earlier they matched `tadah predict`); `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` refuse it: retrain it (`tadah train -c` with its training configuration, or with the potential itself: an `M_BLR` potential of those versions records its `DBFILE`, so train from it as it is or replace that line with the `DBFILE` of the retrain, and add a `DBFILE` line to an `M_KRR` one; *Training*, *Retraining from a potential*). Training data whose compositions cannot determine the per-species constants are refused, `FIXINDEX` indices of descriptor weights start at N + 1, and a C++ caller's `BIAS true` model needs `ATOM` (`tadah train` and `tadah hpo` take it from the `DBFILE`) (see *Changed*). Single-species potentials are unchanged, but for the linear `NORM true` ones without `BIAS` (next paragraph).

**BREAKING for linear `NORM true` potentials without `BIAS`:** a `BF_Linear` or `Kern_Linear` potential with `NORM true` and `BIAS false`, of any number of species, is refused by `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` (`The potential cannot be used: NORM true with Kern_Linear and BIAS false. ...`), and `NORM true` with `M_KRR Kern_Linear` needs `BIAS true` to train (`tadah train`, `tadah hpo` at startup), as with `BF_Linear`. The weight conversion of earlier versions had no intercept column for the normalisation's constant: an `M_KRR Kern_Linear` potential (which `tadah train` wrote until this version) and an `M_BLR BF_Linear` one of 1.2.0 or earlier took descriptor component 0 for an intercept, so their energies and forces are wrong; an `M_BLR BF_Linear` one from 1.3.0-beta.1 (written by `tadah hpo` or the API; `tadah train` refused the configuration) dropped the mean correction, so its energies are off by the same constant for every atom and its forces are right. Retrain such a potential with `NORM false`, or with `BIAS true` (see *Changed*, the `BIAS` entry).

**BREAKING for `tadah predict --error`, `tadah properties pairwise --error` and `tadah train --uncertainty`:** uncertainty output is removed, and the three are refused with the reason: the errors of Tadah! 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 were not standard deviations, and no calibrated estimate replaces them. The predictions and the fit are unaffected. A potential that `tadah train` of 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 wrote has an `UNCERTAINTY` line, `true` or `false`, a retired key now: the potential loads with one notice, and a retrain refuses the line until it is deleted (see *Removed*). For C++ callers the models have no weight covariance (Tadah.MODELS, Tadah.MLIP).

**BREAKING for `LAMBDA -1` fits:** the λ and the weights of a `LAMBDA -1` fit change, by orders of magnitude in λ when the fit has many rows of priority 0 (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for `tadah refit` in builds without the compact fitter:** a `tadah` built without `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`, or with it but with `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` off (`-DTADAH_ENABLE_CERES=OFF`), has no compact fitter and refuses `REFIT_MODE compact`, written or the default (a configuration without `REFIT_MODE` asks for it), before it reads the EAM file (`tadah refit: REFIT_MODE compact (the default) needs the compact fitter, which this tadah was built without ...; set REFIT_MODE dense`); it fitted the table in dense mode after a WARNING. Add `REFIT_MODE dense` to every refit configuration of such a build (see *Fixed*; *Installation* says so under `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES`).

**BREAKING for `FIXINDEX` lists that name a weight twice:** such a list is refused, naming the weight (`FIXINDEX 13,13,14 names weight 13 twice; ...`), by `tadah train` and by `tadah hpo` at startup. The repeat was removed with a warning on standard error, although each `FIXWEIGHT` value pins the weight at its position, so `FIXINDEX 1,1` with one `FIXWEIGHT` value trained and `13,13,14` with two ran a search. Name each pinned weight once, with one `FIXWEIGHT` value for each (see *Fixed*).

**BREAKING for scripts that read what `tadah refit --retarget` writes by default:** without `-o` it writes `<input>_retargeted.hpotarget` (`refit_retargeted.hpotarget` for `refit.hpotarget`); it wrote `<input>_edited.hpotarget`, which for `refit.hpotarget` is the file `--edit` writes by default, so of an `--edit` and a `--retarget` the second overwrote the first one's file. Read the new name, or name the file with `-o` (see *Fixed*).

**BREAKING for `tadah refit` outputs:** every `tadah refit` of an EAM file writes the many-body term as a `DM_Embed` component, `DESCRIPTOR eam DM_Embed` ... `ENDDESCRIPTOR` with `TYPEMB @eam` (the ID of the configuration's block when it declares one), whose `DENSITY` block carries the density form (`FORM`) and whose `EMBEDDING` block the embedding reference (`REFERENCE`); it wrote a `DM_REAM` term with `CGRIDMB`, `SGRIDMB`, `AMPGRIDMB`, `CEMBFUNC`, `SEMBFUNC`, `KDEGMB`, `KDEGEMB`, `RCUTMB`, `RCTYPEMB`, `RHOFORM` and `EMBF0` lines. A configuration without blocks still declares `RHOFORM` (and `EMBF0`), which the block's `FORM` (`REFERENCE`) records. `refit.hpotarget` names the many-body parameters by component path, each group after a `# COMPONENT_PARAMETER <path> <rule>` line under a section of its own: `EAM.DENSITY.CENTRES` (was `CGRIDMB`), `EAM.DENSITY.ETA` (`SGRIDMB`), `EAM.DENSITY.AMPLITUDES` (`AMPGRIDMB`; its `NONNEG` rule replaces the `# RHOFORM NONNEG: AMPGRIDMB bounds are non-negative` header), `EAM.EMBEDDING.CENTRES` (`CEMBFUNC`), `EAM.EMBEDDING.ETA` (`SEMBFUNC`) and `EAM.DENSITY.CUTOFF.RADIUS` (`RCUTMB`). `tadah hpo` refuses an hpotarget or HPO configuration that names the legacy keys for such a potential, naming the path (`OPTIM CEMBFUNC: the potential has no CEMBFUNC key: ... the named path EAM.EMBEDDING.CENTRES; ...`): rename the keys of a target written for an older refit, or write a new one with `tadah refit`; `tadah refit --retarget --rhoform` and `--rcutmb` are refused for a target of component paths. A dense `REFIT_BASIS Gaussian` refit fits and writes the pair function and the density with the smooth cutoff `Cut_Poly` at the table's rcut (`RCTYPE2B Cut_Poly`, the density's `CUTOFF TYPE Cut_Poly`; it wrote the hard `Cut_Dummy`), as a component's Gaussian density needs a real smooth cutoff, so its fitted functions change near rcut. DM_REAM potentials still load, train, predict and edit unchanged (see *Changed*; *Refitting*, *DM_Embed*).

**BREAKING for `tadah predict`, for retraining from a potential, for `tadah hpo -c best_pot.tadah`, for `refit --edit` and for `pair_style tadah`:** a potential's key lines are what is needed to use the model -- its settings, its fit, its `LSCALE` and its resolved `ESHIFT` -- not the training data, the keys that prepared it or the settings of the run that wrote it: no potential that `tadah train`, `tadah refit` (`--edit` too) or `tadah hpo` writes has a `DBFILE`, `EFILTER`, `FFILTER`, `ESHIFT_ATOM`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, `ZERO_COM_FORCE`, `WDBFILE`, `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION`, `VERBOSE`, `NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE` or `HEALTH_LOG` key line, nor one of the retired `EWEIGHT_TEMP` and `WDBFILE_AUTO` (first paragraph) (every potential of `tadah train` had its dataset keys, an `M_BLR` potential its `DBFILE`, a `best_pot.tadah` its run's `HPOTARGET` and `VERBOSE`, and its `VALIDATION` when the run had a validation file); its training record, a comment block above the key lines from which no key is read, lists the run's values of them. `tadah predict` ignores the keys that prepared the training data in a potential of any version: it predicts every structure it is given (a potential's `EFILTER` and `FFILTER` left structures out), filters only with `--efilter`, `--ffilter` and `--zero-com-force`, and with `--no-eshift` compares the model with the unshifted references (a potential's `ESHIFT_ATOM` or `ESHIFT_DBATOM` derived a shift from the data predicted); it applies the potential's `LSCALE`, which it replaced with 1.0. A retrain, and `tadah hpo` seeded from a potential, does not read a potential's lines of them, nor its lines of the settings of the run that wrote it (`VALIDATION`, `HPOTARGET`, `VERBOSE`, `NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE`, `HEALTH_LOG`) (a file with `WEIGHTS`, given with `-c` or included with `CONFIG`), with a WARNING naming those of an older potential: a retrain from a 1.5.x `best_pot.tadah` runs at its own `-v` and `NUMERIC` and writes to its own `-o` (it took the potential's `VERBOSE`, `NUMERIC 12` and `OUTFILE`), `tadah hpo -c` of it validates on its own `--validation` file only (it validated on the file the potential's `VALIDATION` line named), `tadah analysis check_bounds -c` of it names its `HPOTARGET` line and needs `--hpotarget` (it took the potential's line); no program that loads a potential reads its run settings (Tadah.CORE's parser stores none), so its `VERBOSE` line sets the Tadah log level of neither `tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah` nor `refit --edit` (`pair_style tadah` ignores it without a message), and `refit --edit` takes `NUMERIC` from its own run only. A retrain works with any number of datasets (a `WDBFILE` of the first fit stopped it: `WDBFILE size (2) does not match number of datasets (3).`) and uses the potential's `ESHIFT`; the run's own -- on the command line, or on a line of a configuration file that includes the potential -- apply and are recorded, and to derive `ESHIFT` from the data of the retrain delete the `ESHIFT` line and give the run `ESHIFT_ATOM` or `ESHIFT_DBATOM` (a toggle line of the potential is not read, with or without its `ESHIFT` line). To retrain from a potential, add the `DBFILE` lines of the retrain to a copy of it (`tadah train` refuses a configuration without `DBFILE`, saying so); give a `tadah hpo` whose `-c` is a `best_pot.tadah` its `DBFILE` lines and its `--hpotarget` and `--validation` files. A potential that has these key lines still loads; remove the `DBFILE` line of an `M_BLR` potential an earlier version wrote before adding the retrain's, as a `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice is refused (see *Changed* and *Fixed*).

**BREAKING for fits given values they ignored:** a negative `LAMBDA` other than -1, a second `LAMBDA` value, a priority of the data below 0 and the forces, stresses, priorities and error scales that `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2` does not fit are refused; a structure with `eweight` 0 has no energy, so the energy shift derived from the data, `EFILTER` and the energy metrics leave it out (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA` and `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`:** `tadah hpo` refuses both at startup, at the line that closes their block, naming `NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA`. NLopt 2.10 runs `LN_NEWUOA` without the `OPTIM` box, so its evaluations and the best potential can lie outside the bounds the configuration sets, and `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` can stall for 10-60 s per step inside NLopt, where neither `MAXTIME` nor a signal reaches it. Move such a study to `LN_BOBYQA`, which takes another trajectory (its `STEP` rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius) (see *Fixed* and *Changed*).

**BREAKING for an `INNER` block that names a global optimiser:** an `INNER` search is a local search (no nested global search). `tadah hpo` refuses at startup, at the `INNER` block's `ENDINNER` line, in every phase and `STRATEGY TYPE` (`PLAIN`, `MULTISTART`, the `PRESTAGE` refinement and a `REFINE` block, the `EXPLORE` block), an MLSL or AUGLAG whose `INNER` block names a global NLopt algorithm (every one whose name starts with `G` and that runs no `INNER` block of its own: `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ISRES`, `GN_ESCH`, the `GN_DIRECT` and `GN_ORIG_DIRECT` forms, `GN_AGS`, `GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`, `GN_MLSL`, `GN_MLSL_LDS`, `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`; `G_MLSL` and `G_MLSL_LDS`, which need one, keep their refusal, `INNER blocks do not nest`), which 1.5.0 ran as a whole-box search from each of MLSL's points or, under AUGLAG, once in place of a local search. Name a local NLopt algorithm in the `INNER` block (the message lists them; `LN_BOBYQA`, `LN_SBPLX`, `LD_LBFGS`, ...; with `PC_LAMMPS` a derivative-free one, which its refusal of a gradient-based `INNER` block lists), or run the global search on its own, as a `PLAIN` run or as the `PRESTAGE` exploration (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for target files whose blocks do not nest:** `tadah hpo` refuses such a file at the line concerned. It refuses a second `OPTIMIZER` block, an `OPTIMIZER` line inside a block and an `END` line that does not close the innermost open block (`ENDOPTIMIZER` inside a `STRATEGY` block, say). It refuses a block the file leaves open, and a word after the key of a line that opens or closes a block. In 1.5.0 a second `OPTIMIZER` block replaced the settings both blocks gave, `ENDOPTIMIZER` closed the `OPTIMIZER` block from inside any block, and a block left open was read as closed. `OPTIMIZER LIB NLOPT` opened the block and dropped `LIB NLOPT`. Write every optimiser setting in one `OPTIMIZER` block at the top level, and each key on a line of its own. Close each block with its `END` line before the block around it (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for files that give a value twice, or more values than a key takes:** a target-file key that takes one value given twice for what it configures (an optimiser, its starting points, the `NOISE`, `RESTART` or `STRATEGY` settings, `LOG_HP`, a top-level key such as `QFLUSH`), a repeated `PC_EMAE`, `PC_ErRMSE` or `PC_ERSQ` line, a key or block of a `tadah refit` stage given twice, and an `RCTYPE`, `RCUT` or list value of a training configuration that no descriptor takes (`CGRIDMB 2.88 3.0` for one centre) are refused. The message names the key, and in a target file the lines. 1.5.0 used the later value, and read only the values the descriptors take (the cutoff of a single descriptor was its largest `RCUT` value), so keeping the later line, or those values, reproduces a 1.5.0 run; a potential that 1.5.0 wrote with values no descriptor takes loads, with a WARNING that names them. `OPTIM ... AUTO RATIO` with `FLOOR` or `GROUP_FLOOR` (ignored by 1.5.0), `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` with `CHAIN_LENGTH AUTO` or `NT AUTO` (0 evaluations per temperature step in 1.5.0) and a grid generator of 0 points are refused too (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for `CERES:LBFGS`, `CERES:WOLFE` and `CERES:NLCG`:** `tadah hpo` refuses the three at startup, in every phase, at the line that closes their block, and `tadah explain` says why: a point outside the `OPTIM` box is no longer evaluated but scored as a failure graded by its distance to the box (below), and Ceres' Wolfe line search, which the three use, shrinks its step to almost nothing at that score. Move such a study to `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS` (L-BFGS within the box), `DLIB:BFGS` or `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`, each of which takes a trajectory of its own; `PARAM max_lbfgs_rank` and `nlcg_type`, which only the three read, are refused (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for a `PRESTAGE` refinement on NLopt's StoGO, DIRECT or AGS:** `tadah hpo` refuses at startup, at the `ENDOPTIMIZER` line and for any `TOPK`, a refinement on `GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`, `GN_DIRECT*`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT*` or `GN_AGS`: these algorithms never read the point they are given, so the refinement searched the whole box from its centre and never used the exploration's candidates (1.5.0 ran such a refinement with `TOPK 1`; with a larger `TOPK` it ran the exploration and then stopped, once that gave two or more candidates, with `MULTISTART > 1 is incompatible with NLOPT:<ALGO> (single-seed global)`). Refine with a local optimiser, which starts at each candidate (the message lists them), or run the global search as the exploration or as a `PLAIN` run (see *Changed*). As an `INNER` search they are refused by the next rule.

**BREAKING for setfl files whose grid line puts the cutoff more than one step past the r tables:** D2_EAM, DM_EAM, `pair_style tadah` and `tadah refit` refuse a setfl file whose r tables (`nr` points `dr` apart) end more than one step before `rcut` (nr x dr below rcut by more than 1e-4 of it) or whose `rcut / dr` exceeds 2147483647, naming the file, line 5 and the numbers. LAMMPS and tadah take a table's last value at every r past its last point, so such a file did not describe its potential up to the cutoff (with `dr` = 1e-300, `tadah train` of a DM_EAM model crashed). The grid of every setfl file of the LAMMPS distribution, those with `rcut` = nr x dr (the cutoff one step past the last point) included, meets the rule; correct `nr`, `dr` or `rcut` of another (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for configuration lines that join values with a comma or a `+`:** such a line is refused when the file is read, naming the line, the key and the separator (`FIXWEIGHT 0.1,0.2`, `MPARAMS 1.0+2.0`, `ATOM Ta,Nb`): `FIXWEIGHT 0.1,0.2` was read as 0.1 without an error, and a name such as `Ta,Nb` stopped `tadah train` later at an error that did not name the separator. Separate values with blanks; the index lists (`FIXINDEX`, `--index`) keep their commas, and paths and option strings keep a comma or a `+` (see *Fixed*).

**BREAKING for bounded gradient searches:** `tadah hpo`'s finite-difference gradients no longer evaluate outside the `OPTIM` box, with either step rule (`PARAM fd_kernel inhouse` or `ceres`); within a step of a bound they are one-sided, so a search whose gradient reaches a bound can take a different path, and a box too narrow for the step, `diff_method 2` (RIDDERS) and finite-difference settings that never take effect (the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` included) are refused at startup (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` and for gradient searches next to failures:** no point outside the `OPTIM` box is evaluated or can be the best: a point `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`, which ignores the bounds, proposes outside it is scored as a failure graded by its distance to the box, so a CERES run takes another path, its best in the box. And a finite difference never mixes a usable loss with a failure, nor a graded failure with a loss of another kind: a gradient takes the side of its stencil whose losses are of the kind of the loss at the point, and a point without such a side has no usable gradient, so a gradient search next to failed evaluations takes another path too (see *Changed*).

**BREAKING for NLopt gradient searches, NLopt's `MAXEVAL` counts every evaluation:** each loss, each finite-difference point and each analytical gradient, as with `DLIB:BFGS` and CERES; an NLopt gradient search made up to about twice `MAXEVAL` evaluations, and an `ANALYTICAL` one up to `MAXEVAL` losses and as many gradients, so with the same `MAXEVAL` such a search now does less: raise `MAXEVAL` to keep the old totals. `GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND` now need `MAXEVAL`; `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or `RESTART` alone is a startup error (see *Changed*).

**`tadah hpo`'s journal trailer says why the search ended:** `Optimiser status` names the stop (`DLIB:BFGS: MAXEVAL reached`, `TADAH:ANNEAL: STOPVAL reached`, `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT: FTOL_REL reached`, NLopt's `MAXEVAL_REACHED -- …`) and `COMPLETED` is no longer written. A job script that looked for `COMPLETED` no longer matches: test `tadah`'s exit status, not the status text (see *Changed*).

**Check datasets converted by older versions:** the lattice vectors are the rows of a dataset's cell block. Datasets converted from a VASP OUTCAR or a CASTEP `.castep`/`.md`/`.geom` file before 1.3.0-beta.1 store the cell of a non-symmetric structure transposed, those converted from a `vasprun.xml` before 1.3.0-beta.1 have wrong positions, and VASP/LAMMPS/CASTEP files that `tadah data write -s` exported from a non-orthogonal CIF/COD entry before 1.6.0 have a transposed cell. `tadah analysis dataset_stats` lists the structures that can be affected; *Dataset Format* in the docs says how to repair them (see *Fixed*). Potentials and `tadah hpo` results fitted to affected structures need to be regenerated.

**Neighbour lists are exact for any cell:** every periodic image within the cutoff is now found for cells thinner than the cutoff in some direction, triclinic cells and atoms outside the cell (before, e.g., a 2-atom bcc cell with a = 3.41 Å at a 10 Å cutoff kept 168 of each atom's 180 neighbours). Training, `tadah predict` and `tadah hpo` change for such structures, and now agree with `pair_style tadah`; retrain potentials fitted to them (see *Fixed*).

**Re-convert VASP data:** the stored stress is the Cauchy stress in eV/Å³, positive under tension, from every reader, and the stored DFT energy is the free energy F, whose gradient the forces are. Datasets converted from a VASP OUTCAR or `vasprun.xml` by Tadah 1.0.0 to 1.5.x hold VASP's pressure sign (a `STRESS true` fit on them fitted the negated stress) and the energy without entropy (OUTCAR) or energy(σ→0) (`vasprun.xml`); re-convert them (*Dataset Format* in the docs says how to tell). CASTEP `.md` data converted by those builds still hold the kinetic stress; re-convert those too (see *Changed*).

### Added
- `[umbrella]` **The documentation page *What a Fit Minimises*** (`fit_objective.rst`): the sum every fit minimises, its observations (the energy per atom or per square root of the atoms, each force component, the nine entries of each stress tensor), the error scales and their units (`ESIGMA` meV/atom, `FSIGMA` eV/Å, `SSIGMA` GPa) and relative parts, `ENORM`, `ENERGY`, the priorities (`eweight`, `fweight`, `sweight` times `WDBFILE`), `LAMBDA`, the remedy for data that spans a wide range (a dataset priority first, then the relative part of `FSIGMA` and `SSIGMA`), and the conversion of every retired key, zero values included. The glossary defines *error scale* and *priority*, the help of the keys links to the page, and *Training*, *Quick Start* and the Nested Fitting pages are written in its terms.
- `[mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **The balance table of a fit.** `tadah train` prints, after its fit, the error scales of the fit and per quantity (energies, forces, stresses), over every dataset and per dataset, the observations, the RMSE and the root mean square of residual / error scale over every entry of priority above 0 (those zero by symmetry included), the share of the minimised sum and the effective parameters each determines, with the prior's row and a one-line legend of each column (at `-v 1` and above), and writes the table at the end of the potential's training record. The rows are of the training data as the fit read it (after `EFILTER`, `FFILTER` and `ZERO_COM_FORCE`; a dataset of `WDBFILE 0` has no entry, and a row without one shows `-`). A dataset's RMSE is the one `tadah predict -A` gives for it with the same filters; the row of every dataset pools the entries of every dataset, and is not the mean over the datasets of `predict -A` and of the `[avg]` metrics of `tadah hpo`. The effective parameters and the prior's sum to the rank, the directions the solve keeps (all of them under `OALGO` 2 and 4). Its lines are at most 78 characters wide for fewer than 10^7 observations per row. `tadah hpo` writes the table of the fit of each new best potential (of its training data, not of the validation data of its loss) into `best_pot.tadah`, computing it only for an evaluation whose loss may make it the best, and prints it once, when the run ends. A fit whose `FIXINDEX` pins every coefficient has none, in both. `tadah refit --edit` leaves the table of its input's fit out of the edit's record. The table costs one pass of the order of n p² over the n rows and p coefficients of the fit, like the solve, and memory of the order of p², of 1024 rows and of three rows per dataset, not a copy of the rows. Documented on the page *The Balance Table*, whose terms *balance table* and *effective parameters* the glossary defines, with *Example 4 - Reading the Fit Balance Table* (`examples/CLI/ex_4_fit_balance`), whose tables `tadah_cli_example_ex_4_fit_balance` checks (the example check compares each `<potential>.balance.txt` an example ships with the table of the potential its run writes, honours `tadah train -o`, and lets a command read the files of the other examples). Tests: `tadah_fit_balance_train`, `tadah_fit_balance_pinned`, `tadah_fit_balance_none`, `tadah_fit_balance_hpo`, `tadah_fit_balance_hpo_killed`.
- `[umbrella]` **The documentation of a retired key** (the blueprint field `retired`, Tadah.CORE): the configuration-keys pages (`docs/tools/read_toml.py`) show a retired key under its label with a warning, **Retired.** and its reason, then *Replaced by* and *Conversion*, and their introduction says what a key marked **Retired** means; the *Command Line Interface* page (`docs/tools/generate_cli.py`) lists an option of a retired key with **Retired**, **Replaced by** and **Conversion**. Tests: `docs/tools/tests/test_read_toml.py`, `test_generate_cli.py`.
- `[hpo]` **`tadah hpo` warns at startup about an `INIT` or `RESTART` block that a search which does not start at its point never reads**, once each, naming the line that opens the block: NLopt's StoGO, DIRECT and AGS (and an `AUGLAG` whose `INNER` search is one) never read the point they are given, so as the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser under `PLAIN` or as the `PRESTAGE` exploration they never evaluate an `INIT` block's points (`the INIT block on line 2: the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L does not start at the point it is given (DIRECT evaluates the centre of the OPTIM box first and then divides the whole box; NLopt never reads that point), so the search never evaluates the block's INIT points. To start the search at them, use an optimiser that starts at the point it is given.`), and those that draw no random numbers either (`GD_STOGO`, `GN_DIRECT`, `GN_DIRECT_L`, the `_NOSCAL` forms, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT*`, `GN_AGS`, `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` in one dimension) ask for round 0's points again in every `RESTART` round (`the RESTART block on line 10: ... so every RESTART round, whatever points it starts from, asks for round 0's points again, which the run's record answers: no round after round 0 evaluates a new point, and the RESTART block's only effect is to end the run once round 0 has had no new best in AFTER_STAGNATION 2 evaluations. ...`); `GD_STOGO`, which obtains gradients and forms them anew in each round, evaluates round 0's finite-difference stencils again, up to `AFTER_STAGNATION` evaluations per round, and the warning says so (`... and their gradients: the run's record answers their losses, but a round forms its gradients itself and evaluates their finite-difference stencils again (GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL): no round after round 0 evaluates a new point, and each round evaluates up to AFTER_STAGNATION 2 of round 0's points again before it ends without a new best.`); `GD_STOGO_RAND` below `POPULATION` 2n + 1 (NLopt's default is 2n) starts every round with the local search of round 0 from the box centre (`... asks first for the points of round 0's first local search again ...`); and on a search that draws random numbers (`GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` in two or more dimensions, `GD_STOGO_RAND` at `POPULATION` 2n + 1 or more) a `RESTART` block's `METHOD` lines are named as never evaluated (`... so no RESTART round evaluates the points of the block's METHOD lines: a round's search differs from round 0's only in the random numbers it draws. ...`). Such runs gave no word of it; the WARNING `INIT chain produced N seeds but ... consumes only the first` is no longer given for such a search, which consumes none. See *Configuration File* (*How many seeds does the optimiser actually consume?*, *RESTART decorator*).
- `[hpo][mlip][umbrella]` **`tadah explain ALGO` lists the optimisers of `tadah hpo`** by library, from the caps registry: whether each is global or local, gradient-based or derivative-free (MLSL and AUGLAG: the local search they run), whether it needs a stop or ignores the `OPTIM` box, and the refused ones (`tadah explain LIB` too, in any letter case). `tadah explain <LIB>:<ALGO>`, or `tadah explain <ALGO>`, prints one optimiser's stops, `PARAM` keys and `STEP` use, or why it is refused (`tadah explain CERES:LBFGS`, which says why it is refused, `tadah explain ld_lbfgs`); an unknown `LIB:ALGO` exits 1 naming what is wrong. This ALGO help (`OptimizerSpec::print_caps`, now to any stream) was reachable only from HPO's tests, `tadah explain ALGO` said `no command or configuration key 'ALGO'`, and it had nothing on the CERES drivers (`(no info)`). MLIP's explain command asks a hook the application sets (`engines::set_explain_hook`), so MLIP does not depend on HPO; a tadah built without HPO says so for `ALGO` and `LIB`. An `ALGO` that is also a command or configuration key prints that text first, then the optimiser's: `tadah explain RANDOM` prints the data key `RANDOM`, then ``RANDOM also names an optimiser of `tadah hpo`:`` and TADAH:RANDOM's help. `tadah hpo --help` names `tadah explain ALGO`. See *Configuration File* (*Algorithms by library*, the `ALGO` row).
- `[hpo]` `tadah hpo` refuses at startup `OPTIM MPARAMS` on a function that reads no such value (`BF_Linear`, `BF_Polynomial2`, `Kern_Linear`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ`, or an index past `Kern_RBF`'s sigma), on the `Kern_Polynomial` degree (an integer) and on a `Kern_RBF` sigma box that is not positive; and at its line `OPTIM` on an integer value (`SBASIS`) or an internal key (`ESTDEV`). See *Nested Fitting*, *Special cases*, and *Regressors*, *Non-linear models* (`OPTIM MPARAMS` is checked at startup).
- `[hpo]` `tadah hpo` refuses at startup `OPTIM RCUT2B` / `RCUTMB` on the cutoff of a `D2_EAM` / `DM_EAM` (inside a `D2_mJoin` / `DM_mJoin`, that descriptor's own value): the descriptor takes the cutoff of its setfl file and refuses another, so every candidate but the `-c` value failed its evaluation (FAILSCORE) and the run ended with status 0. The pre-flight audit and `tadah analysis suggest_bounds` no longer propose searching it. See *Nested Fitting*, *Special cases*.
- `[models]` `Function_Base::mparams()`, the `MPARAMS` a function reads; `shared_setfl()`, a setfl file parsed once per contents for every D2_EAM and DM_EAM of a process; `log_warning_once()`; `D_Base::cutoff_fixed_by()`, what fixes a value of a descriptor's cutoff key (the setfl file of D2_EAM and DM_EAM).
- `[umbrella]` Tests of the *Quick Start* example: `quickstart: in.ml with the shipped pot.tadah conserves energy and keeps the bcc crystal` (test_tadah, needs `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`) runs `docs/source/quickstart/in.ml` with the shipped `pot.tadah` in-process and checks the total-energy drift (below 1 meV/atom), the temperature, the initial pressure, the potential-energy change and the bcc fraction after the run (common neighbour analysis); the 1.5.0 example failed every check. The guard `tadah_quickstart_reproduces_pot` (label `examples`) retrains the potential with `tadah train -c config.train` and checks that it has the keys of the shipped `pot.tadah` and predicts the same energies, forces and stresses on `db.predict`, so the shipped file cannot go stale.
- `[cmake-tools][core][models][mlip][md][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` **`ctest -L headers`: every header compiles on its own.** cmake-tools' `tadah_add_header_check(<target>)` compiles each header of a module's `include/` in a translation unit that includes it twice and nothing else, with what a user of the library gets (for the `tadah` executable, its own include directories and definitions); a header that relies on something included before it, or has no include guard, fails, and the output names it. One entry per module: `tadah.core_headers` (with the generated blueprint header), `tadah.models_headers`, `tadah.mlip_headers`, `tadah.md_headers`, `tadah.lammps_headers`, `tadah.hpo_headers` and `tadah_headers`. The checks are not part of the default build, a new header needs no registration, and a rerun compiles only what changed; each entry builds on the machine's physical cores, or on `TADAH_HEADER_CHECK_JOBS` processes (`-DTADAH_HEADER_CHECK_JOBS=4`). `ctest -R tadah_header_check` tests the function, its refusals included, on a throw-away project. The installed tree is not checked yet (some installed headers need third-party headers that are not installed).
- `[cmake-tools][core][models][mlip][md][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` **`ctest -L text`: the repositories' text carries no personal or process notes.** cmake-tools' `tadah_add_text_check(<name> [ROOT <dir>])` adds the entry `<name>_text`, which reads a repository's text files (in a git clone, those git tracks or would track) and fails on each line that carries a person's initials, a reference to co-workers, a decision attributed to the code's owner, the name or state directory of an AI tool, a note kept outside the repository or filler wording, naming the file, the rule and the line (`testing/tadah_text_check.cmake` lists the rules and the files read). Every module checks its repository, and Tadah.MLIP also cmake-tools; `tadah_text_check_tool` tests the function. The comments, docs and messages were rewritten to state the code's behaviour and reasons in place, and HPO's test names and tags carry no development-plan labels (`hpo_adjoint_pilot`, `test_hpo_adjoint_pilot.cpp`, tags `[adjoint]` and `[failed_masking]`).
- `[cmake-tools][hpo][umbrella]` **Tests of the build configurations without NFP and without Ceres.** `tadah_without_nfp_links` (label `cmake`, in a build with `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`) builds `tadah` as a build without NFP makes it -- its sources, compiled without `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`, linked with `tadah.mlip` and no HPO -- and runs it (`--version`; `--tadah-pc-worker` is an unknown argument; `tadah hpo` is refused naming `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`, below). The `[not-built]` cases of test_tadah run `tadah hpo`, `tadah -h`, `tadah hpo -h` and `tadah explain hpo` in the build without NFP, and pin in a build with it that none of them says hpo is not built. `hpo_configure_without_ceres` (Tadah.HPO) configures HPO without Ceres, from the cache of the build that runs it, and checks that `tadah.hpo` has Eigen; `hpo_configure_without_ceres_preset` checks that a configure from that cache finds what the build found through it (a package found only through `-DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`). `tadah_header_check_cmake_old` and `_minimum` test the CMake check of the header-check tests, and `tadah_catch_skip_cmake_minimum` cmake-tools' refusal of CMake 3.15 for the tests (below). `tadah_cmake_minimum_features` checks that no CMake file of Tadah.MLIP uses a command, option or test property newer than its `cmake_minimum_required()` (it found 17 uses of `cmake --fresh`, `ctest --test-dir` and `string(JSON)`, then two `SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`, 3.16, under the umbrella's 3.15), and that no file under `tests/` declares first more than the tests' minimum, 3.16 (it found seven scripts that declared 3.18). *CONTRIBUTING.md*, *Running tests*, says how to build and test the configurations without Ceres and without NFP, and what each skips (a build without NFP: every Catch case that runs `pair_style tadah`, the *Quick Start* one and the 13 of `test_pair_coeff_reload.cpp` and `test_dimer_pe_atom.cpp`, 17 entries with the two NNFinder cases and `tadah_cli_example_ex_2`; its list named the *Quick Start* case alone, and the labels without an entry there left out `lammps`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **The MPI build (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) has tests of its own, and the whole suite runs in it.** `ctest -L mpi` runs the multi-rank tests through CMake's `MPIEXEC_EXECUTABLE` (four process slots): the rank invariance of `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` (`hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix`), a signal one rank receives (`hpo_mpi_signal_*`, at 1, 2 and 4 ranks) and `tadah hpo` on 1 and 2 ranks (`hpo_mpi_e2e_*`: the `RESTART` and gradient-search refusals, the LAMMPS EAM fast kernels, a global best that rank 1 finds and publishes, the density gates and the density report). The rest of the suite runs as one-rank MPI jobs: test_hpo and test_tadah initialise MPI as `tadah` does, and their LAMMPS cases create their instances on `MPI_COMM_SELF`; 107 of them aborted in an Open MPI 5 build ("MPI_Comm_rank() function was called before MPI_INIT"). Every case that builds a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker pool or asks for that mode is Skipped in that build, which has `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` only and refuses PROCESS (the `pc pool:` cases that build a pool, the two `Two runs in one process` cases under `PROCESS`); in the serial build a worker of test_hpo that a case starts without `SKIP_WITHOUT_PC_PROCESS_WORKERS()` refuses to start, as that build's workers do. `LammpsRunner::create_instance` takes no communicator: it runs every instance on the build's, `hpo_lammps_comm()` (nine cases had passed the serial build's 0, a null communicator under Open MPI, and aborted there); test_tadah's three LAMMPS cases call it so. `hpo_mpi_build_tests_lint`, in every build, fails an HPO test case that builds a pool or writes a PROCESS request in the MPI build's view without that call first, such a pool or request outside every case, and a use of the LAMMPS class other than as a pointer, a reference or a smart-pointer holder (an instance comes from `create_instance`); the HPO changelog lists what it does not see. The serial build runs none of the MPI tests. Docs: *Installation* (*MPI-Parallel Hyperparameter Optimisation*: building LAMMPS and Tadah!MLIP with MPI, the launcher that matches the MPI library, testing the build) and `CONTRIBUTING.md` (*Running tests*). The MPI build is not part of the default presets.
- `[hpo]` **`ctest -L bench`: HPO's LAMMPS-driven cases run in the suite**, four entries, about 8 s together: `hpo_lammps_hessian`, `hpo_adjoint_pilot` and `hpo_bench_pair_tadah` on a Ta potential shipped with HPO's tests (`HPO/tests/data/bench_ta.tadah`, a copy of the *Quick Start* potential; its header gives its provenance), and `hpo_host_e2e` (`HPO_Host`'s analytical gradient, with a `PC_LAMMPS` script among its cases). These 19 hidden Catch cases had no CTest entry, and without `TADAH_BENCH_POT` the first 16 skipped. `TADAH_BENCH_POT` still names another potential; one that cannot be read or run is a failure. The `box_dofs=6` Hessian case pins that its Voigt shear rows vanish on its orthogonal cell, the recorded box-convention issue of the analytic-gradient path. Tests also pin how NLopt's MLSL goes on next to a failure region: it moves on from a rejected local start with every LD_* local search, but ends its whole search, `STOPPED -- no usable gradient`, when an `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*` or `LD_VAR*` local search fails at the edge of the region (see *Configuration File*, *Points without a usable gradient*); AUGLAG stops at a rejected start. See `CONTRIBUTING.md`, *Running tests*.
- `[lammps][umbrella]` **`ctest -L lammps`: `pair_style tadah` on several MPI ranks.** Tadah.LAMMPS's `tadah.lammps_mpi_<case>` entries (labels `mpi` and `lammps`, every NFP build) run an `lmp` built with `-DBUILD_MPI=ON` and ML-TADAH (`-DTADAH_LMP_MPI=`) under the launcher of its MPI (`-DTADAH_LMP_MPIEXEC=`) on 1, 2 and 4 ranks, and on 4 ranks in a `4 1 1` grid whose sub-domains are narrower than the ghost cutoff, and compare each run by atom id with the serial `lmp` (`-DTADAH_LMP_SERIAL=`, optional) or the one-rank run: forces, per-atom energies and virials, the energy and the pressure tensor at step 0 and the trajectory after 100 NVE steps agree to round-off for the *Quick Start* potential (the generic many-body kernel), two species, a D2_Knot5 + DM_REAM potential (the `EAMFAST` kernel and the generic one), a non-linear model (the full-list kernel) and a pair potential; `tadah.lammps_mpi_dimer` compares step 0 of the *Tadah!N2* `DIMER` potential on 512 N2 molecules (newton off) and checks that the per-atom energies sum to the energy in every run. Without the two variables the six entries are Skipped, so the default build skips them besides the three optional-data entries. No ghost-communication defect was found; the entries needed no change to `pair_style tadah` (its `DIMER` per-atom energies are fixed, below: a defect on one rank as well). *Installation* says how to build the `lmp` executable (`--target lmp`), how to run `pair_style tadah` on several MPI ranks (*Running pair_style tadah on several MPI ranks*: which kernels were checked on how many ranks, the `newton` setting each potential needs), and that runs on different rank counts start from the same state only with `velocity ... loop geom` or one data file (`create_atoms` numbers the atoms rank by rank, and the default `loop all` gives velocities by atom ID: the *Quick Start* `in.ml` reaches 415, 419 and 426 K after 100 steps on 1, 2 and 4 ranks, and the same thermo rows for all 1000 steps with `loop geom`); `CONTRIBUTING.md`, *Running tests*, describes the entries.
- `[hpo]` **The vendored ASA keeps its CRLF line endings** (HPO `.gitattributes`, attribute `-text` on `asa.c`, `asa.h`, `asa_usr_asa.h`, `ASA-LICENSE`, `ASA-NOTES`, `ASA-README.txt`), in which the TADAH-PATCH hunks of `asa.c` are written: `git add --renormalize` under `core.autocrlf`, or `git add` under a global attributes file `* text eol=lf`, wrote them with LF endings. Tests: `hpo_vendored_asa_line_endings`, `hpo_vendored_asa_gitattributes`, `hpo_vendored_asa_gate_tool`.
- `[hpo]` **An NLopt-version tripwire**: `kNloptTestedVersion` (2.10.0) records the NLopt the NLOPT refusals (`LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`, `LN_NEWUOA`, `NLOPT_LD_LBFGS_NOCEDAL`) and the pinned NLopt behaviour were measured on, and the test case `the NLopt HPO is built against is the version its NLopt refusals were measured on` fails on another, naming the repros to run again (`HPO/docs/issues/nlopt_*.md`).
- `[models]` Tests: `tadah.models_ream_goldens_native_link`, the DM_REAM goldens in a program compiled and linked with `-march=native` under LTO, as `tadah` is built with `TADAH_MARCH_NATIVE`: MODELS, compiled with the presets' flags, gives it the SIGNED density of earlier versions bit for bit, and one case calls `ReamDensity` from the program's own unit, so a density method moved into a header fails it (see *Changed* for what the bitwise identity covers).
- `[hpo]` Per-dataset `PC_ERMSE` / `PC_FRMSE` validation targets and weights (indexed form).
- `[umbrella]` Docs: a changelog page (this file); Nested Fitting sections on the global loss (a *mean* of loss terms), the inner/outer loops and the step-by-step order of one evaluation; reference entries for `PC_ISOLATION`, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`, `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT`, `BUMPGATE`, `PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy`, `LOSS MAPE`, `GRAD_STRATEGY`, `PARAM fd_kernel` / `tol_gradinf`, `AUDIT` / `AUDIT_SAMPLE` / `HEALTH_LOG`, the `TADAH_*` environment overrides, and the `TADAH_LAMMPS_LIBS` / `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` CMake options.
- `[umbrella]` `ctest -R tadah_catch`: regression tests for the cmake-tools Catch discovery (`tests/catch_discovery`). A canary Catch binary has test names with `[low, high)`, `;`, `\`, commas, lone and quoted brackets, a leading `#`, `~`, `-`, `"` or tab, an `exclude:` prefix, names made only of characters special in a test spec (`-`, `~`, `[]`, ...), a tag alias, a trailing space and CMake syntax; each must be registered once, under its exact name, with a spec that selects exactly that case. A canary name with a hidden twin and `EXTRA_ARGS` containing `--warn` must stop discovery. A fake lister covers 256 names (the exit status wraps to 0), CRLF output, a last line ending in a lone `\r`, empty output, a crash, a count that disagrees with the output and names Catch cannot select. `catch_discover_tests()` is also checked end to end (every option, including list options and the emulator, arrives exactly; re-discovery when the script changes).
- `[umbrella]` `ctest -R tadah_version`: `tadah --version` matches the blueprint, and the CMake version reader is exercised against fixtures.
- `[umbrella]` Docs build: `TADAH_CORE_SOURCE_DIR`, `TADAH_HPO_SOURCE_DIR` and `TADAH_DOXYGEN_XML_DIR` name the CORE and HPO checkouts and the Doxygen XML explicitly, so the site builds offline from any checkout or worktree (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, *Building the documentation*). A missing input stops the build with the list of locations tried.
- `[umbrella]` `ctest -L docs`: the unit tests of the docs build tools (input lookup, `hpo.html` redirect check, configuration-keys page generator) are the CTest entry `tadah_docs_tools`. A run in which cases skip because a tool they need (`node`, Sphinx, PyYAML, `pytomlpp`) is missing is reported as *Skipped*, not *Passed*. `BUILD_TESTING=ON` now needs a Python 3 interpreter.
- `[umbrella]` `ctest -R tadah_removed_option`: configure tests of the rejected `TADAH_BUILD_MPI=ON` (from `-D` and from a `-C` preset) and of the accepted `OFF` and unset option.
- `[umbrella]` `ctest -R tadah_hpo_failed_evaluations`: `tadah hpo` on a tiny problem whose evaluations fail (`tests/cli_failed_evaluations`). With no usable loss (a skip cap no potential meets under `TADAH:RANDOM`; trainings that throw under an eight-dimensional `DLIB:GFS`) it must exit with status 1, say so on stderr and in the journal, count every evaluation as an unusable loss and write no `best_pot.tadah`; a `DLIB:GFS` run whose three starting points fail must end as a successful one.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` ASA: `PARAM Gener_Mov_Avr 0` switches off the adaptive block resizing, as upstream's README documents, so every generated block holds `Gener_Block` states (with `Gener_Block = Gener_Block_Max`, the moving average could still shrink it to 1). Upstream's option check rejected 0; TADAH-PATCH 7 accepts it. Opt-in: the default window (3) is unchanged. *ASA* documents it.
- `[hpo]` `ctest -L e2e`: the hidden host end-to-end groups (`hpo_fixweight_e2e`, `hpo_durable_e2e`, `hpo_embf0_e2e`, `hpo_rcut_e2e`, `hpo_rhoform_e2e`, `hpo_rhogate_e2e`, `hpo_rhobargate_e2e`, `hpo_bumpgate_e2e`) run under CTest, one entry each; they never ran there.
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` Blueprint field `allowed_values`: the only values of a string key, checked at the key's line (`EMBF0` takes `ZERO` or `FREE`); the error names the line of the file and the file (`Invalid value for key EMBF0 on line 6 of config.train: 'frei' (allowed: ZERO, FREE)`). The configuration-keys page lists them.
- `[hpo][models][umbrella]` **Density gates `RHOGATE` and `RHOBARGATE` for DM_REAM searches, and a density report.** `RHOGATE <tol> [R_LO <r>] [NPTS <n>] [SEED ERROR|WARN]` (`RHOFORM SIGNED` only) rejects a candidate whose pair density dips below `-tol * rho_max` on `[R_LO, RCUTMB]`; `RHOBARGATE <bcc|fcc|hcp> <a0> <f_lo> <f_hi> [NPTS <n>] [SEED ERROR|WARN]` (every form) rejects one whose lattice density rhobar(a) is not strictly decreasing on `a0 [f_lo, f_hi]`. Like `BUMPGATE`, a reject happens before training and is a graded band-D failure measured by how far the candidate is from passing; when several gates fail the measures add up and the reject is counted for the first failing gate (SUMMARY `rhogate=N rhobargate=N`, DIAGNOSTICS per-gate lines). A gate is evaluated for every candidate and needs an OPTIM line on a density dimension (`AMPGRIDMB`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRIDMB` or `RCUTMB`): a gate without one cannot act and is an error at its line naming those keys (it was accepted and the `-c` verdict reused); every CONFIG/WARM seed of the INIT chains and of an active RESTART block is checked at startup (`SEED ERROR` stops the run, `SEED WARN` warns). Every run with a bare DM_REAM many-body term now reports the density of the `-c` potential, each WARM seed and the best in `hpo_run.log` (`Density form`, `Density rho(r)`, `Density rhobar`). Their numbers follow the number grammar of every hpotarget key (`NPTS` an integer in decimal digits). Without the keys every evaluation is exactly as before. Docs: *Nested Fitting: configuration* (`nfp_configuration.rst`: a new "Feasibility gates" section, which also documents `BUMPGATE`, and "DM_REAM potentials and the density form").
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` **`tadah refit --retarget --rhoform SIGNED|NONNEG|MONOTONE`** states the density form of the potential an hpotarget searches around. Under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` the rewritten `AMPGRIDMB` boxes are non-negative by the writer's rule and the file gets the parsed `# RHOFORM <form>: ...` header line, where the writer puts it (e.g. an amplitude `2.5e-5` with the group floor `6e-5`: `[-3.5e-5, 8.5e-5]` becomes `[0, 8.5e-5]`), so the result is the file `refit` writes for a potential in that form; a header that names another form is an error at its line. A configuration (`-c`) may not set `RHOFORM` or `EMBF0` for `--retarget` (they have no effect on an hpotarget), and for `--edit` only to the potential's own values (a conflict otherwise, as in `tadah predict`). *Refit* documents both.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` `tadah analysis dataset_stats` lists the structures whose cell block is not symmetric (by 1-based index and label, with their count), on standard output: only in these can a cell stored transposed by an older version be told apart from a correct one. *Dataset Format* has the data-migration note.
- `[models][lammps]` **`DM_Embed`, a scalar EAM-form many-body descriptor composed in a `DESCRIPTOR` block**: independent density and embedding families (Blip, Blip5, Gaussian, degree-3/5 knots, Wendland C2 and C4), spatial cutoffs, local density forms (`FORM`) and references (`REFERENCE`), optional envelopes; `pair_style tadah`'s scalar EAM collapse (`EAMFAST`) covers a single component. See *DM_Embed*.
- `[core][mlip]` **Component blocks in refit configurations.** A `tadah refit` configuration may declare a `DM_Embed` block (the pair family by `REFIT_PAIR_BASIS`; AUTO grids; the block's `FORM` and `REFERENCE`); a component fit has the basis its block declares and does not escalate it (`REFIT_MAX_ESCALATIONS` is refused with a block), and `REFIT_COMPONENT_ITERATIONS` is the iteration limit of its compact fit. Components survive `refit --edit`, `--retarget` (`--set` takes component paths) and the starter targets, which give each component a section of its own and end the knot boxes of a `CUTOFF TYPE NONE` density at its `RADIUS`; `tadah analysis bfunc --type MB DENSITY`, `MB EMBEDDING` and `MB Y` print component curves; a trainer of a DM_Embed potential writes no `RCTYPEMB` line. See *DM_Embed*, *Refitting*.
- `[hpo]` **Named component parameters in `tadah hpo`**: `OPTIM <ID>.DENSITY.CENTRES` and the other component paths, in `ETA` or physical-width coordinates, through bounds, analytical derivatives, candidates, support and density gates and warm starts; a legacy many-body key (`CGRIDMB`, `AMPGRIDMB`, `CEMBFUNC`, ...) of a DM_Embed potential is refused naming the path. The compact component refit reports its own refusals, prints no Ceres progress and warns when it stops at `REFIT_COMPONENT_ITERATIONS` without converging. See *Configuration File* (`OPTIM`), *DM_Embed*.
- `[umbrella]` Unified `DM_Embed` component configuration and workflow documentation: basis normalization, spline degree/order, physical widths, smoothness, named NFP parameters, refitting, editing and both pinned/re-solved coefficient HPO workflows.
- `[hpo][core][mlip]` **`tadah hpo --check` checks a setup without running it.** It reads the training configuration (`-c`), the target file (`--hpotarget`) and the validation configuration (`--validation`) and makes the checks that `tadah hpo` makes before its first evaluation: the working directory, where the run writes its journal, result files and `best_pot.tadah`, the keys and blocks of the target file and its `DUMP` and `BDUMP` directories, the optimiser of each search and its settings, the search space, the LAMMPS scripts of the `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_FAILPROBE` lines, and the training and validation datasets, which it loads. It starts no `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker and does not take the lock of `best_pot.tadah`: a lock file that exists and cannot be opened to read and write stops it, as it stops the run, but a worker that cannot start, or a lock another run holds, stops the run only. It then prints the search parameters with their boxes, the optimiser of each search with its stops, the loss terms, the numbers of training and validation structures and the warnings and hints that `tadah hpo` writes to its journal, `hpo_run.log`, at startup (`tadah hpo --check: config.hpo passed the startup checks, with 2 warnings.`), and exits with status 0. It evaluates no candidate and writes no file. An error ends it with the message and the exit status of `tadah hpo`, after the warnings and hints that came before it, on standard error. `CHECK` is a setting of the run, as `HPOTARGET` is: a `CHECK true` line in a training configuration without `WEIGHTS` makes `tadah hpo -c` a check too, and `tadah train` reads no `CHECK`. A file whose own key lines include `WEIGHTS` is a potential, whose `CHECK` line is not read: `tadah hpo -c` of such a file runs, and a WARNING names the line (`CHECK` then goes on the command line or in a configuration file that includes the potential). No potential has a `CHECK` key line: the training record of a `tadah train` or `tadah refit` potential lists the `CHECK` line of its configuration, and that of a `tadah hpo` potential none. Tests: the `[hpo-check]` cases of test_tadah, `test_hpo_check.cpp` (HPO), `test_blueprint_parser.cpp` and `test_core_user_config_parser.cpp` (CORE), `test_train_engine_datasets.cpp` and `test_key_provenance.cpp` (MLIP).
- `[umbrella]` **The Nested Fitting *Reference* pages list every line, block, key and option of a target file, every optimiser and every `PARAM` key, as `tadah hpo` reads them.** Every documentation build writes them from HPO's registries (`blueprint/hpotarget.toml` and its part files, `blueprint/optimizers_gen.toml`, `blueprint/optimizers_doc.toml`, `blueprint/anneal_keys.toml`): *Top-Level Keys and Blocks* (how a line is read, the eight blocks, the top-level keys, the environment variables that override a key, and each removed key with the message `tadah hpo` gives for it), *OPTIMIZER Block Keys* (the keys of an optimiser and of the `STRATEGY`, `INIT`, `NOISE` and `RESTART` blocks, the `STRATEGY` types and the `INIT` methods), *Targets, Losses and Search Parameters* (the validation metrics, `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_FAILPROBE` with their options, what their LAMMPS script provides (`pair_style tadah` and `pair_coeff * * EXTERN <element> ...`, which take the candidate potential) and the settings of their scripts, the `PC_OBSERVABLE` kinds, the loss functions, the `OPTIM` lines with their `AUTO` options, and the keys `OPTIM` refuses), *Optimisers* (each library and each `LIB:ALGO` with what it is; each `LIB:ALGO` with its status, the keys that stop it, whether it needs a stop, what it reads, how it uses `STEP`, the point it starts at, its random draws and its bounds, or the reason it is refused and the optimisers that replace it), *Annealing PARAM Keys* and *ASA PARAM Keys*. An entry gives the syntax, the values, the default, the blocks the key may appear in, what a repeated line does, the rules that relate it to other keys, each by its title, the environment variable that overrides it, the messages of the checks `tadah hpo` makes on it, each captioned with when it comes, and examples that `tadah hpo` accepts, each later one with a caption; a rule says in words what its message holds, after the message. In the text of an entry the name of a key, block, option or optimiser links to its entry, and an abbreviation is spelled out at its first use on a page. Its code lines are at most 80 characters, and the pages show no horizontal scroll bar at 390, 768, 900, 1024 and 1280 px. Documentation pages cite an entry with the roles `:hpo:key:`, `:hpo:block:`, `:hpo:opt:`, `:hpo:param:` and `:hpo:topic:` (a rule or another entry headed by words, shown by its title), and a name that no entry has, that several entries share or that the registry lists as removed and no entry has fails the documentation build, naming the page and line (and, for a removed name, the hint `tadah hpo` prints), so the pages follow every change to a key. `CONTRIBUTING.md`, *Building the documentation*, describes the generator and the roles.

### Changed
- `[core]` **BREAKING — a configuration is the concatenation of its lines**: the lines of the file a `CONFIG` line names are read in place of that line. The lines of a key of several values add up in line order on either side of an include (`DBFILE eos.tadahdb` above `CONFIG inc.train`, whose file has `DBFILE elastic.tadahdb`, trains on both datasets), up to the number of values the key takes. A key of one value is set once in a configuration file and the files it includes: a second line, in the same file or another, above or below the `CONFIG` line, is refused naming both lines (`Key BIAS is set on line 1 of run.train and on line 2 of inc.train (CONFIG on line 2 of run.train): a key of one value is set once in a configuration file and the files it includes with CONFIG`). A line a run does not read, such as the `EFILTER` line of an included potential, sets nothing, and an option of the command line still takes precedence over the lines of its key. Tadah! 1.5.0 let the lines of an included file replace the lines of their keys above the `CONFIG` line without a message (a `DBFILE` above it was dropped), and a second line of a key of one value replace the first. The Quick Start (Config File) and `tadah explain CONFIG` give the rules and the base of a relative path, the working directory. Test: `Subcommand: train -c reads a CONFIG include in place of its line: a key of one value set twice is refused, DBFILE lines add up`.
- `[core][mlip]` **BREAKING — a configuration file refuses a key of another command** marked *Command key*. A configuration file of `tadah train`, `tadah hpo`, `tadah refit` or a `tadah analysis` command (`-c`, and the files it includes with `CONFIG`) refuses at its line an option of another command (`ANALYTICS` of `tadah predict`, `INDEX` of `tadah data print`) or a key of another command's configuration file (`REFIT_MODE` of `tadah refit`), naming the key, the line, the file and the commands that read it (`Key ANALYTICS on line 18 of config.train is not accepted here: ANALYTICS is the option --analytics (-A) of tadah predict, not a key of a configuration file of tadah train`); a command reads its own keys (`REFIT_MODE` in a file of `tadah refit`). The error names the commands that read the key, not one that refuses it as a retired option (`RESCALE is the option --rescale of tadah analysis bfunc`), and a positional key as the argument of its command (`OPTION is the argument of tadah explain`). The blueprint marks the 79 keys concerned (`command_only`). The settings of a `tadah hpo` run (`HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION`, `CHECK`, `HEALTH_LOG`) stay accepted in a training configuration and go into the potential's training record. A potential that holds a line of a command key (one written by `tadah train` of Tadah! 1.5.0) loads without it (`tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah`, `-c`, a seed of `tadah hpo`), with one warning per key on standard error, or in `log.lammps` under `pair_style tadah` (`WARNING: Key ANALYTICS is ignored: ANALYTICS true on line 40 of pot.tadah is the option --analytics (-A) of tadah predict, not a key of a potential; the line is not read`). Tadah! 1.5.0 accepted every such line, which changed nothing. *Quick Start* (Config File) gives the rule, and the *Configuration File* reference marks each such key (*Command key*) with the commands that read it, and shows no example line for one that no command reading a configuration file reads. Tests: `TaskFactory refuses a key of another command in a configuration file`, `TaskFactory names the commands that read a key of another command, not one that retires it, and a positional key as the argument of its command`, `Subcommand: a configuration file with a key of another command is refused, and a potential's line of one is not read`, `Subcommand: hpo -c refuses a key of another command`.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **BREAKING — `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2` refuses an `OALGO` other than the default**: implementation 2 solves (K + `LAMBDA` sigma^2 I) w = t by Cholesky and reads no `OALGO`; `OALGO 3 0.5`, `OALGO 2`, `OALGO 4` or `OALGO 1` with a rank threshold other than 1e-8 is refused by `tadah train`, and by `tadah hpo` when it starts, naming the line and implementation 1, which takes it, and `tadah hpo` refuses `OPTIM OALGO` with it at startup (`... reads no OALGO: OPTIM OALGO would be ignored. ...`), whose every candidate but the default the fit would refuse. Tadah! 1.5.0 fitted the same potential whatever `OALGO` said, and an `OPTIM OALGO` search changed nothing. Tests: `M_KRR implementation 2 refuses an OALGO other than the default`, `Under M_KRR implementation 2, OPTIM OALGO is refused at startup`.
- `[models][mlip]` **The pre-flight audit judges a radial density channel by its reach** (`AUDIT warn` and `error`, `tadah analysis check_bounds`): a Gaussian channel `exp(-SGRIDMB (r - CGRIDMB)^2)` fails when it is below 1e-6 at every neighbour distance within `RCUTMB`, a blip channel when its support holds none, and the message names the width. Each descriptor of a `D2_mJoin` or `DM_mJoin` line is judged by its own channels, its own cutoff and its other values, and a channel is named by its entry of the whole key (`CGRIDMB(3) = 30`). Tadah! 1.5.0 failed every centre outside the neighbour distances whatever its width: a seed with its Gaussian at the origin (`CGRIDMB 0`, `SGRIDMB 0.2`, about 0.2 at the nearest neighbour of bcc Ta) failed every corner of check_bounds and was refused by `tadah train` and `tadah hpo` under `AUDIT error`. In a joined line, 1.5.0 judged every channel of the line by the check of each descriptor, at the first cutoff. check_bounds computes the statistics of the data at the largest cutoff of its box, which 1.5.0 left at the seed's, and reads a grid generator as the grid it makes. Tests: `Density reach: a Gaussian channel fails only when it is below 1e-6 at every neighbour distance`, `Density reach: a blip channel fails only when its support holds no neighbour distance`, `check_bounds: a Gaussian density centred at the origin passes the corners where its width reaches the neighbours`, `check_bounds: the statistics reach the largest cutoff of the box`, `check_bounds: a grid generator is read as the grid it makes`, `Subcommand: train with AUDIT error trains a Gaussian density centred at the origin whose width reaches the neighbours`, `preflight_audit: each descriptor of a DM_mJoin line is judged by its own channels and cutoff`, `Subcommand: train with AUDIT error judges each descriptor of a DM_mJoin line by its own channels`.
- `[models][mlip][core]` **`tadah analysis suggest_bounds` reads the seed and says where each range comes from.** A Gaussian density centred at the origin gets no `CGRIDMB` line and a width over which the Gaussian drops by a factor of 0.9 to 1/20 between the first two neighbour shells (about 0.04 to 1.1 Å^-2 for bcc Ta); any other centre gets itself ± the FWHM of the neighbour shell nearest to it, and its width that of the shell. Every range holds the seed's value, where a search of `tadah hpo` starts, so that the block passes `tadah hpo` with its seed: where the data alone give a range that does not hold the seed's value, the range is widened to hold it (the data end the range of a cutoff at the 99th percentile of the distances within the seed's cutoff). The rationale of a row names the form and the data (the centres and widths of the Gaussian densities), says "fixed range" and why, or gives the scale and the role of the parameter, and says when the range was widened. Entries of a key with different ranges get their own lines; a value of a descriptor's `TYPE2B` line (the screening parameters of `D2_ZBL`, the exponents of `D2_MIE`) gets a row without an `OPTIM` line, as `tadah hpo` does not search it; each descriptor of a `D2_mJoin` or `DM_mJoin` line gets the rows of its own values, numbered as the whole key numbers them (the cutoff of the second descriptor is `OPTIM RCUT2B (2)`); a grid generator is read as its grid; the table names an entry of a key as an `OPTIM` line numbers it, from 1 (`D2_Blip.CGRID2B(1)`), and the table and the block call the ranges suggestions, to be checked before use; the block is headed with the command that writes it. Tadah! 1.5.0 proposed the first shell's ranges whatever the seed (a width up to 58.8 Å^-2 for a Ta seed at the origin, about exp(-298) at r_min), ended the range of a cutoff below the seed's cutoff, headed every range "data-driven", numbered the entries of a key from 0 (`CGRID2B[0]`), gave a `D2_ZBL` seed one line `OPTIM TYPE2B (1-7)`, gave each descriptor of a joined line every value of the line, read `LIN` as a number (an `ERROR` line, exit status 0, `OPTIM CGRID2B (1-4)` for 7 blips) and named `tadah hpo --suggest-bounds`, an option no command has. Tests: `bound_suggester: a Gaussian density centred at the origin gets no CGRIDMB row and a width from the drop between the first two shells`, `Gaussian density suggestions are centred on the seed's centre`, `bound_suggester: a shell-centred Gaussian density gets a CGRIDMB row from r_min`, `bound_suggester: every range holds the seed's value`, `bound_suggester: a fixed range is named as such, and the block names tadah analysis suggest_bounds`, `print_suggestions: writes a header and one row per suggestion`, `print_suggestions: the heading names every kind of rationale`, `analysis suggest_bounds reads a grid generator as the grid it makes`, `write_optim_block gives one line per run of entries with the same range`, `bound_suggester: a value of a TYPE2B line gets a row without an OPTIM line`, `analysis suggest_bounds gives each descriptor of a D2_mJoin line its own values and OPTIM indices`, `analysis suggest_bounds gives each descriptor of a DM_mJoin line its own channels`, `Subcommand: the block of analysis suggest_bounds passes hpo --check with its seed`.
- `[umbrella]` **BREAKING — one command per run.** A command line with two commands, `tadah train -c config.train -o a.tadah predict -d db.tadah -p a.tadah` or the same command twice, is refused before either runs, naming them (`tadah runs one command per run: the command line gives 2, tadah train and tadah predict (each runs as a tadah command of its own)`, exit 105); they ran one after the other in one process, the last command's `-v` applying to both. So is every other form of a second command: a command typed again after another (`tadah data print -d db.tadah data split ...`, `tadah predict ... analysis cutoff ... predict ...`) and a second command that lacks an option (`tadah predict -d db.tadah -p a.tadah predict -p a.tadah`, `tadah explain train.verbose train`), which Tadah! 1.5.0 refused as an argument no command takes (`The following argument was not expected: data`, exit 109) or for what the second command lacked (`At least 1 option from [-t,--task,-s,--structure,-d,--dbfile] is required`, exit 106), and a command that groups others given alone after a command (`tadah predict -d db.tadah -p a.tadah analysis`), where 1.5.0 ran the first command and exited 0. The commands are counted from the words of the line before the options of any command are checked, each option taking the values it needs, whatever they are, and further values up to the next option or command name (`tadah train -c predict` reads the file `predict`), so the refusal comes first where the first command lacks an option (`tadah predict -p x train -c y`: 2 commands, exit 105; Tadah! 1.5.0 printed `At least 1 option from [-t,--task,-s,--structure,-d,--dbfile] is required`, exit 106) and names every command (`tadah data print -d a split -d b data`: 3). A command that groups others given alone, `tadah data`, `tadah properties` or `tadah analysis`, is refused naming its commands (`tadah data takes one of its commands: balance, convert, dedup, merge, print, sample, split or write`, exit 105); the three exited 0 and printed nothing. The three take no option: they had `--task`, and `data` and `properties` `--verbose`, with which a run failed (`tadah data -v 2`, `tadah data -v 2 print -d db.tadah` and `tadah properties -v 2 pairwise ...` exited 1 with `TaskFactory::createTasksFromInput - taskInput.getTaskName() is not a valid task: DATA`, or `PROPERTIES`). `tadah` alone takes `-h` and `--version`. An argument that no command takes is named first, with exit status 109, also when the command line lacks an option (exit status 106), gives an option without one it needs (107) or with one it excludes (108): `tadah train --foo` printed `Exactly 1 option from [-t,--task,-c,--config] is required` (exit 106) and prints `The following argument was not expected: --foo` (exit 109), and `tadah predict -d db.tadah --foo` exited 107 (`--dbfile requires --potential`). *Command Line Interface* gives the rule of one command per run. Tests: `Subcommand: a command line with two commands is refused before either runs`, `Subcommand: a second command is refused in every form, also where CLI11 takes its name for an argument`, `Subcommand: the commands of a command line are counted from its words`, `Subcommand: a command that groups others is refused without one of its commands`, `Subcommand: an argument no command takes is named before what the command lacks, needs or excludes`.
- `[mlip]` **BREAKING — `tadah predict`, `tadah properties pairwise` and `tadah analysis descriptor` take no key that is not one of their options**: a program's run that gives one (a key of the model such as `RCUT2B`, a `DESCRIPTOR` block, `WDBFILE`, `EMBF0`, `RHOFORM`) is refused before anything is read, naming it (`tadah predict takes no key RCUT2B: the model is the potential's, as it was fitted (its keys are those of the potential file)`). A `TASK predict` section of a task file merged its keys over the potential's: `RCUT2B 4.0` there changed the cutoff of the model, and so the predictions, without a word.
- `[core]` **An error of a line of a file names the key, the line and the file**, for a configuration file, a file it includes with `CONFIG` and a potential: an unknown key, with the closest key (`Invalid key FOOBAR on line 3 of config.train (closest key: FORMAT)`); a value the key's types do not take, with the key's schema, format and examples after it (`Invalid value for key RCUT2B on line 3 of config.train: 'abc'`; a wrong number of values alike); more values than the key takes (`Too many values for key MODEL on line 2 of config.train: MODEL takes at most 3, and its lines read so far give 4`); a block that is not closed (`OPTIMIZER on line 2 of config.train is not closed; expected ENDOPTIMIZER`); and a `CONFIG` line whose file does not open (`Failed to open config file: other.cfg (CONFIG on line 2 of config.train)`) or includes a file of the chain again. Tadah! 1.5.0 named neither the line nor the file of the line: `Invalid key: FOOBAR`, `Invalid schema for key: RCUT2B`, `Exceeded capacity for key: MODEL`, `OPTIMIZER block is not closed with ENDOPTIMIZER`, `Failed to open config file: other.cfg`. A configuration file that `-c` names and that cannot be opened is reported as `Failed to open config file: <file>`.
- `[mlip]` At `-v 2` the `INFO` line of a run names the command as it is typed: `Running tadah train`, `Running tadah data sample`. It was `Executing task: TRAIN`.
- `[core][mlip][hpo][lammps][models][umbrella]` **BREAKING — error scales and priorities replace `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT`** (the first paragraph of this release). A fit minimises the sum over its observations of priority × (residual / error scale)², plus the `LAMBDA` penalty, with one rule on every path: `tadah train`, every evaluation of `tadah hpo` (its analytical gradient included) and the C++ API (`M_BLR` and `M_KRR` `train`, the fit of precomputed descriptors too). The keys `ESIGMA` (meV/atom, default 1), `FSIGMA a [b]` (eV/Å, default 0.1 0), `SSIGMA a [b]` (GPa, default 0.5 0; the nine entries of the tensor, its squared Frobenius norm), `ENORM ATOM|SQRT` and `ENERGY true|false` (Tadah.CORE); an error scale of a quantity a fit leaves out other than its default, an error scale not above 0 and all of `ENERGY`, `FORCE` and `STRESS` false are refused; at `-v 2` every fit names its error scales and which are defaults. A potential writes their lines, and `tadah predict` does not read them (a potential trained with `FORCE true` predicts without `-F`). `WDBFILE` is a priority applied in the fit, not a transform of the data, and `tadah hpo` searches `ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` and `WDBFILE` in every evaluation; it refuses at startup a search that cannot change the fit (the error scale of the only quantity, or `WDBFILE` of the only training dataset, with `LAMBDA` 0 or -1) and one under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL`, and warns when every error scale, or every dataset's `WDBFILE`, is searched with `LAMBDA` 0 or -1 or with `OPTIM LAMBDA`; a `WARM` seed without the line of a searched key (a potential of an earlier version has no `ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA` or `SSIGMA` line) does not have its value, and `WARM_ON_MISMATCH` applies.
