The retired keys: every load of a potential works with one notice (`tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah` once per `pair_coeff`, `tadah analysis`, `tadah properties`, `tadah refit --edit`, which keeps their lines, and a `tadah hpo` `WARM` seed), and every fit refuses them with the conversion, `tadah hpo` and `tadah hpo --check` when they start, a `--validation` file at its line, `OPTIM` of one at its line; `tadah explain EWEIGHT` (and the others) prints the conversion, and `--wdbfile-auto` and `--eweight-temp` give it. A force zero by symmetry, whose row of the design matrix is zero to rounding, is no observation, so a rigid translation of the atoms changes no fit. The stress metrics of `tadah predict -A`, `PC_SRMSE_GPA` (global and per dataset), the journal, the `RESULT` rows and `--skip-above` are in GPa over the nine entries; `PC_SRMSE` is refused with the conversion (about ×160.2: 130.8 for a residual on the diagonal only, 185.0 for a shear residual only). The starter target of `tadah refit` weights the validation RMSEs by the default error scales (`PC_ERMSE 0 1`, `# PC_FRMSE 0 100`, `# PC_SRMSE_GPA 0 4`). Docs: *What a Fit Minimises*; the module changelogs give the details and the tests.
- `[mlip]` `tadah explain` shows a role of a help text (a link to a page of the documentation) by its text.
- `[models]` **BREAKING — `LAMBDA` takes one value: 0 (no penalty), a positive ridge penalty, or -1, which requests the evidence approximation of `OALGO 3`; every fit refuses another value before it solves.** A negative value other than -1 is refused (`LAMBDA -2: only LAMBDA -1 requests the evidence approximation, which OALGO 3 runs; another negative value requests nothing. ...`): 1.5.0 fitted it as `LAMBDA 0` under `OALGO 1`, 2 and 4 and ran the evidence approximation with it under `OALGO 3`. A second value is refused, naming the rank threshold of `OALGO 1` and 3, `OALGO`'s second value (`LAMBDA 0 1e-12: LAMBDA takes one value, ...; a second value is not read. The rank threshold of OALGO 1 and 3 is OALGO's second value (OALGO 1 1e-12, OALGO 3 1e-12).`): 1.5.0 ignored it, although the help of `LAMBDA` called it the rank threshold. `LAMBDA -1` under another `OALGO` names the configuration's, and `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2` refuses it naming implementation 1. A potential with a second value loads (prediction does not read `LAMBDA`); a retrain from it is refused.
- `[models][hpo]` **BREAKING — every fit refuses an `OALGO` that requests nothing, before it solves.** `OALGO`'s second value `M`, the rank threshold of `OALGO 1` and 3, sets a threshold for 0 < `M` < 1; below 0 `OALGO 1` takes the machine precision of LAPACK, and at 0 or below `OALGO 3` leaves out only a singular value of 0. Refused, naming the line and the values it takes: under `OALGO 1` an `M` of 0 or of 1 or more, which DGELSD replaces by the machine precision; under `OALGO 3` one of 1 or more, which left out every singular direction (1.5.0 wrote a potential whose coefficients were all 0); a second value under `OALGO 2` and 4, which 1.5.0 ignored; and a first value other than 1 to 4. `tadah hpo` refuses them when the run starts, and refuses at its line an `OPTIM OALGO (2)` box that is not inside (0, 1). A potential with such a line loads; a retrain from it is refused.
- `[models]` **`OALGO 3` with `LAMBDA -1` and an intercept column says what the evidence approximation needs**: `BIAS false`, or every intercept weight pinned with `FIXINDEX` and `FIXWEIGHT` (`LAMBDA -1 requests the evidence approximation, which only OALGO 3 runs; OALGO 3 penalises every weight alike, and the model has 1 intercept column(s) ...`). 1.5.0 refused it too, saying `use OALGO=1 or OALGO=2 instead`, which refuse `LAMBDA -1`.
- `[mlip]` **BREAKING — a priority of the training data is a number >= 0.** A dataset file whose priorities line (`eweight fweight sweight`) has a negative value is refused as it is read, naming the file, the structure, its label and the value (`... structure 'second': eweight '-1' is negative: a priority is a number >= 0 ...`); a priority that is negative or not finite in data given through the C++ API is refused by the fit, `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2` included, before anything else reads the priorities, naming the structure and its dataset (with `BIAS true` too: such a priority is not taken for intercepts the data cannot determine). In 1.5.0 two negative weights of a row cancelled (`EWEIGHT -1` with every `eweight` -1 fitted as if both were 1), and one was refused as a value of the design matrix a descriptor gave. A negative `WDBFILE` value is refused, naming the dataset; 1.5.0 applied it.
- `[mlip]` **BREAKING — a structure with energy priority 0 (`eweight` 0) has no energy of its own** (its energy line, which the dataset format needs, holds a placeholder), and what reads energies leaves it out, as training does: `ESHIFT_ATOM` and `ESHIFT_DBATOM` (whose shift of a species that only such structures contain is 0, with a WARNING naming it, while the other species are fitted), `EFILTER` (it is not dropped for its energy), the energy metrics (the Energy lines of `tadah predict -A`; `PC_EMAE`, `PC_ERMSE`, `PC_ErRMSE` and `PC_ERSQ` of `tadah hpo`) and the energy statistics of `tadah analysis dataset_stats`. `tadah predict -A` does not measure a dataset whose structures all have energy priority 0 (`nan`, left out of the mean, and a note names it), and prints `nan` Energy lines, without a refusal, when no dataset has an energy; `tadah hpo` does not measure such a validation dataset either (`nan`, left out of the averages and of the analytical gradient), and refuses at startup a run in which no validation dataset has an energy, or in which an indexed `PC_ERMSE` gives such a dataset a weight above 0. `FFILTER` leaves out a structure with force priority 0, and `dataset_stats` its force magnitudes. In 1.5.0 the placeholders were counted: an `ESHIFT_ATOM` shift averaged a placeholder energy in, and a placeholder outside the `EFILTER` window dropped the structure with its forces.
- `[models][mlip]` **BREAKING — M_KRR implementation 2 (`MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2`) weighs the energies per atom over `ESIGMA` and refuses the forces, stresses and priorities its solve ignores.** It solves (K + `LAMBDA` σ² I) w = t, σ = `ESIGMA` in eV, so its `LAMBDA` weighs against the energies per atom over `ESIGMA` as in implementation 1 (1.5.0 weighed them in eV: a 1.5.0 configuration fits as it did with `LAMBDA` times 10⁶, or `ESIGMA 1000`). `FORCE true`, `STRESS true`, `ENERGY false`, `ENORM SQRT`, an `FSIGMA` or `SSIGMA` other than its default, a `WDBFILE` value other than 1 and a structure whose `eweight` is not 1 are refused before the fit, each naming implementation 1, which fits them. 1.5.0 fitted the same model with or without them.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `tadah hpo` refuses when it starts what every evaluation would refuse**, with the message of the fit: when its evaluations fit the model, a `LAMBDA` every fit refuses (a negative value other than -1, a second value, -1 under an `OALGO` other than 3 or under `M_KRR` implementation 2), `OALGO 3` with a `LAMBDA` other than 0, or with `OPTIM LAMBDA`, and an intercept column that `FIXINDEX` does not pin (`BIAS true`), and under implementation 2 `OPTIM` of `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` or `WDBFILE`; whatever its evaluations fit, an `M_KRR` implementation other than 1 and 2; once the training data are read (`tadah hpo --check` reads them too), a priority below 0 and what implementation 2 ignores. A run whose evaluations fit nothing (no training data, or `FIXINDEX` pinning every weight) reads no `LAMBDA`. In 1.5.0 `LAMBDA -1` under `OALGO 1`, 2 or 4, a negative `LAMBDA` under implementation 2, an implementation other than 1 and 2, `OALGO 3` with `BIAS true` and a `LAMBDA` other than 0, and a negative weight of a quantity the fit fits failed every evaluation, and the run ended without a potential (with `FIXINDEX` pinning every weight, `best_pot.tadah` carried the implementation other than 1 and 2); the other cases ran to their end, a negative `LAMBDA` fitted as described in the `[models]` entry and the values implementation 2 ignores without effect. The *Reference* pages of Nested Fitting give each message.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `OPTIM` refuses at its line a box of `LAMBDA` that reaches below 0, `OPTIM LAMBDA` when the training configuration has `LAMBDA -1`, and `OPTIM` of `LSCALE`, `ESHIFT`, `ESHIFT_ATOM`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, `EFILTER`, `FFILTER` and `ZERO_COM_FORCE`**, which prepare the data once, when the run starts. `OPTIM ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` and `WDBFILE` act in every evaluation (the error-scales entry). 1.5.0 fitted a candidate `LAMBDA` below 0 as `LAMBDA 0` under `OALGO 1`, 2 and 4 and with the evidence approximation under `OALGO 3`, and applied a candidate weight in the first evaluation of each worker thread only, which failed when the weight was below 0. It accepted `OPTIM` of `LSCALE`, `ESHIFT`, `EFILTER`, `FFILTER`, `WDBFILE`, `WDBFILE_AUTO` and `EWEIGHT_TEMP`, and every evaluation trained on the data as the run had prepared them, whatever the candidate value.
- `[hpo]` **The evidence approximation (`LAMBDA -1`) is reported once per run**: a fit stopped at its iteration limit gives its WARNING once, in the journal (`hpo_run.log`), an evaluation writes nothing of the evidence approximation on standard output (see the `[models]` entry), and a candidate whose targets the evidence approximation refuses fails its evaluation, with the refusal in the journal once. 1.5.0 gave no WARNING, printed the table of iterations of every evaluation on standard output at the default `VERBOSE 1`, and scored `FAILSCORE` for targets that made alpha and beta infinite as a non-finite loss.
- `[hpo]` **The help and some messages of `tadah hpo` in full words.** `tadah explain` describes how an optimiser uses `STEP`, the options of `ASA:ASA` and the `PARAM` keys of the annealers without shorthand (`per-dim`, `rhobeg`, `an OUTER STEP`), and says that `LADDER_ADAPT` needs 3 replicas or more. The Ceres refusals and the startup WARNING of `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` name Ceres' line-search solver, and the DIAGNOSTICS section of `hpo_run.log`. The hint of the removed `INIT_INCLUDE_CONFIG` and `INCLUDE_CONFIG` states the rule, ``removed. The values of the training configuration for the search parameters are a starting point only when the INIT block has a `METHOD CONFIG` line.``, and a target file with `PC_OBSERVABLE` and `PC_LAMMPS` lines is refused with `... PC_OBSERVABLE and PC_LAMMPS lines are mutually exclusive. Remove one set of lines and rerun.`
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — a count of starting points is at most 1000000, and `NOISE CALIBRATION` at most 2147483647 evaluations**, refused at the line: the `K` of a `METHOD` line, `K_TOTAL` and `STRATEGY TOPK` (`INIT METHOD RANDOM K must be an integer from 0 to 1000000, got '1000001'`). 1.5.0 read each count up to 18446744073709551615, and a large one stopped `tadah hpo` with `HPOEngine error: vector::reserve`, or the run when it started for `NOISE CALIBRATION`. A chain of `METHOD` lines draws its points in a time that grows with their number, not with its square.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — every key of the target file that takes one value is set once for what it configures.** A key given twice for one optimiser (in one block, in a repeated block, or in `OPTIMIZER`, `STRATEGY` and `REFINE`, which configure the same optimiser), for its starting points (also across the `INIT` blocks inside `OPTIMIZER`, `STRATEGY` and `REFINE`), or in the `NOISE`, `RESTART` or `STRATEGY` settings, a second `LOG_HP`, a `STEP <KEY>` override given twice for the same indices, and a top-level key given twice (`QFLUSH`, `OUTPUT`, `BOUTPUT`, `DIGITS`, `DUMP`, `BDUMP`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, `BUMPGATE`, `PC_ISOLATION`, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`) are refused at the second line, naming the first: `ALGO is set on line 7 in the STRATEGY block and again here in the REFINE block; both blocks configure the same optimiser, so each key is given once`; `SEED is set on line 5 in the INIT block inside OPTIMIZER and again here in the INIT block inside STRATEGY; both blocks configure the same starting points, so each key is given once`; `QFLUSH is set twice (first on line 3); each key is given once`. Lines that add are read as before: `METHOD` lines add to their list, `OPTIM`, `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` lines add, `PC_ERMSE` and `PC_FRMSE` lines of other datasets add, and `STEP <KEY>` overrides of other indices refine the block-wide `STEP`. `FAILSCORE`, `LOSS` and `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` (one key with its alias `MAXTIME`) are set again only after a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line has taken the first value, and refused otherwise (`LOSS is set twice (first on line 3) and no PC_LAMMPS or PC_FAILPROBE line between takes the first; each key is given once`): each such line takes the value given above it, the run-wide uses take the last line (the validation metrics' loss, the ceiling of every loss, the `PROCESS` worker deadline), and `--varloss`, `--failscore` and `--maxtime` give a `PC_LAMMPS` line its own values. A `tadah refit` stage takes one `OPTIMIZER` block with at most one `INNER` block and sets each of their keys once too, naming the key as written, `MAXEVAL` and its alias `MAXITER` being one key: `REFINE: MAXEVAL is set twice in the OPTIMIZER block (as MAXEVAL and as MAXITER, its alias); each key is given once`; a second `OPTIMIZER` or `INNER` block and a repeated `ENABLED` or `MAX_ADD` are refused. 1.5.0 used the later value without a message (for `LOG_HP`, with a warning; a second `OPTIMIZER` block of a `tadah refit` stage set again the keys it gave, and a second `INNER` block replaced the first), so keeping the later line of each key gives a 1.5.0 file the settings it ran with.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — a repeated top-level `PC_EMAE`, `PC_ErRMSE` or `PC_ERSQ` line is refused** at its line, naming the line of the first (`Error on line 7: PC_EMAE: repeated declaration (first declared on line 6)`), as a repeated global `PC_ERMSE` or `PC_FRMSE` line is; the message of a repeated global `PC_ERMSE` or `PC_FRMSE` line names the first line too (it said `repeated global declaration or mixed global/indexed forms`). 1.5.0 used the later line, so keeping it alone gives a 1.5.0 file the loss it ran with.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `OPTIM ... AUTO` refuses `FLOOR` and `GROUP_FLOOR` on a geometric box**, given as `RATIO <f>` or the default `RATIO 2` of a positive scale key such as `SGRID2B` (`OPTIM WATOM: FLOOR and GROUP_FLOOR set the smallest half-width of an AUTO FRAC box; AUTO RATIO makes the geometric box [x0/f, x0*f], which they do not change, so they are given with FRAC <p> only`). 1.5.0 accepted them and ignored them (`AUTO RATIO 2 FLOOR 5` gave the box of `RATIO 2` alone), so a line without them keeps a 1.5.0 search space.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` refuses `PARAM CHAIN_LENGTH AUTO` and `PARAM NT AUTO`** at startup (`TADAH:ANNEAL_PA: PARAM CHAIN_LENGTH must be a number (got 'AUTO'; AUTO is accepted by TADAH:ANNEAL only)`). `AUTO` derives these values from `MAXEVAL` in the serial `TADAH:ANNEAL` only; without the line `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` takes its defaults, 50 and 5. In 1.5.0 `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` accepted `AUTO` and gave every temperature step 0 evaluations, so the run stopped after its initialisation. The help text and the key tables offer `AUTO` for `TADAH:ANNEAL` only.
- `[models]` **BREAKING — every `RCTYPE`, `RCUT` and list value of a training configuration belongs to a descriptor, or the configuration is refused, naming the line, the key and both counts** (`TYPEMB DM_mBlipEnv takes 1 CGRIDMB value, but CGRIDMB has 2 values: TYPEMB DM_mBlipEnv 0 1 1 7 7 Ta Ta`). Each descriptor of a `TYPE2B` or `TYPEMB` line takes one `RCTYPE2B` and `RCUT2B` (`RCTYPEMB`, `RCUTMB`) value and the list values its SIZE parameters ask for (`CGRID2B`, `SGRID2B`, `KDEG2B`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRIDMB`, `AMPGRIDMB`, `CEMBFUNC`, `SEMBFUNC`, `KDEGMB`, `KDEGEMB`), in the order of the TYPE lines, for a single descriptor and a `D2_mJoin` or `DM_mJoin` line alike. More values are refused as fewer are, and so is a list key of the line's type that no descriptor of the line reads (`TYPE2B D2_LJ takes 0 CGRID2B values, but CGRID2B has 3 values: TYPE2B D2_LJ Ta Ta`). In 1.5.0 the extra `CGRID*`, `SGRID*`, `CEMBFUNC` and `SEMBFUNC` values were dropped without a message (`CGRIDMB 2.88 3.0` trained with `CGRIDMB 2.88` and wrote it). The extra `RCTYPE*`, `RCUT*`, `AMPGRIDMB` and `KDEG*` values were written into the potential and not read, except that the cutoff of a single descriptor was its largest `RCUT` value. Removing the values the line does not take reproduces a 1.5.0 potential; for a single descriptor, the largest `RCUT` value stays. A potential with such values loads (`tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah`, `tadah train -c` and `tadah hpo -c` on the potential): each key gets one WARNING that names the values, which no descriptor reads, and says the potential works unchanged without them (`WARNING: Part of RCUT2B (6) is ignored: TYPE2B names 2 descriptors (D2_Blip, D2_Knot5), which take its first 2 values; the potential works unchanged without it`). Training from such a potential leaves them out, and the potentials it writes are without them; the `OPTIM` indices of `tadah hpo -c` address the values kept (a single descriptor's `RCUT2B 4 6` keeps 6, index 1), and an index past them is refused naming the values left out. A single descriptor reads every value of the list keys its TYPE line gives a SIZE for, so in a potential more of those values than the SIZE parameter asks for are refused, as in a configuration; 1.5.0 read them all.
- `[models][core]` **BREAKING — a grid generator whose values cannot make a grid is refused, naming the key and the generator.** `LIN <n> <start> <stop>` and `GEOM <n> <start> <stop>` take a whole number n from 1 to 1000000 and two finite endpoints, and `GEOM` takes endpoints above 0 (`CGRID2B: Invalid grid specification: the number of points of 'LIN -3 2 4' must be a whole number from 1 to 1000000`); a generator whose points are not all finite is refused too (`LIN 3 -1.7e308 1.7e308`, whose stop minus its start is beyond the largest number). A generator of 0 points (`LIN 0 2 4`), which 1.5.0 expanded to no value, is refused; removing it reproduces a 1.5.0 potential (a descriptor that takes no value of the key needs none). In 1.5.0 `LIN -3 2 4` stopped with `cannot create std::vector larger than max_size()`, and `GEOM 3 2 -4` gave NaN values. A generator that its list ends before, which only the C++ API could pass (a configuration or potential file with one is refused when it is read), was read past the end of the list. The help of `CGRID2B`, `SGRID2B`, `CGRIDMB` and `SGRIDMB` names these two generators (its format line writes `<generator>`; it named `LOG`, `L` and `U`, which do not exist) and states how the values of a joined line are split among its descriptors.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — an `INNER` search is a local search: an `INNER` block that names a global optimiser is refused at startup**. The search an `INNER` block names is the local search NLopt's MLSL starts at the points it samples in the `OPTIM` box, and AUGLAG, which HPO runs without a constraint, runs once from its own starting point. A global NLopt algorithm there (each whose name starts with `G` and that runs no `INNER` block of its own: `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ISRES`, `GN_ESCH`, the six `GN_DIRECT` forms, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L`, `GN_AGS`, `GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`, `GN_MLSL`, `GN_MLSL_LDS`, `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`) searched the whole box from each of those points (under AUGLAG, once: the global search under another name), and is refused before any data is loaded, at the `INNER` block's `ENDINNER` line, in every phase and `STRATEGY TYPE` (the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser under `PLAIN` and `MULTISTART`, the `PRESTAGE` refinement at the `STRATEGY` level or in a `REFINE` block, the `EXPLORE` block): `Error on line 10: the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS cannot run NLOPT:GN_CRS2_LM as its INNER search: an INNER search is a local search, which NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS starts at the points it samples in the OPTIM box, and NLOPT:GN_CRS2_LM is a global optimiser. Use a local NLopt algorithm in the INNER block, which starts at the point it is given (NLOPT:LD_AUGLAG, ..., NLOPT:LN_SBPLX), or run NLOPT:GN_CRS2_LM as the exploration (EXPLORE block) or once, as STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN.` (for StoGO, DIRECT and AGS it says where they start instead). `G_MLSL` and `G_MLSL_LDS`, which need an `INNER` block of their own, keep the refusal "INNER blocks do not nest", and an `INNER` block of another `LIB` its own. `tadah explain` of an MLSL or AUGLAG form says what its `INNER` block takes (`INNER ... ENDINNER: the local search it runs, from ...: LIB NLOPT and a local ALGO; a global one ... is refused, ...`). 1.5.0 accepted each of these as the `INNER` search and ran it (it accepted `G_MLSL`, `G_MLSL_LDS`, `AUGLAG` and `AUGLAG_EQ` there too, which NLopt failed with `nlopt invalid argument`). With `PC_LAMMPS`, the refusal of a gradient-based optimiser in an `INNER` block names what an `INNER` block takes there (`--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS but INNER's optimiser NLOPT:LD_MMA is gradient-based. PC_LAMMPS requires a gradient-free optimiser, and an INNER search is a local NLopt algorithm: name a derivative-free one in the INNER block (NLOPT:LN_AUGLAG, NLOPT:LN_AUGLAG_EQ, NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA, NLOPT:LN_COBYLA, NLOPT:LN_NELDERMEAD, NLOPT:LN_PRAXIS, NLOPT:LN_SBPLX).`); it gave the other blocks' advice (`... (NLOPT GN_*/LN_*, TADAH RANDOM/GRID/ANNEAL). The only gradient-based exception is DLIB:BFGS ...`), of which an `INNER` block takes only the `LN_*` algorithms. `tadah refit`'s EXPLORE `INNER` block takes local searches only, as before. See *Configuration File* (*INNER block*).
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — the blocks of the target file must nest, and a line that opens or closes a block holds its key alone.** `tadah hpo` refuses every other form at the line concerned. A second `OPTIMIZER` block: `Error on line 7: OPTIMIZER block given more than once (the first opens on line 3); write every optimiser setting in one block`. An `OPTIMIZER` line inside a block: `OPTIMIZER block must appear at the top level of the file (it is inside the STRATEGY block opened on line 4)`. An `END` line that does not close the innermost open block: `ENDOPTIMIZER does not close the innermost open block, the STRATEGY block opened on line 4 (ENDSTRATEGY closes it)`. A block the file leaves open: `OPTIMIZER block not closed with ENDOPTIMIZER`, at the line that opens the innermost open block. A word after the key of a line that opens or closes a block: `OPTIMIZER opens a block and takes no value (unexpected 'LIB'); write each key on its own line inside the block`. In 1.5.0 a second `OPTIMIZER` block reopened the first and replaced the settings both gave. An `OPTIMIZER` line inside a block went back to the `OPTIMIZER` level. `ENDOPTIMIZER` closed the `OPTIMIZER` block from inside any block in it, while the other `END` lines were refused there. A block left open at the end of the file was read as closed, and the words after a block's key were dropped. A repeated `INIT`, `NOISE`, `EXPLORE`, `REFINE` or `RESTART` block continues the first, as in 1.5.0. See *Configuration syntax and block layout*.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `tadah hpo` evaluates no point outside the `OPTIM` box, and none can be the best**. Only `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` proposes such points (Ceres' line-search solver has no bounds); every other optimiser and every finite difference keeps to the box, and a coordinate within rounding outside it is evaluated on the bound. A point further out is not trained or validated, and gets no `DUMP` archive: it is scored as a failure graded by its distance to the box, in [0.99975, 1) × `FAILSCORE` (band D's range, whatever `FAILSCORE_GRADING` says), so it is never the best, a `WARM` seed or a `PRESTAGE` candidate; its gradient is that score's slope, which points away from the box, so a descent step leads back into it, with no evaluation; it has its `loss.tadah` and `params.tadah` rows and counts against `MAXEVAL`; the first is a WARNING (`eval 4: CGRID2B[2] = 0.9 lies outside its OPTIM box [1, 2]: it is not evaluated, and is scored 1.797284568240756e+308, a failure graded by its distance to the box, ...`), and DIAGNOSTICS count them (`Out-of-box points`). 1.5.0 evaluated such a point where it was proposed, with neighbour lists short of a cutoff beyond the box, and it could become the best. A CERES run takes another path: on the one-dimensional straddle problem of HPO's tests (`CGRID2B[2]` in [1, 2], `FAILSCORE 1e9`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`) from 1.4, `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` proposes 3 points outside the box, evaluates none of them and ends `FTOL_REL reached` after 30 evaluations, its best at 1.9854. Docs: *Configuration File* (*Points outside the OPTIM box*, the CERES rows, *Failure handling — graded FAILSCORE*, *Positive density forms*). Test: `tests/cli_failed_evaluations` (`ceres_box`).
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `CERES:LBFGS`, `CERES:WOLFE` and `CERES:NLCG` are refused**, at the line that closes their block: their Wolfe line search interpolates the score of a point outside the box and shrinks its step to almost nothing (a 2-D run stopped at its start after 16 evaluations; a 1-D one moved by 2.5e-7 per iteration until `MAXEVAL`). The refusal names `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS` (L-BFGS within the box), `DLIB:BFGS` and `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`, whose Armijo line search steps back from such a point and which stays; `PARAM max_lbfgs_rank` and `nlcg_type`, which only the refused three read, are refused on it. `tadah explain CERES:LBFGS` says why. Docs: *Configuration File* (the CERES rows, the refusal table), *Optimizer*. In an `INNER` block, whose search is a local NLopt algorithm, one of them (any optimiser of another `LIB`) is refused by that rule instead, at startup: `... names CERES:LBFGS, but NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS runs its INNER search with NLopt: set LIB NLOPT and an NLopt ALGO (such as LN_BOBYQA) inside the INNER block`, advice an `INNER` block can follow (tests: `startup validation: an INNER block needs an optimiser that runs one and an NLopt algorithm`, and with `PC_LAMMPS` in `test_pc_observable_parser.cpp`).
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING for gradient searches next to failures — a finite difference never mixes a usable loss with a failure**: each component is differenced with points of the kind of the loss at the point (usable, a graded failure, a failure). A central stencil point of another kind gives way to the one-sided difference on the other side (one evaluation more), or the 2-point one when the far point lies outside the box or is of another kind too; a forward step of another kind steps backward; with no such side the point has no usable gradient, as before. The first such difference of each dimension is journalled and DIAGNOSTICS count them (`Mixed-kind stencils`). A search next to the failure region goes on from a point whose stencil reaches into it: from 1.6 on the one-dimensional problem of `tests/cli_failed_evaluations`, whose stencil point 1.4 fails, `DLIB:BFGS` differences 1.8 and 2.0 and reaches the bound 2, its best, `FTOL_ABS reached` after 39 evaluations (1.5.0 took the central difference with the failed 1.4, which overflows under the default `FAILSCORE`, and ended the search there as if `MAXEVAL` were reached). Docs: *Configuration File* (*Points without a usable gradient*, *Finite-difference gradients and the OPTIM box*). Test: `tests/cli_failed_evaluations` (`fd_straddle`).
- `[models][umbrella]` **BREAKING — the setfl reader refuses a grid whose r tables do not reach the cutoff, or whose spline index at the cutoff is no int** (Tadah.MODELS' `EAMSetflIO::read_setfl_text`, at the grid line, for D2_EAM, DM_EAM, `pair_style tadah` and `tadah refit`): a table holds r = 0, dr, ..., (nr - 1) dr, and LAMMPS (`pair_style eam/alloy`) and tadah take its last value at every r past its last point, so the cutoff may lie at most one step past it (nr x dr >= rcut, to 1e-4 of rcut): `EAMSetflIO: setfl file 'Cu.eam.alloy', line 5: the r tables do not reach the cutoff: nr = 1000 points dr = 0.001 apart end at r = (nr - 1) x dr = 0.999, and rcut = 5 lies more than one step past their last point (nr x dr = 1); LAMMPS (pair_style eam/alloy) and tadah would take each table's last value for every r from 0.999 to 5. Correct nr, dr or rcut on the grid line ('nrho drho nr dr rcut').`; an `rcut / dr` above 2147483647 is refused likewise. Such a file was read: with `dr` = 1e-300 every distance was evaluated at an index past int, so `tadah train` of a DM_EAM model crashed (a segmentation fault) and `tadah refit` fitted garbage. The setfl files of the LAMMPS distribution (27 checked; Zhou's and Mendelev's put `rcut` one step past the last point) are read as before. `refit.eam` with `OUTPUT_GRID_SCALE` keeps the input's span unless that would leave the cutoff more than one of its steps past its last point; then it ends one step before `rcut` (a one-step input scaled up kept its last point one input step, several of the new steps, before `rcut`). A scale other than 1 whose point counts round to the input's writes the input's grid itself, as 1 does (its step was recomputed from the span, which can differ from the input's in its last digit). See *Refitting* (*Reading the result*) and `tadah explain SETFL`.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **BREAKING (C++ API) — `RefitEngine::compact_fit` returns nothing, and `tadah refit` has no dense fallback in compact mode**: the compact fitter fits the table or throws; an engine whose hook returns without a fit is an internal error (`std::logic_error`). In 1.5.0 the hook returned `bool`, and a `false` return (HPO's engine, for want of a reachable window) fitted the table in dense mode, a defaulted basis with `tadah refit: defaulted basis falls back Knot5 -> Blip for the dense path (set REFIT_BASIS explicitly to control this).`; neither the fallback nor its WARNING is left.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — a `PRESTAGE` refinement on a search that does not start at the point it is given is refused at startup**. NLopt's StoGO (`GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`), DIRECT (`GN_DIRECT`, `GN_DIRECT_L`, `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND`, their `_NOSCAL` forms, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L`) and AGS (`GN_AGS`) never read that point: they evaluate the centre of the box first (AGS a point of its curve near it) and then search the whole box, so as the refinement they never used the exploration's candidates (in a 2-D run none of their six refinement evaluations was at the candidate). Such a refinement, at the `STRATEGY` level or in a `REFINE` block, is refused for any `TOPK`, before the `TOPK` rule, at the `ENDOPTIMIZER` line, naming where it starts and every local optimiser that starts at its point: `Error on line 14: STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE cannot refine with NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L: it does not start at the point it is given (DIRECT evaluates the centre of the OPTIM box first and then divides the whole box; NLopt never reads that point), so the refinement would never use the exploration's candidates, for any TOPK. Use a local optimiser, which starts at each candidate (CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT, DLIB:BFGS, ..., NLOPT:LN_SBPLX), or run NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L as the exploration (EXPLORE block) or once, as STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN.` 1.5.0 ran such a refinement with `TOPK 1`; with a larger `TOPK` it ran the exploration and then stopped, once that gave two or more candidates, with `MULTISTART > 1 is incompatible with NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L (single-seed global). ...`. As an exploration and in a `PLAIN` run they are accepted as before; as an `INNER` search, a global one, they are refused (the entry above). See *Configuration File* (*TYPE PRESTAGE*).
- `[hpo]` **`tadah refit` compact mode: a reachability analysis whose `r_reach` is below the table's `dr` is an internal error**: `std::logic_error` (`tadah refit[compact]: internal error: the reachability analysis gave r_reach = ... A (REFIT_REACH_SMIN ... x the nearest-neighbour distance of the <lattice> lattice of a0 = ... A), less than the step of the table's radial grid, dr = ... A, although the crystal gate of tadah refit (eam_lattice::require_crystal_on_grid) refuses every such table; this is a bug, please report it`), where an `r_reach` <= 0 logged a WARNING and fitted the table in dense mode. No configuration reaches it: the crystal gate refuses such a table (the next entry). In 1.5.0, which read `REFIT_REACH_SMIN` without a range, `REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0`, or a subnormal a0 with `REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0.1`, fell back to the dense fit there.
- `[models][mlip][hpo]` **`tadah refit` refuses a lattice constant whose crystal the table cannot describe**: before any fitting, in both modes, a table whose crystal has its nearest neighbours at or beyond the table's cutoff at a0 is refused (LAMMPS counts a pair only below the cutoff, so there the crystal has no interaction: a lattice constant written in picometres, `tadah refit: the lattice constant of the EAM header, a0 = 361 A, puts the nearest neighbours of the fcc crystal 255.26554800834364 A apart, at or beyond the table's cutoff, rcut = 5 A, and LAMMPS (pair_style eam/alloy) counts a pair only below rcut: the crystal has no interaction in LAMMPS. Edit the element line of the setfl file (...): set the equilibrium lattice constant, in Angstrom (a value in picometres is 100 times the value in Angstrom).`), and so is one whose crystal has its nearest neighbours less than the table's step `dr` apart at the strongest compression the refit tests (a/a0 = `REFIT_REACH_SMIN`, 0.75 when unset), naming the element line to edit (`tadah refit: the lattice constant of the EAM header, a0 = 1e-323 A, puts the nearest neighbours of the fcc crystal 0 A apart at the strongest compression the refit tests (a/a0 = REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0.1), less than the step of the table's radial grid, dr = 0.0125313 A: the table describes no pair distance of that crystal. Edit the element line of the setfl file (...): set the equilibrium lattice constant, in Angstrom.`): a lattice constant written in metres (`3.615e-10`) rather than Angstrom, or a subnormal one such as `1e-323`, which the setfl reader reads. 1.5.0's gate asked for a known lattice and a positive a0 only, and fitted such a table: for a0 = `1e-323` or `361` the dense fit wrote `pot.tadah` and `refit.eam`, its self-test comparing crystals without a neighbour within the cutoff (0.000 meV/atom), and with a0 = `361` the compact fit's windows began above the cutoff. No real potential is refused: its nearest neighbours lie well inside its cutoff, and at `REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0.1` their distance is about 0.2 A or more, its `dr` about 0.001 to 0.01 A. See *Refitting* (*Reading the result*).
- `[umbrella]` **The documentation build needs Python 3.11 or later**: `docs/source/config/read_anneal_keys.py`, which `conf.py` runs, reads HPO's `blueprint/anneal_keys.toml` with `tomllib` only, and on an older Python stops the build with `read_anneal_keys.py needs Python 3.11 or later: it reads HPO's blueprint/anneal_keys.toml with tomllib, which Python 3.10 does not have (<interpreter>); build the documentation with Python 3.11 or later`; it fell back to the third-party `toml` package (still in `docs/requirements.txt`, for the CLI page). `CONTRIBUTING.md` (*Building the documentation*) says so. Test: `docs/tools/tests/test_read_anneal_keys.py` (`tadah_docs_tools`).
- `[mlip][hpo][core]` **BREAKING — the keys that prepared the training data are in a potential's training record, not among its key lines, and neither `tadah predict` nor a retrain from a potential applies a potential's** (a potential's key lines are what is needed to use the model, its keys and fit, its resolved `ESHIFT` and its `LSCALE`). `EFILTER`, `FFILTER`, `ESHIFT_ATOM`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, `ZERO_COM_FORCE` and `WDBFILE` (and the retired `EWEIGHT_TEMP` and `WDBFILE_AUTO`, the error-scales entry): `tadah train`, `tadah refit` (`--edit` too) and `tadah hpo` (`best_pot.tadah`, its rank-local copies, the `DUMP` and `BDUMP` archives) write no key line of them, and the training record lists those the run was given, after `DBFILE` (the defaults no one set, `ESHIFT_ATOM false` and the like, which every potential of `tadah train` had as key lines, are left out). `tadah predict` removes them from the potential, a potential of an earlier version included, and says so in one `INFO` line at `-v 2` naming those not at their default; it predicts every structure it is given and filters only with `--efilter`, `--ffilter` and `--zero-com-force` (it takes no other key: the entry on the keys that are not its options, under *Changed*), and derives no shift from the data it predicts. It applied a potential's `EFILTER` and `FFILTER` (a structure outside the training window was left out of `energy.pred` and the `-A` metrics, said only at `-v 2`), printed `WARNING: Multiple ESHIFT sources set; precedence: explicit > ATOM > DBATOM.` for a potential trained with `ESHIFT_ATOM` or `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, and with `--no-eshift` such a toggle fitted a shift to the data predicted (`--no-eshift` now compares the model with the references as they are); a potential's `ZERO_COM_FORCE true` was never applied (the default of `tadah predict`, `false`, replaced it). `tadah train` and `tadah hpo` do not read a potential's lines of them: a file that has `WEIGHTS` (`tadah train -c pot.tadah` plus a `DBFILE` line, a `-c` seed, a potential that a configuration file includes with `CONFIG`) has them skipped as its lines are parsed, with a `WARNING` naming those not at their default that the run does not give itself (a toggle neither when the run sets the other toggle true: the two choose the source of the shift and exclude each other, so `--eshift-dbatom` beside a copy's `ESHIFT_ATOM true` is not warned about), the potential file and where the run's own go (`tadah train: the configuration file old.train is a potential (it has WEIGHTS): its EFILTER 5 6, ESHIFT_DBATOM true and WDBFILE 1 0.5 prepared the training data of the fit that wrote it, not of this run, and are ignored ...`; `old.tadah, which the configuration file outer.train includes with CONFIG, is a potential ... or an EFILTER line in outer.train, to apply it to this run`); the run's own -- a command-line `--wdbfile` and the like, a line of the file that includes the potential, before or after its `CONFIG` line -- apply and are recorded; the toggles are skipped with or without the potential's `ESHIFT` line, so to derive the shift from the retrain's data delete that line and give the run `--eshift-atom`/`--eshift-dbatom` or a toggle line of the file that includes the potential (the `WARNING` then names the toggles among the keys the run can give, and beside an `ESHIFT` line ends `The ESHIFT of the configuration, the energy reference of the model, overrules ESHIFT_ATOM and ESHIFT_DBATOM: ...`); in 1.5.0 a potential's toggle beside its `ESHIFT` was overruled with `Multiple ESHIFT sources set`, and without it derived the shift from the retrain's data. A retrain from a potential trained with `WDBFILE` works with any number of datasets: it stopped (`WDBFILE size (2) does not match number of datasets (3).`), and a potential's filters and weights applied to the retrain's data; a seed of `tadah hpo -c` with an older `WDBFILE` was refused the same way. A line of the run that included such a potential with `CONFIG` was mixed with the potential's line of the key: one after the `CONFIG` line was appended to its values (`WDBFILE`) or refused (`Exceeded capacity for key: EFILTER`), one before it was replaced by the potential's. `pair_style tadah` reads none of them, as before. See *Training* (*Load-time data transforms*, *The transforms at predict time*, *Training record*, *Retraining from a potential*) and *Nested Fitting* (*Load-time data transforms*, reproducing a candidate). Tests: `[dataset-keys]` cases of `test_tadah_cli_exit_status.cpp` (`Subcommand: a retrain that includes a potential with CONFIG applies the keys of its own lines that prepare the training data`, `Subcommand: hpo seeded from a potential with CONFIG applies the configuration file's keys that prepare the training data` among them), `pair_style tadah: the dataset keys of an older potential change no energy`.
- `[mlip][hpo][lammps]` **The training record's opening line is `# --- training record (no key is read from it) ---`**: it said `(not read by tadah)`, and `tadah refit --edit` reads the block, to copy it. No released version wrote the old line; `refit --edit` refuses a block it opens at its end line. The *Quick Start* `pot.tadah` has the new line.
- `[mlip][hpo][lammps]` **A potential keeps the keys it does not use in its training record**, a block of comment lines right above its key lines, for the record only:

  ```
  # --- training record (no key is read from it) ---
  # DBFILE db.train
  # VALIDATION config.val
  # --- end of training record ---
  ```

  one line for each of `DBFILE`, the keys that prepared the training data (the BREAKING dataset-keys entry above), `VALIDATION`, `HPOTARGET`, `CHECK` (the setting of `tadah hpo --check`, which `tadah train` and `tadah refit` do not read; a `tadah hpo` potential records none), `VERBOSE`, `NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE` and `HEALTH_LOG` the run was given, with the values as the configuration file or the command line wrote them (a default no one set is not listed: `tadah train -c config.train` records the `DBFILE` of `config.train` alone). `tadah train`, `tadah refit` and `tadah hpo` (`best_pot.tadah`, its rank-local copies, the `DUMP` and `BDUMP` archives) write it; a retrain from a potential records its own run (the potential's record is a comment of its configuration, which the retrain does not read); `tadah refit --edit` copies its input's record, hand edits included, after `# edited by tadah refit --edit from <input>`, as an edit fits nothing, and refuses an input whose record is malformed, naming the file and the lines: an opening line without its end line (with the end line deleted, the refusal names the opening line and the potential's first key line, before which to restore it), an end line without its opening line, or a line inside the block that does not start with `#`. No key is read from the block: loading, `tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah`, a retrain and `tadah hpo -c` behave as without it, and a potential without a record (an earlier version's) loads as before. The DATA section of `best_pot.tadah`'s header gives each training dataset's statistics and its entry of the record's `DBFILE` line instead of its path. In 1.5.0 a potential had its configuration's `DBFILE` (`M_BLR`), `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` and `AUDIT_SAMPLE` as key lines (and a `best_pot.tadah` its run's `VERBOSE`), and the others not at all. The *Quick Start* `pot.tadah` has the record `tadah train -c config.train` writes (`# DBFILE db.train`); its key lines lose `ESHIFT_ATOM`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, `WDBFILE_AUTO` and `ZERO_COM_FORCE` (the dataset-keys entry above). See *Training*, *Training record*, and *Refitting tabulated EAM potentials*, *Editing a refitted potential*.
- `[hpo]` **A point the run has evaluated is answered from its evaluation when a search asks for it again, if every evaluation of the run is a function of its point** (NLopt and `DLIB:BFGS`): the run records each evaluation with a usable loss by the point evaluated, whichever search or backend asked for it, and a call at such a point, the same doubles bit for bit, that asks for nothing the record lacks (a gradient only when the same search formed one there) gets its loss without an evaluation -- a point the search asked for before, `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` landing on the same clamped corner again, `LN_SBPLX` and `LN_NELDERMEAD` reflecting onto a bound point, `LD_SLSQP` and `LN_COBYLA` stepping onto a bound point they had, `AUGLAG` evaluating the point its local search returned once more, `DLIB:BFGS`'s line-search trials clamped onto one point -- or one an earlier search of the run evaluated: a `PRESTAGE` refinement's start (the exploration's best), a point an earlier `MULTISTART` start reached, a `RESTART` round from `METHOD CONFIG` retracing round 0. No row, and not counted in HPO's count of evaluations, which counts each search's own and is what `DLIB:BFGS`'s `MAXEVAL` (and so a `BUDGET_PER_START`, a `PRESTAGE` phase's `MAXEVAL`) bounds; NLopt counts every call against its own `MAXEVAL`, one answered from an earlier search's evaluation too, so an NLopt phase or start is charged for it (an `LN_SBPLX` refinement of `MAXEVAL 4` whose first call is the exploration's best makes 3 evaluations); the journal names an earlier search's evaluation as such. Each search evaluates its own gradients' stencils. A `PRESTAGE` exploration's call answered from the record is a candidate for the refinement, as its evaluation would be, so an exploration that retraces an earlier round still hands its candidates on. Under `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` a call that asks for a gradient the record lacks takes the recorded loss as the loss at the point of its finite differences, whose stencil alone is evaluated (MLSL's local search asks for the gradient at a sample it evaluated without one, and each local start was evaluated again); under `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` the point is evaluated, and when `DLIB:BFGS` asked for its loss just before (at its start, or where a line search ends) that evaluation answers the loss call too, one evaluation and no reuse (a `PRESTAGE` refinement's start is evaluated once and counted once in the `RESTART` window). The `RESTART` window counts each such call as one evaluation without a new best: a search that only asks for points the run has makes no evaluation, and the window still ends or relaunches it (`LD_MMA` held at a bound with `RESTART` as its only stop), and its `StagnationStop` line counts such calls. A run whose loss has a `PC_LAMMPS` line (LAMMPS instances kept from one evaluation to the next, the `--hotvar` chain), a `PC_OBSERVABLE` that carries state (`cohesive_energy`'s lattice constant) or a `--skip-rel` filter evaluates every repeat again, as every run did, and counts as repeats evaluated again the points an earlier search of the run evaluated (a `PRESTAGE` refinement's start), naming them as an earlier search's. A repeat of a failed evaluation is evaluated again too, and so is one that asks under `ANALYTICAL` or `HYBRID` for a gradient its evaluation did not form. The journal names the first reuse of each search and, once, why a repeat is evaluated again; DIAGNOSTICS has `Repeated points reused` and `Repeats evaluated again`. NLopt counts a reused call among its own calls (its `MAXEVAL`): a derivative-free search still ends after `MAXEVAL` calls, with fewer evaluations, and its `MAXEVAL_REACHED` line says how many calls were answered, as the journal's first-reuse line and DIAGNOSTICS say; a gradient search can get further within its `MAXEVAL`, unless it mostly asks again (`LD_MMA` held at a bound: 18 evaluations of `MAXEVAL 60`, NLopt's count ending it). On a 2-D search on the Ta test data of the HPO tests (`PC_ERMSE` and `PC_FRMSE`, `OPTIM RCUT2B (1) 4 6` and `CGRID2B (2) 0.1 2.0`, the optimum on a corner) `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` at `MAXEVAL 40` make 10 evaluations where they made 20, `LN_SBPLX` 45 of its 60 calls; `LD_SLSQP`'s steps onto that corner are distinct points and are evaluated. See *Nested Fitting Configuration*, *Points a search asks for again*.
- `[hpo]` **A `RESTART` round's `RANDOM` and `LHS` points come from a stream of their own, and a `RESTART` block without `SEED` names the seed its points are drawn with.** Round r draws a stream of its own for `SEED + r`, not the generator an `INIT` block's `RANDOM`/`LHS` draws for that seed, so a round whose `RESTART SEED + r` is an `INIT` block's `SEED` does not start where round 0 did (`SOBOL` draws block `SEED + r`). Without a `SEED` each round drew `std::random_device() + r`, unnamed; a run now draws one seed *S* at its first restart, round r draws with *S + r*, and the journal says so (`RESTART: the RESTART block has no SEED, so its restart points are drawn from std::random_device: restart r draws with S + r (SEED S in the RESTART block draws the same points again)`; the round line `RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED <S + 1>, SEED 6`). See *Configuration File* (*RESTART decorator*).
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA` is refused** in every phase, at the line that closes its block (`ENDOPTIMIZER`, `ENDEXPLORE`, `ENDINNER`), naming `NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA`, as `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` is (the `OPTIM` box is the configuration's contract): `Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA is refused: NLopt 2.10 runs LN_NEWUOA without the OPTIM box: it passes NEWUOA no bounds, so its evaluations leave the box and the best potential can lie outside the bounds the configuration sets (...). It also needs two OPTIM dimensions or more. The recommended replacement is NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA, ...`. It was accepted with a startup WARNING: a 2-D run of `MAXEVAL 25` made 20 evaluations outside the box and wrote a `best_pot.tadah` at `RCUT2B` 8.11 for an `OPTIM` box of [4, 6], and a 1-D run passed startup and ended `FAILED -- dimension 1 must be >= 2` after no evaluation. A study moved to `LN_BOBYQA` takes another trajectory (its `STEP` rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius). `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` is now the only backend that ignores the bounds; the WARNING at the first point outside the box names `CERES:*` alone, and `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`'s refusal names `LN_BOBYQA` alone. See *Configuration File* (the `NLOPT` row of *Algorithms by library*) and *Optimizer*.
- `[hpo]` **A gradient search whose budget cannot pay for one step is warned about at startup.** A gradient search evaluates the loss at its starting point and the gradient there before any point it steps to: with `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` 1 + 2n evaluations in n dimensions (1 + n with `PARAM diff_method 1`), with `ANALYTICAL` 1 + 1, with `HYBRID` at least 1 + 1; a `MAXEVAL` of that or less (1 + 2n or less, 2 or less under `ANALYTICAL`) ended the search at its start (`MAXEVAL_REACHED`, `DLIB:BFGS: MAXEVAL reached`) with no word. An MLSL whose local search takes gradients first evaluates its starting point and its first N samples without a gradient (N its `POPULATION`, NLopt's default 4), and its first local search starts at one of these points and takes the gradient there. Under `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` (central differences; n in place of 2n with `PARAM diff_method 1`) a `MAXEVAL` of 2n + N + 1 or less lets no local search step where the run takes the loss there from the run's record (every evaluation of the run a function of its point; the stencil alone is evaluated: *Points a search asks for again*; `GD_MLSL` with `INNER LD_LBFGS` in 2-D: 9 or less), and 2n + N + 2 or less where a `--skip-rel` filter makes it evaluate that loss again (10 or less); a run with `PC_LAMMPS` refuses such an MLSL, and one with a `PC_OBSERVABLE` refuses `NUMERICAL`. Under `ANALYTICAL`, whose gradient follows an evaluation of its point, the run evaluates that loss again and then one analytical gradient: N + 3 or less lets no local search step (7 or less in the example); under `HYBRID` that, and more for each parameter it differences. A run with a `PC_OBSERVABLE` is `ANALYTICAL` or `HYBRID`. Startup warned of none of these MLSL budgets either. Now: `WARNING the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: MAXEVAL 4 takes no step: its gradient search evaluates the loss at a starting point (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (4 evaluations: central differences in 2 dimensions, GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL) before it evaluates a point it steps to, which needs MAXEVAL 6 or more`, and for an MLSL `... NLOPT:GD_MLSL: MAXEVAL 9 takes no step of a local search: MLSL evaluates its starting point and its first 4 samples without a gradient (5 evaluations; POPULATION 4, NLopt's default), then its first local search, from one of them, takes the loss there from the run's record (no evaluation) and evaluates the gradient there (4 evaluations: ...) before it evaluates a point it steps to, which needs MAXEVAL 10 or more` (with a `--skip-rel` filter: `... evaluates the loss there again (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (...) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 11 or more`; under `ANALYTICAL`: `... evaluates the loss there again (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (1 evaluation: one analytical gradient, GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 8 or more`), for every phase with a budget of its own (each `MULTISTART` start with its `BUDGET_PER_START` or share of `MAXEVAL`, the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, an `INNER` block whose `MAXEVAL`, in NLopt calls, is 1). A search on NLopt or `DLIB:BFGS` that starts at a point the run has evaluated, and asks for that point first, takes the loss there from the run's record the same way: a `PRESTAGE` refinement, which starts at an exploration candidate (`the PRESTAGE refinement NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: MAXEVAL 4 takes no step: its gradient search takes the loss at its starting point from the run's record (no evaluation) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 5 or more`; it steps at `MAXEVAL 5`), and the run's first search under `NOISE CALIBRATION` (`PLAIN`, the first `MULTISTART` start, a `PRESTAGE` exploration), whose seed point the calibration evaluated; under `ANALYTICAL`, `HYBRID` or a reuse blocker `... evaluates the loss at its starting point again ...`. Under `NOISE CALIBRATION` the later `MULTISTART` starts begin at `INIT` points the run has not evaluated, so where the budgets differ the first start is warned apart (`the first MULTISTART start NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: BUDGET_PER_START 4 ... takes the loss at its starting point from the run's record ..., which needs BUDGET_PER_START 5 or more`, `each MULTISTART start after the first NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: BUDGET_PER_START 4 ..., which needs BUDGET_PER_START 6 or more`; the first start steps at `BUDGET_PER_START 5`). NLopt's StoGO (`GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`) does not start at the point it is given -- its first point is the centre of its box --, so it spends the loss there, the gradient and one step wherever its phase starts (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GD_STOGO: MAXEVAL 5 takes no step: its gradient search, which does not start at the point it is given, evaluates the loss at a point of its own (1 evaluation) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 6 or more`, under `NOISE CALIBRATION` too; as the `PRESTAGE` refinement it is refused, and so is a StoGO `INNER` search, a global one: see the entries at the top of *Changed*). A warning, not an error: such a probe of the start can be deliberate. See *Configuration File* (the `MAXEVAL` row).
- `[core][hpo]` **A second Ctrl-C (SIGINT), or a second SIGTERM, ends `tadah hpo` at once**, killed by the signal (a shell reports 130, 143; `tadah: second SIGINT: ending at once`), as `kill -9` would: `best_pot.tadah` keeps the last best it saved, and the history files can lack the rows still queued. The first signal still stops the run once the evaluations in progress finish, and is now announced at once on standard error (`tadah: SIGINT received: stopping once the work in progress can stop and save its results; a second SIGINT (Ctrl-C) ends tadah at once, and what is not yet written is lost`). Signals of different kinds (SLURM's `SIGTERM` after its `SIGUSR2` warning) and a second `SIGUSR2` do not escalate. `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` workers die with `tadah`. A second signal did nothing, so a run inside a long evaluation (a LAMMPS run, a slow fit) could be ended only with `kill -9`. Tests: CORE `test_signal_handler.cpp`, `tadah_hpo_second_signal_*` (`cli_signal/check_hpo_second_signal.py`). See *Configuration File* (*Signals*).
- `[cmake-tools][umbrella]` **The tests need CMake 3.16** (`BUILD_TESTING` ON; the build without tests still accepts 3.15, and one with `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP` needs 3.24, Tadah.HPO's minimum). CTest reports a skipped test as Skipped through the test property `SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`, which it has from 3.16 and ignores before: on CMake 3.15 every native skip -- a `TADAH_SKIP` case, `tadah_docs_tools` without its tools, the header-check entries below 3.19 -- was reported Passed. cmake-tools' `testing/TadahTestSkip.cmake`, which `include(Catch)` loads in every module's tests, stops such a configure step: `Tadah's tests need CMake 3.16 or later: CTest reports a test case that calls TADAH_SKIP as Skipped through the test property SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION, which an older CTest ignores, reporting the test Passed. This is CMake 3.15.7; use CMake 3.16 or later, or configure with -DBUILD_TESTING=OFF`. Tadah.MLIP's `tests/CMakeLists.txt` declares 3.16.
- `[models][umbrella]` **`RHOFORM SIGNED` is bit for bit the density of earlier versions in a Tadah.MODELS compiled without FMA contraction** (the presets' flags on x86-64: no `-march=native` or `-ffast-math` in MODELS' flags; LTO and a `tadah` built with `TADAH_MARCH_NATIVE` included), as *Descriptors* (`DM_REAM`) says. Measured with GCC 15.2 at the 1770 golden points: the same bits with the presets' flags, and with `-march=native` in MODELS without LTO; with `-march=native` and LTO 205 points differ from the earlier version built the same way (by up to 97 ulp). In a MODELS compiled with `-march=native` the bitwise relations its tests check, the density's own (`scan_grid`, `pair_density`) and its agreement with the refit and `potedit`, do not all hold whatever `ream_density.cpp`'s flags (12 cases fail; with that file at `-ffp-contract=off`, 11, the refit and `potedit` ones among them), and they all hold with `-ffp-contract=off` for every unit. Pinning `ream_density.cpp` was not done: it moves which relations fail; a library-wide `-ffp-contract=off` for MODELS, which would also cover AArch64 (whose compilers fuse at default flags), is not applied.
- `[umbrella]` **The GitLab `docs_sphinx` job installs `docs/requirements.txt` every time**, into a virtual environment of its own (`python3 -m venv`; the job fails, naming `python3-venv`, when the runner image cannot make one), and builds with that environment's `sphinx-build`. It installed them only on a runner without `sphinx-build`, so a runner with any Sphinx built without the extensions they list. Test: `test_ci_docs_job.py` (`tadah_docs_tools`). HPO's build jobs stay `when: manual`.
- `[models]` The warnings descriptors give about their configuration (a knot past the cutoff, a bump past a `Cut_Dummy` cutoff, a non-compact basis with `Cut_Dummy`, a `DM_mEAD` or OrbitalWise `DM_Poly` channel function that is not rotationally invariant for L > 0: `DM_mRLR`, `DM_mSQRT`, `DM_mBlip`, `DM_mBlipEnv`, `DM_OW_*`/`DM_GOW_*` other than `_SQ`) are logged once per process, not once per descriptor object (tadah hpo builds the descriptors of every evaluation).
- `[models][hpo]` **With `VERBOSE` > 0 a `tadah hpo` run prints a kernel's settings line once** (`Kern_RBF | sigma: 1 | gamma: 1`; `Kern_Polynomial`, `Kern_Sigmoid`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ`), the line of its `-c` model, at startup; a second run in the process, such as a program's second `hpo_run()`, prints its own. Every model the run built printed it: the startup builds, the model of every evaluation and the potential each `PC_LAMMPS` script runs (a `Kern_RBF` run of 4 evaluations with one script printed it 9 times; 1.5.0 built the trainer once per worker thread and the `PC_LAMMPS` potential once per evaluation). The `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` workers print none. Outside `tadah hpo` every kernel built prints its line, as before: `tadah train`, `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah`. At `VERBOSE 0` nothing is printed, as before. *Training* (*pot.tadah format*) and *Nested Fitting Configuration* say so. Tests: MODELS `test_kernel_settings_once.cpp`, HPO `hpo_tadah_cli_kernel_line_once` and `hpo_kernel_line_e2e`, and `Subcommand: each run prints the settings line of the kernel it builds`.
- `[mlip]` **Removed: `Trainer::reinit()` and `DesignMatrix::refresh_context_scalars()`** (C++ API). Retrain a model in place with `model->reset_trained()`.
- `[hpo]` **A build with `BUILD_TESTING` and `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP` needs Python 3.11 or later**, which HPO's tests now declare: their registry drift gate `hpo_anneal_registry_sync` reads TOML with `tomllib`. An older interpreter is refused at the configure step; before, it was accepted and that test failed (`No module named 'tomllib'`). Without NFP the requirement stays Python 3. *Installation* (`BUILD_TESTING`) and `CONTRIBUTING.md` say so.
- `[hpo]` **The journal names the pid of a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker that died**: `pc-worker 2 (pid 41237) killed/died: ...` (it was `pc-worker 2 killed/died: ...`), the pid the kernel's out-of-memory line names; *Nested Fitting Configuration* (`PC_ISOLATION`) shows the line. A worker whose `HELLO_ACK` reports another pid than the one forked fails the handshake. `PcWorkerPool::worker_pid(slot)` gives the pid, and the pc-pool SIGKILL test uses it instead of `ps`/`awk`, without which it skipped its kill leg and passed.
- `[umbrella]` **The *Quick Start* potential and LAMMPS example** (`docs/source/quickstart`). The potential is a smooth-cutoff `D2_Blip` pair term plus a `DM_mBlipEnv` embedding of a pair density (`Cut_Cos` cutoffs, `M_KRR Kern_Linear`, `STRESS true`), retrained on the same `db.train`, fitted with the default error scales (1 meV/atom for the energy per atom, 0.5 GPa for the stress), and `in.ml` builds bcc Ta at 3.30 Å, next to the potential's own lattice constant of 3.288 Å (EAM reference 3.304 Å; C′ 57 GPa and bulk modulus 115 GPa at that lattice). The 1.5.0 example ran a `D2_LJ` potential with a hard cutoff (`Cut_Dummy`, 6.5 Å) fitted to energies only, whose own bcc lattice is near 2.9 Å, on a bcc lattice of 3.5 Å: the run started at −74 GPa, the crystal tore into dense clusters at the fixed volume and the released energy heated it from 1000 K to about 8500 K, while the hard cutoff let the total energy wander by 35 meV/atom. The example moves the total energy by up to 0.08 meV/atom, cools from 1000 K to about 500 K (equipartition), starts at about −0.5 GPa and stays bcc. A smooth-cutoff `D2_LJ` at its own lattice, the fix first proposed, was not used: no `D2_LJ` refit of these data holds bcc (C′ = (C11 − C12)/2 < 0 at its own lattice constant for each of `Cut_Dummy`, `Cut_Cos` and `Cut_PolyS` fitted to energies, energies and stresses, or energies, forces and stresses). The new potential has no `EAMFAST` fast path (it is implemented for pure two-body and `DM_REAM` models only), so `pair_style tadah` runs the generic kernel: about 1 s per 1000 steps of the 250 atoms on one core, against about 0.2 s for the old potential on its fast path. The C++ API examples train with `ESIGMA` and `SSIGMA`, and the potential of example 2 and the expected outputs of both are those of the examples' run. Docs: *Quick Start* explains the new keys and the effect of the stresses (the lattice constant is 3.381 Å fitted to energies only, 3.294 Å fitted to energies, forces and stresses), what to expect from `in.ml`, the fast path (its log line explained with the `D2_LJ` potential of 1.5.0) and what the example potential is and is not (a finite pair repulsion at short range, a spurious minimum of strongly compressed bcc near 2.25 Å, the timestep it needs above a few thousand K). The numbers the page quotes are checked: `tadah_quickstart_numbers` (those of the potential and of fits of its data, each within half a unit of its last digit) and the `test_tadah` cases `quickstart: ...` (those of the LAMMPS runs).
- `[core]` The `EAMFAST` description quotes its speed-up and log line for a `D2_LJ` potential (`RCUT2B 6.5`, `Cut_Dummy`) instead of the quickstart potential, and names `DM_REAM` as the only many-body descriptor with the EAM collapse.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **`M_KRR` potentials record `ALPHA` and `BETA`**, as `M_BLR` ones do: the evidence approximation's starting guesses of the configuration, or after a `LAMBDA -1` fit the values it found (`LAMBDA` = `ALPHA`/`BETA`), which `M_KRR` potentials did not record; a retrain from a `LAMBDA -1` potential starts the approximation from them. `best_pot.tadah` has no `DBFILE` line (its header still names the training datasets): a potential used as the `tadah hpo -c` configuration needs its `DBFILE` lines added.
- `[core]` The `DBFILE`, `ALPHA`, `BETA`, `WEIGHTS`, `MODEL` and `SBASIS` descriptions state what a potential's key lines hold (no `DBFILE` line, its training record naming the datasets of the fit: see the training-record entry under *Changed*; `ALPHA` and `BETA`, after a `LAMBDA -1` fit the values the evidence approximation found), that a `DBFILE` listing one file twice is refused, and what `M_KRR Kern_Linear` implementation 2 fits.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **BREAKING — a potential has no `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` or `VERBOSE` key line; its training record lists them.** (see the training-record entry under *Changed*) `best_pot.tadah`, the `BDUMP` and `DUMP` archives of `tadah hpo` and the potentials of `tadah train`, `tadah refit` and `refit --edit` write no key line of these settings of the run (nor of `NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE` and `HEALTH_LOG`), and list the run's values of them in their training record, a comment block above the key lines; every other key is written as before. A `best_pot.tadah` had the run's `--hpotarget`, `--validation` and `-v` values (and `NUMERIC 12`), so used as the `-c` configuration of a later run without `--validation` it validated on the file its `VALIDATION` line named, and its `VERBOSE` set the log level of whatever read it (a retrain from it, `pair_style tadah`) and the verbosity of the model built from it. Inside a `tadah hpo` run the potential the `PC_LAMMPS` scripts run is built at the run's `VERBOSE` (`-v`), as before. A potential that has the key lines (an earlier version's) still loads, and none of them is read: `[core]` no parse stores a potential's `VALIDATION`, `HPOTARGET`, `VERBOSE`, `NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE` or `HEALTH_LOG` line (`core::run_setting_keys()`), so its `VERBOSE` sets the log level of no program that loads it (`tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah`, `refit --edit`, a retrain, `tadah hpo -c`) nor its models' verbosity, and `[mlip][hpo]` a retrain and `tadah hpo -c` name those they read in the WARNING of a potential's lines not read (`its VERBOSE 2 and NUMERIC 12 were settings of the run that wrote it, not of this run, and are ignored`; `train` names no `VALIDATION`, `HPOTARGET` or `HEALTH_LOG`, `hpo` no `OUTFILE`); `tadah hpo -c` of such a potential validates on its own `--validation` file only (with a validation metric and none it is refused), `[mlip]` `tadah analysis check_bounds -c` of such a potential names its `HPOTARGET` and `VERBOSE` lines in the WARNING and, without `--hpotarget`, is refused (`tadah analysis check_bounds: no HPOTARGET: name the hpotarget file with --hpotarget, or with an HPOTARGET line in a configuration file without WEIGHTS. best_pot.tadah is a potential (it has WEIGHTS): its HPOTARGET config.hpo line was a setting of the run that wrote it and is not read.`; it took the potential's `HPOTARGET` line), and `refit --edit` of it is exact (its `NUMERIC 12` is not read and moves into the edit's record). The journal names the run's files. Tests: `Subcommand: train -c of an older potential reads none of its run settings`, `Subcommand: predict takes no verbosity from a potential's VERBOSE line`, `Subcommand: hpo -c of an older best_pot.tadah does not validate on its VALIDATION file unless --validation is given`, `Subcommand: analysis check_bounds -c of an older seed potential names its HPOTARGET line, not read, and asks for --hpotarget`. See *Training*, *Training record*, *Retraining from a potential*, and *Nested Fitting*.
- `[core]` The `VALIDATION` description says what `tadah hpo` reads, one validation configuration file whose `DBFILE` lines are the validation datasets (it said "Validation dataset file(s)."); `HPOTARGET`, `VERBOSE` and `VALIDATION` say that a potential has no line of them, its training record listing the run's, and `DBFILE` that `tadah train` refuses a configuration without one.
- `[models]` **`LAMBDA -1` (evidence approximation): λ and the weights change, most for `LAMBDA -1` potentials fitted with rows of priority 0.** It counts the observations (the rows of priority above 0 that are not zero in every column) where it counted every row of the design matrix, its ridge-augmentation rows (one per weight) and its rows of priority 0, which made β too large. What the change amounts to depends on those rows. The augmentation rows alone move λ a little (on the 9-row matrix of the test, 4 weights, from 8.13e-3 to 1.469e-2), less the more observations a fit has per weight. Rows of priority 0 can outnumber the observations many times: a `FORCE true` fit of structures stored with `fweight` 0, as the DFT readers store structures without forces, counted every force component. With 100 energies for 9 weights beside 16 200 such rows (the first 100 structures of the quickstart `db.train` with `fweight` 0), λ is 1.9e-5, where Tadah! 1.5.0, fitting the same sum (its `EWEIGHT` (1000/54)², which is `ESIGMA 1` for these 54-atom cells), counted the 16 200 rows as observations and gave a λ orders of magnitude smaller. A fit with no more observations than effective weights is refused.
- `[core][lammps]` **`pair_style tadah` writes the notices of a potential's keys that have no effect as LAMMPS warnings** on rank 0, in log.lammps and on the screen, each time `pair_coeff` loads such a potential, so the log.lammps of every LAMMPS instance has them, also a second instance of the same process (the LAMMPS Python module in a loop, `tadah hpo`'s `PC_LAMMPS` evaluations) (`WARNING: pair tadah: potential 'pot.tadah': Deprecated key SIGMA is ignored: ...`; also `MBLOCK` and the other deprecated keys, `EMBF0` and `RHOFORM` without DM_REAM); they went to standard error only, once per process on every rank, and no log.lammps had them. The other commands still write each such notice once per process to standard error.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah properties pairwise` gives the two-body and many-body blocks' energies per atom**, as the header says (they were the dimer's energy, twice the value).
- `[core][models][mlip][lammps][hpo]` **BREAKING — `BIAS true` with two or more species: one intercept column per species on every path.** Every atom's descriptor starts with N = size(`ATOM`) one-hot intercept columns in `ATOM` order, then the two-body and the many-body descriptors, in training, `tadah predict`/`hpo`/`properties`/`analysis` and `pair_style tadah` alike. The training path (MLIP `DescriptorsCalc`) gave every atom one intercept column shared by the species while `pair_style tadah`, the ridge, the normaliser and the NORM weight conversion used one per species, so from 1.3.0-beta.1, when the per-species columns came to `pair_style tadah`, every potential trained with `BIAS true` and N >= 2 species gave silently wrong LAMMPS energies and forces (a Ta-Nb `D2_LJ` potential: -622.5 eV/atom against -7.65 eV/atom from `tadah predict`; with 1.2.0 and earlier the pair style used one shared column too), `NORM true` corrupted it in tadah too, `LAMBDA > 0` left a descriptor weight unpenalised, and HPO's analytical gradient read past each atom's descriptor. With the change:
  - a potential whose `WEIGHTS`, kernel basis vectors or (a non-linear `NORM true` model) `NMEAN`/`NSTDEV` do not fit its descriptor is refused when it predicts (`tadah predict`, `pair_coeff`: one check, MODELS `require_weights_fit`), with a message naming `WEIGHTS`, `DSIZE`, `BIAS` and `ATOM` and, for a multi-species potential with one shared intercept column, saying to retrain it; a linear model's `NMEAN`/`NSTDEV` are not read, so a linear NORM potential retrained by an earlier `tadah train -c`, which appended them, still predicts;
  - a linear potential (`BF_Linear`, `Kern_Linear`) with `NORM true` and `BIAS false` is refused when it predicts: the weight conversion of earlier versions had no intercept column for the normalisation's constant. M_KRR `Kern_Linear` (until this version) and M_BLR `BF_Linear` (up to 1.2.0) took descriptor component 0 for an intercept, so the energies and forces are wrong (an M_KRR `Kern_Linear` potential of the quickstart Ta data: -0.44 against -8.04 eV/atom); M_BLR `BF_Linear` from 1.3.0-beta.1, written by `tadah hpo` and the API (`tadah train` refused the configuration), dropped the mean correction, so its energies are off by the same constant for every atom and its forces are right. Retrain it with `NORM false` or `BIAS true`; `tadah train -c pot.tadah` retrains such a potential (an `M_BLR` potential of earlier versions records its `DBFILE`: train from it as it is or replace that line; add a `DBFILE` line to an `M_KRR` one; *Retraining from a potential*): `tadah train` and `tadah hpo` build the model to be fitted without the potential's `WEIGHTS`, `BASIS`, `NMEAN`, `NSTDEV` and `SIGMA`, which the fit replaces, also for `M_KRR` (which stopped with `This object is already trained!`; see *Fixed* for a kernel potential retrained with another `SBASIS`);
  - `BIAS true` refuses (`tadah train`, `tadah hpo` at startup) training data whose energy-weighted structures cannot determine the model's per-species constants, its intercept columns (MODELS `Function_Base::intercept_columns`: a linear model's weights 1 to N, `BF_Polynomial2`'s products (k,k); a non-linear kernel's are those of the basis its fit draws, which starts with the intercept vectors, so a dot-product kernel's weights 1 to N, checked before the fit too; `Kern_RBF` has none, and M_KRR implementation 2, whose solve fits the basis vectors' targets, is not refused) (all of one composition, e.g. a fixed stoichiometry, or no energy weight), naming the rank, the intercept weights and the remedies the configuration can take: under `NORM false`, `BIAS false` with explicit `ESHIFT` values or `ESHIFT_ATOM`, other compositions, `FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT`; under `NORM true`, where a linear model needs `BIAS true` and `FIXINDEX` is refused, other compositions or `NORM false`; not `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, whose fit of the species energies is singular on such data (it sets every `ESHIFT` to 0, with a WARNING, so the constant goes into the descriptor weights; see *The bias term*);
  - `BIAS true` requires `ATOM` (also through the API; `tadah train` takes `ATOM` from the `DBFILE`, and `tadah analysis bfunc`, which plots a descriptor's radial basis, needs none), and an atom of an element `ATOM` does not list is refused;
  - `NORM true` with `M_KRR Kern_Linear` requires `BIAS true`, as with `BF_Linear` (it folded the NORM correction into a descriptor weight);
  - `FIXINDEX` indices of a linear model's descriptor weights start at N + 1 (they started at 2): `tadah train` and `tadah hpo` warn when `FIXINDEX` is used with `BIAS true` and N >= 2, naming the model's intercept weights (`BF_Polynomial2`: weights 1 and D + 1 for two species; a dot-product kernel: weights 1 to N);
  - the KRR basis seeds one intercept vector per species (`SBASIS` from N + 1, which `tadah hpo` checks at startup); `BF_Polynomial2` keeps its D (D + 1) / 2 products, N (N - 1) / 2 of which (two different intercepts) are zero;
  - the two-body force and stress rows are expanded from the first column of the two-body block, which a `D2_mJoin` reports too (Tadah.MODELS `D2_mJoin::get_fidx()`);
  - `pair_style tadah`: `compute_dimers` gives each atom of a molecule pair its own species' intercept (and the intercept loops of `compute_2b_mb_half` and `compute_2b_mb_full` stop at the local and ghost atoms, see *Fixed*).
  Single-species potentials are unchanged (the same descriptors, weights and predictions), but for the linear `NORM true` `BIAS false` potentials above, which are refused. Past `tadah hpo` results of multi-species `BIAS true` runs (PC_LAMMPS objectives, analytical gradients) from 1.3.0-beta.1 until this version are invalid. Docs: *Descriptors* (the bias term), *Training*, *Nested Fitting*, *Refit*, the `BIAS`, `DSIZE`, `NORM`, `WEIGHTS` and `FIXINDEX` keys. Tests: CORE `test_intercept_layout.cpp`, MODELS `test_weights_fit.cpp` and `test_stale_weights.cpp`, MLIP `test_descriptors_calc_bias.cpp`, Tadah.LAMMPS `test_weights_layout.cpp`, HPO `test_multispecies_bias.cpp`, and `test_tadah` `[bias]`.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo`'s journal counts an evaluation that raised an exception as one** (`Evaluations that threw : N (x%)` in DIAGNOSTICS, `threw=N` in the `SUMMARY` lines), not as a NaN/Inf clamping: a run whose every training failed read `100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss that was clamped`. The evaluation still scores `FAILSCORE`. The `LOSS FUNCTION` section names the failures that score `FAILSCORE` (it said `returned when an evaluation produces NaN/Inf`), and under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` a `Fail grading` line names those graded instead (a skip cap, a failed term of the run -- a `PC_LAMMPS` simulation, or a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term, whichever the run has -- and a gate reject). *Configuration File* (`FAILSCORE`, *Run log*).
- `[mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **One stress convention: the Cauchy stress in eV/Å³, positive under tension** (σ = (1/V)∂E/∂ε, what the model predicts; the negative of the pressure LAMMPS reports). The VASP readers negate VASP's tensor; the VASP readers store the free energy F (OUTCAR stored the energy without entropy, `vasprun.xml` energy(σ→0)); the CASTEP `.md` reader removes the kinetic stress exactly (it removed nothing). LSCALE divides the stress by s³; `get_virial_pressure()` is −tr(σ)/3. The Tadah.MLIP example and quickstart data (LAMMPS molecular dynamics with the Ta1_Ravelo_2013 EAM potential) already had this convention and are unchanged. Docs: *Dataset Format*, *Datasets converted from VASP by an earlier build*, *LSCALE*.
- `[mlip][core][umbrella]` **`tadah data dedup`** keeps the first of each set of equal structures, matches atoms one to one in any order and through any lattice translation of either cell (so whether two structures are the same depends neither on the atom order, nor on how the cell is written, nor on which is compared with which; the copy kept is the first in file order), and needs `--threshold`. Comparing a structure with a singular cell, which 1.5.x deduplicated without the cell, is an error, and so are a threshold at which two images of a position could both match (above about 0.2887 of the shortest lattice vector) and an extremely skewed cell basis.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` **A structure without a stress tensor gets stress priority 0** (`sweight`) from every DFT reader, so a `STRESS true` fit ignores its stress instead of fitting it to zero; one INFO line per file says how many. A `.castep` structure without forces gets force priority 0 the same way. **A dataset value that is not a finite number** (`nan`, `inf`, `1e309`), or an atom line with fewer than its seven fields or a non-number among them, **is refused**, naming the file and the structure; a whitespace-only line separates structures. **CASTEP `.md`/`.geom`:** a malformed frame is an error naming the file, the frame and the line; a last frame the file ends inside is skipped with one warning. **CORE `Matrix3d`** has no scalar `+=`/`-=` and gains `A*B`.
- `[mlip][hpo][core][umbrella]` **The force and stress metrics leave out structures with priority 0:** `tadah predict -A` and `tadah hpo`'s `PC_FRMSE`/`PC_SRMSE_GPA` skip a structure stored with force priority 0 (`fweight`) in the force metrics and one with stress priority 0 (`sweight`) in the stress metrics, as training does; they compared the prediction with the zeros stored in place of the missing data.
