A dataset in which every structure has that priority 0 is not measured: `nan`, left out of the average, and named in a note (`predict -A`) or a journal line (`tadah hpo`); a run in which no dataset has the data is refused, naming the files (`predict -A` with `-F`/`-S` before predicting, `tadah hpo` at startup), and so is an indexed `PC_FRMSE` or `PC_SRMSE_GPA` weight above 0 on such a dataset, and a dataset the filters leave empty in `predict -A`. A priority of 0 set by hand also keeps a structure out of these metrics. Docs: *Dataset Format*, *Structures without forces or a stress tensor*.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — the `--validation` file of `tadah hpo` holds `DBFILE`, `EFILTER` and `FFILTER` (and `CONFIG`); every other key is refused at its line**, with its reason, by the run and by `--check`: the keys that set a fit (`FORCE`, `STRESS`, `ENERGY`, `ENORM`, the error scales, `WDBFILE`), the retired keys, and those of the model, of its fit and of the run. 1.5.0 accepted them and ignored them.
- `[hpo][mlip][core][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo`: validation follows its metrics.** `FORCE`/`STRESS` (`-c`, or `-F`/`-S`, which take precedence) decide only what training fits, as in `tadah train`; validation predicts forces whenever `PC_FRMSE` is set and stresses whenever `PC_SRMSE_GPA` is set, and `best_pot.tadah` keeps the training values. `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` covers `PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false`. `FORCE` and `STRESS` in the `--validation` file are refused at their line. The journal's POTENTIAL SETUP states `Fit forces (FORCE)` and `Fit stresses (STRESS)` with where each came from (configuration file, command line, default), and `Validation predicts`. With `FORCE`/`STRESS` true and no `PC_FRMSE`/`PC_SRMSE_GPA`, validation no longer predicts forces/stresses (faster; a validation energy may differ in the last bit for `D2_LJ`, `D2_ZBL` and `D2_Blip5`). `M_KRR` potentials (`pot.tadah`, `best_pot.tadah`) now record `FORCE` and `STRESS`, as `M_BLR`'s do. Docs: *Configuration File*, *What validation predicts*.
- `[core][models][mlip][md][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` **Version single source of truth works in every layout.** Every module reads `[TADAH].version` from CORE's blueprint *after* its Tadah dependencies are available, so fetched (non-sibling) builds no longer report `0.0.0`; a missing blueprint or version line is a configure error, and the blueprint is a configure dependency (a bump re-runs CMake, so `tadah --version` cannot go stale). The docs read the same file from the sibling CORE checkout (cloning only on CI/RTD), and the landing page shows `|release|`.
- `[umbrella]` Docs: Nested Fitting is split into *Background*, *Quick Start* and *Configuration File* pages next to *Optimizer* and *Worked Examples*; every key, default and behaviour on those pages was checked against the code (`BOUTPUT` is a cadence over new bests, `DUMP`/`BDUMP` naming, `params.tadah` column order, DLIB offers `BFGS`/`GFS` only, `INIT METHOD` is the canonical keyword, `RESTART` needs both `AFTER_STAGNATION` and `MAX`, root keys a `PC_LAMMPS` line depends on must precede it, …).
- `[umbrella]` Docs: the old `hpo.html` redirects to the pages it was split into and keeps deep links working. `#background` and `#nested-fitting` open *Background*, `#quick-start` opens *Quick Start*, sections that moved to the ANNEAL page since 1.3.0-beta.1 follow it, `#best-only-snapshots` and `#bound-transforms` open the sections that replaced them, and every other anchor is carried over to *Configuration File*, which kept the old section ids; `#see-also` (a list of links dropped in the split) and any anchor that page does not have open its top. Anchors of the tagged 1.0.0/1.1.1 page follow their sections too (`#hpo-quick-start` opens *Quick Start*). Every `html` build checks the old page's anchors (as it was at 1.0.0, 1.1.1, on `develop`, at 1.3.0-beta.1 and on `v1-dev` before the split) against the built site; other HTML builders warn that the redirect is unchecked, and Read the Docs search skips it.
- `[hpo]` `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` (and `TADAH_PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`) are parsed strictly; an invalid value is an error instead of being ignored.
- `[cmake-tools]` Local branch detection uses the upstream ref (`git rev-parse @{upstream}`) instead of parsing `git status`, which truncated names at the first digit.
- `[core][models][mlip][md][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` Every repo takes Catch2 and its test discovery from cmake-tools (`tadah_setup_tests()`, `include(Catch)`); the vendored copies of `catch.hpp`, `Catch.cmake` and `CatchAddTests.cmake` are removed, and cmake-tools is fetched at `v1-dev` instead of `main`. `CONTRIBUTING.md` describes the discovery rules.
- `[umbrella]` Docs: *Configuration File* has a *Stopping a run* section — the exit status, the journal's `Optimiser status` for each way a run ends, what `best_pot.tadah` and the history files hold after a signal, an error or `SIGKILL`, how a SLURM batch job delivers its advance-warning `SIGUSR2` to `tadah` (`exec` with `--signal=B:USR2@…`, or `srun`), and what a multi-rank `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` run reports; *Potential archives*, `PC_ISOLATION`, `MULTISTART`, `PRESTAGE` and `RESTART` describe the new behaviour.
- `[mlip]` The pre-flight audit hint for a critical hyperparameter that is fixed in the seed and absent from every `OPTIM` block names the cause, a seed value that "misses the neighbour distances of the training data (e.g. a cutoff or radial grid that does not overlap the neighbour shells)", instead of "(BASINS-style)".
- `[umbrella]` Docs: *Installation* lists `TADAH_HPO_MPI` among the build options and describes the MPI-parallel HPO build (the old *Massively Parallel Version* section, its `tadah_mpi` naming, its `libtadah.mpi.hpo` name and the `-DTADAH_BUILD_MPI=ON` example are gone); the "not functional" MPI caveats in the introduction, *Training*, the Nested Fitting quick start and configuration pages are removed; *Performance & parallelism* describes the one-process worker pool and `TADAH_HPO_MPI` (the old `hpo.html` anchors `#desktop-build-openmp` and `#mpi-build` are routed to its renamed subsections); the configuration-keys page marks deprecated keys and shows no usage example for them (`read_toml.py`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A `SEED` in the `RESTART` block seeds its `METHOD` chain**, so restarts are reproducible: restart r draws its fresh points with `SEED + r`, and the journal's `RESTART r/MAX` line shows it (`RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED 43, SEED 6`). The key was accepted and ignored. Without it each restart still draws from `std::random_device` (one draw per run, which the journal names: the `RESTART` stream entry above); the first round and the optimiser seeds `RESTART` derives for each round are unchanged. The restart points come from a stream of their own, not the optimisers' (the `TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` entry below), so the `RESTART` `SEED` may equal an optimiser's. *Configuration File* (*RESTART decorator*) describes it.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A Sobol seed selects a whole block of *K* points** (`METHOD SOBOL K` in an `INIT` or `RESTART` block): seed *s* draws block *b* = *s* mod 65536 (the seed's 16 low bits, as before), the points *b·K* … *b·K + K − 1*, so seeds that differ modulo 65536 draw disjoint points, among them the seeds `SEED + r` of consecutive restart rounds. A seed skipped *s* points, so under a `RESTART` `SEED` consecutive restart rounds shared *K − 1* of their *K* fresh points, and an `INIT` block with the same `SEED` and *K* overlapped the first restart. `SOBOL 1` is unchanged: seed *s* draws point *s* mod 65536. *Configuration File* (*INIT block*, *RESTART decorator*) describes it.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **The key order of the HPO dimensions is the stable, documented contract** (*Configuration File*, *Dimension order*): by `OPTIM` key, then by index (`FIXWEIGHT`: by `FIXINDEX` slot), whatever the order of the `OPTIM` lines. The docs now say that it also numbers ASA's `PARAM <Name>[i]` (the `dim` column of `hpo_search_space.tsv`) and orders the hpotargets `tadah refit` writes; declaration order was considered and not adopted. The diagnostic-marker row for `RESTART i/M -- fresh init from …` names the `RESTART` block's init method, which is what the run prints.
- `[cmake-tools][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **Optional-fixture tests are reported as Skipped, not Passed.** Catch2 v2 has no skip, so a test case whose optional data was absent returned after a warning and CTest counted a pass that checked nothing. cmake-tools' `tadah_test_skip.hpp` provides `TADAH_SKIP(<reason>)`, which returns from the test case; when the test case ends without a failed assertion, a Catch listener writes the marker line `TADAH-SKIP: <reason>` to standard error, and every entry `catch_discover_tests()` registers has a `SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` for that line (`TadahTestSkip.cmake`; `PROPERTIES` that set one stop the build). A test case that asks to be skipped and fails -- before the request, in another `SECTION` or after it -- stays Failed. The two MLIP `NNFinder: real CH4 phase-I structure` cases (`TADAH_TEST_PHASEI_DB`) and HPO's Corana 1987 oracle use it; `hpo_strategy_matrix` (`TADAH_HPO_INTEGRATION_BUNDLE`) exits with its `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` 77. With the documentation tools installed and without the optional data, the suite now reports exactly three Skipped entries (`tadah_docs_tools` is Skipped too when one of its tools is missing; with the `tadah.lammps_mpi_*` entries under *Added*, a build with `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP` also skips those six unless `TADAH_LMP_MPI` and `TADAH_LMP_MPIEXEC` name an MPI `lmp`). An explicitly set `TADAH_TEST_PHASEI_DB` that names a file that cannot be read fails the two phase-I cases instead of skipping them. HPO's test build requires Python 3, as the umbrella's did: its registry drift gate `hpo_anneal_registry_sync` silently did not exist without it. `CONTRIBUTING.md` and the cmake-tools README give the rules; `ctest -R tadah_catch` checks the marker through a real CTest run (`tadah_catch_skip_marker`).
- `[core][mlip]` `Mat6R3C::row()` and `Matrix3d::row()`/`col()` return const copies, so assigning to one (`m.row(n) = v`), which compiled and changed only the copy -- how the `DIMER` distances were lost -- is a compile error; `Mat6R3C::set_row()` writes a row, and `calc_dimer` uses it.
- `[models][mlip][lammps][core][umbrella]` **A `DIMER` potential whose descriptors name specific element pairs is refused when it is loaded** (`tadah train`, `tadah predict`, `pair_style tadah`). The `DIMER` evaluators treat every atom as Z=1 (with the pair weight of Z=1, not the `WATOM` weight of the element), so a `TYPE2B`/`TYPEMB` declared for `N N` applied to no pair and the model predicted from zero descriptors without a word (0 eV from `tadah predict` for the published Tadah!N2 with `N N` pairs). Such a potential now stops with `DIMER evaluates every atom as Z=1; declare TYPE2B/TYPEMB with * * element pairs`; the published Tadah!N2 (`* *`) loads as before. The `DIMER` key's description and *Trained MLIPs* say so.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`PC_OBSERVABLE` columns are `L(Ecoh)[k]` (`loss.tadah`) and `Ecoh[k]` (`outvar.tadah`), `k` the 1-based ordinal of the line**, and the journal names the term `cohesive_energy[k]`; they were 0-based and cut at every `DIGITS` (`L(cohesive*`). **An unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` resolves to `ANALYTICAL`** with `PC_OBSERVABLE` in each block that runs an optimiser and obtains gradients, the `STRATEGY`-level block and `EXPLORE` (the journal records it; an `INNER` block is not resolved, since MLSL and AUGLAG hand their local search the enclosing block's gradient), and **`ANALYTICAL` is refused at startup on a setup the analytical gradient does not cover** (e.g. `OALGO` other than 4, `FIXINDEX`, no training data, a `PC_OBSERVABLE` line with `--loss MAPE`/`RMSLE` or `--unconverged force-scaled`), naming the setting or the line and `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`: such a `PC_OBSERVABLE` run now needs those settings or `HYBRID`. *Configuration File* documents the columns, the resolution and the settings (*Typed observables*, the `GRAD_STRATEGY` row, the `INNER` block, weight pinning and runs without training data), and *Quick Start* and *Background* follow.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`GRAD_STRATEGY` inside an `INNER` block is a startup error that names the line**, like `SEED` there (`Error on line N: GRAD_STRATEGY is not accepted inside an INNER block: the enclosing block's GRAD_STRATEGY applies to its local search; set GRAD_STRATEGY on the enclosing STRATEGY block`). MLSL and AUGLAG hand their `INNER` search the gradient of their own objective, which the enclosing block's `GRAD_STRATEGY` obtains, so a value written in the `INNER` block was accepted and never used. Move the line to the enclosing `STRATEGY`, `EXPLORE` or `REFINE` block. *Configuration File* (`INNER` block, the `GRAD_STRATEGY` row, *Typed observables*) says so.
- `[cmake-tools][umbrella]` **On Read the Docs the Tadah modules are fetched, and the docs clone CORE and HPO, at `$READTHEDOCS_GIT_IDENTIFIER`**, the branch or tag Read the Docs checked out; `$READTHEDOCS_VERSION_NAME` and `$READTHEDOCS_VERSION` follow it (`tadah_get_git_branch`, `docs/source/config/source_locator.py`). The `latest` and `stable` versions are named after no ref, so their builds fetched and cloned a ref called `latest` or `stable`. `$CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME` still comes first. Tests: `ctest -R tadah_git_branch` (every source of the ref, the upstream and local branch of a throw-away repository, a detached HEAD and a tarball), `test_source_locator.py`.
- `[umbrella][lammps]` Docs: *Installation* describes how `TADAH_GIT_BRANCH`, the ref the Tadah modules are fetched at, is found when it is not given (the CI and Read the Docs variables, then the upstream or local branch of the checkout CMake was started on); it said the default was `main`. In the LAMMPS build that checkout is LAMMPS's, so the instructions pass `-DTADAH_GIT_BRANCH=v1-dev` (or a release tag such as `v1.6.0`), and a release tarball needs its tag. Tadah.LAMMPS' READMEs say the same. `RELEASING.md` describes the actual process: `v1-dev` replaces `develop`; cmake-tools is tagged first and the release commit pins its tag in all seven repos; tag pipelines fetch the modules at the tag; CI builds no docs on tags and Read the Docs builds from its webhook; the push order (cmake-tools, then the leaves, `core` last, or `-o ci.skip`); tarballs need `-DTADAH_GIT_BRANCH`. `CONTRIBUTING.md` names `v1-dev` as the working branch. *Installation*, the README quickstart, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and Tadah.LAMMPS' READMEs clone `v1-dev` or a release tag by name (`git clone -b v1-dev`, `-b v1.6.0` with `-DTADAH_GIT_BRANCH=v1.6.0`); *Installation* said to clone `main`, which carries no 1.x release, and a plain `git clone` checks out the repository's default branch. The `CONTRIBUTING.md` sibling checkouts get the directory names the develop build looks for (`cmake-tools` was upper-cased) and its develop build sets `TADAH_DEVELOP=ON` (`CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Develop` did not take the siblings). `RELEASING.md` states which repo fetches which, tags `tadah.lammps` before `hpo`, which fetches it (and pushes `v1-dev` in the reverse order), and sets the Read the Docs default branch and version.
- `[core][models][mlip][md][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` The README badges and links and the community-file pointers of every repo point to the `v1-dev` line (GitLab `v1-dev`, `https://tadah.readthedocs.io/v1-dev/`); they pointed to `develop`.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo` exits with status 1 when the run ends on its own and no evaluation produced a usable loss**; it exited with 0. A job script that took 0 for success now sees such a run fail (*Fixed* below; *Configuration File*, *Stopping a run*).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`FAILSCORE` must be a positive finite number**, and so must `PC_FAILPROBE --failscore`: 0 or below is an error at its line (`FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got '0'`). With 0 or below there was no failure band, so a run whose every evaluation failed ended with status 0, `COMPLETED` and a `best_pot.tadah` that held a failed evaluation, and `PC_FAILPROBE --failscore 0` made every probe read as failed. Negative losses (negative-weight `--varloss` terms) stay usable under any positive `FAILSCORE`, and a `PC_LAMMPS` line's `--failscore`, a substituted variable value, may still be any finite number (*Configuration File*).
- `[core][models][mlip][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` **BREAKING — DM_REAM potentials evaluate F(0) = 0 by construction (`EMBF0 ZERO`, the new default).** The embedding basis is zero-referenced, g_m(rho) - g_m(0), on every path (train, predict, hpo, `refit`, `refit --edit`, LAMMPS generic/dimer/EAMFAST), so an isolated atom has energy 0.0 exactly with a linear model and BIAS false, for every HPO candidate, without any penalty term. For linear models forces and stresses are unchanged and every atomic energy moves by -F(0) (F(0) of the stored WEIGHTS), so energy differences at fixed atom count and cohesive/formation energies are unchanged; for non-linear models energies and forces change. **Migration:** add `EMBF0 FREE` to a potential written by tadah 1.5.0 or earlier to reproduce its energies (bit-identical). Every DM_REAM potential now carries an `EMBF0` line, so 1.5.0 binaries refuse it ("Invalid key: EMBF0"). Training a DM_REAM model on absolute energies under ZERO needs ESHIFT/ESHIFT_ATOM/ESHIFT_DBATOM (or BIAS true, whose intercept column shifts FIXINDEX/FIXWEIGHT indices and disables EAMFAST; a HINT says so); for a retrain or a `tadah hpo -c` run from a potential (a `tadah refit` potential with a `DBFILE` line) the toggle is the run's own, `--eshift-atom` or `--eshift-dbatom`, which the HINTs and the `refit.hpotarget` note name: a toggle line of the potential is not read. External setfl converters that read WEIGHTS must tabulate F(rho) - F(0) unless the potential says `EMBF0 FREE`. `EMBF0` takes `ZERO` or `FREE` only (any case); another value is an error at its line (CORE `allowed_values`), and a potential without a DM_REAM block does not carry it (writers drop it with a warning). Docs: `descriptors.rst` (DM_REAM), `refit.rst`, `training.rst`, `nfp_configuration.rst` (*DM_REAM potentials and the embedding reference*).
- `[core][models][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **Numbers keep full precision, and potential files are exact.** `Context` (18 fixed decimals), mJoin and `LIN`/`GEOM` grids (NUMERIC decimals), LSCALE/ESHIFT (6 digits), LAMMPS read-backs (`%.15g`) and FAILSCORE clamps (`%f`) rounded values on their way through the code; all are lossless now. **In potential files `NUMERIC` now means significant digits (`%.{N}g`), default 18 (was 12), so every double reads back bit-identically.** **The cutoffs and radial grids are written exactly whatever `NUMERIC` is**: every potential `tadah train`, `tadah refit`, `tadah refit --edit` and `tadah hpo` write (`best_pot.tadah`, its `DUMP` and `BDUMP` archives) records the numbers of `RCUT2B`, `RCUTMB`, `CGRID2B`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRID2B` and `SGRIDMB` below 17 as the shortest text that reads back as the same value (`RCUT2B 5.58190742024` at `NUMERIC 6`), so a potential written with `NUMERIC` below 17 loads in the `tadah` that wrote it: a load checks these keys exactly (`D2_EAM` and `DM_EAM` refuse a cutoff other than that of their setfl file, new in this release: the `D2_EAM` of 1.5.0 replaced it with the file's after a WARNING, and its `DM_EAM` could not be built from a configuration; a knot channel's cutoff is its largest knot; the knot and Blip descriptors warn of a knot or a bump past the cutoff, and `tadah hpo`'s bump-support gate refuses its `-c` potential when a `Cut_Dummy` bump reaches past it). The `NUMERIC` WARNING names the keys written exactly, and the configuration-keys page marks them (*In a potential file*; blueprint field `exact_in_potential`). Potential writers never write NUMERIC (`tadah refit` now reads it too); an explicit value below 17 is honoured with a WARNING. Potentials written by 1.5.0 `tadah hpo` carry an auto-inserted `NUMERIC 12` line, which no run reads (a run that reads a potential reads none of its run settings: the BREAKING entry of the `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` and `VERBOSE` key lines), so a retrain, `tadah hpo -c` and `refit --edit` of such a file write exact potentials. Numerics shift at the ~1e-13 level; newly trained LSCALE/ESHIFT potentials may differ by up to ~1e-5 eV/atom. Docs: `training.rst` ("Number format"). Tests: CORE `test_context.cpp`, `test_number_format.cpp`, `test_blueprint_exact_in_potential.cpp`; MLIP `test_dump_pot_file.cpp`; Tadah.LAMMPS `test_numeric_exact_keys_load.cpp` (pair_coeff loads such a D2_EAM + DM_EAM potential); test_tadah `Subcommand: train with NUMERIC below 17 ...` (D2_EAM, DM_EAM, a D2_mJoin with D2_EAM, D2_Blip; `tadah predict` loads each), `Subcommand: a Cut_Dummy bump that train writes with NUMERIC below 17 stays inside the cutoff for predict and hpo` (D2_Blip, D2_Blip5, a DM_REAM Blip density: no bump WARNING, and in an NFP build `tadah hpo` takes the potential as its `-c` potential) and `Subcommand: hpo with NUMERIC below 17 ...` (NFP: `best_pot.tadah` and the archives); the docs tools' `test_read_toml.py`.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` **Dataset files and exports follow the same rule.** `tadah data convert|merge|split|sample|dedup|balance` wrote NUMERIC fixed decimals (values below ~0.01 lost digits, below 5e-19 became 0), so a rewritten dataset trained on slightly different values. They now write NUMERIC significant digits, default 18 = exact, and so do the LAMMPS data and VASP POSCAR exports of `tadah data write` (the CASTEP cell export keeps decimal places); an explicit NUMERIC below 17 warns. The wider default also widens prediction and analysis outputs. Every `tadah` reader accepts what the writers produce, subnormals included (a POSCAR exported with a subnormal coordinate failed to load).
- `[umbrella]` **Toolchain:** floating-point `std::to_chars` is now required (libstdc++ >= 11, libc++ >= 14; `installation.rst`). Example `API/ex_1` writes its potential through `Context::write_potential`, and its config drops the lossy `NUMERIC 14` (predictions unchanged).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Changes in how runs end.** RESTART with NLopt now restarts on stagnation; under MULTISTART each start, and under PRESTAGE each refinement seed, gets its own stagnation window, and after the last one a batch in which any stagnated restarts the whole strategy (all starts, or both PRESTAGE phases), whether or not it improved the best; an exhausted RESTART ends `STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in W evals after R restart(s)`. A MULTISTART whose every start fails fails the run (was `COMPLETED`, exit code 0). SIGINT during NLopt, a MULTISTART start, PT/PA calibration or NOISE_CALIBRATION ends as `SHUTDOWN`, exit status 128 + the signal number. `NLOPT_ROUNDOFF_LIMITED` is graceful. Errors read `FAILED -- <error>` (was `STOPPED with error:`); one rule, in `drive_run()`, decides every ending, and a best potential that could not be saved ends the run `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <error>` (exit status 1) however it ends, a failed reset of `best_pot.tadah` before the first evaluation included (it was a startup error without a `RESULT` section). `TADAH_HPO_MPI` builds: a rank that exits non-zero and is not joined by every other rank within 30 s aborts the job instead of leaving the others blocked. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (*Stopping a run*).
- `[core][models][mlip][hpo][lammps][umbrella]` **BREAKING — DM_REAM potentials declare a density form, `RHOFORM SIGNED | NONNEG | MONOTONE`, with no default.** `SIGNED` is the density of every tadah before 1.6.0, bit for bit (rho = W f_c sum a_n psi_n, amplitudes of any sign); `NONNEG` requires every amplitude >= 0, so rho >= 0 by construction; `MONOTONE` builds rho from integrated-bump steps S_n with amplitudes >= 0 (bump bases only, a non-negative non-increasing cutoff), so rho >= 0 and non-increasing, and the lattice density falls monotonically under expansion. Every path (train, predict, hpo, `refit`, `refit --edit`, the LAMMPS generic and EAMFAST kernels) evaluates the declared form. A DM_REAM potential or configuration without the key is refused with a migration message, and another value is an error at its line of the file (as for `EMBF0`): `tadah predict` refuses a key-less potential, and `pair_coeff` does too, its LAMMPS error now carrying the reason; the LAMMPS banner prints the form. The configuration of `tadah refit --edit` (`-c`), and that of `tadah analysis bfunc` with a `POTENTIAL` line, may set only the potential's own `RHOFORM` (`RHOFORM conflict: pot.tadah declares RHOFORM SIGNED and the configuration file sets RHOFORM NONNEG. ...`), and none for a potential without one. **Migration:** add `RHOFORM SIGNED` to a DM_REAM potential or configuration written before tadah 1.6.0 to use it unchanged. Every DM_REAM potential tadah writes carries the key, so older binaries refuse it (`Invalid key: RHOFORM`). Rebuild `lmp` and `tadah`. Docs: `descriptors.rst` (DM_REAM), `training.rst`.
- `[models][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah refit`, `refit --edit` and `--retarget` work in the declared form.** The refit configuration must set `RHOFORM`. `NONNEG` and `MONOTONE` fit non-negative amplitudes by non-negative least squares in both modes (compact: variable projection on the passive set, Ceres bounds); `MONOTONE` needs a bump basis (`REFIT_BASIS Blip`/`Blip5`, since the compact default is `Knot5`); a table the form cannot follow is refused before fitting. `--edit --remove` keeps every density amplitude >= 0. The hpotarget keeps `AMPGRIDMB` boxes non-negative under the positive forms and records the form in a header line that `--retarget` honours. Dense `SIGNED` refits are unchanged apart from the `RHOFORM SIGNED` line. Dense non-negative fits cost more as `NRHO_BASIS` grows. Docs: `refit.rst` ("Density form").
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo` under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE`:** `AMPGRIDMB` AUTO boxes are clamped at 0 (origin `AUTO FRAC>=0`, an implicit `GROUP_FLOOR 1e-3`), boxes that reach an invalid density (`AMPGRIDMB` lo < 0, `WATOM` lo <= 0, `MONOTONE` `SGRIDMB` lo <= 0) are refused, no candidate with an amplitude below 0 is evaluated (a coordinate within rounding below 0 is evaluated on 0; one further below lies outside the box and is scored, not evaluated), and an amplitude at its 0 bound is marked `LO*` rather than bound pressure. In every form: a WARM seed must declare the run's `RHOFORM` when density keys are searched, and `OPTIM` on structure keys (`RHOFORM`, `TYPE2B`, `TYPEMB`, `RCTYPE2B`, `RCTYPEMB`, `KDEG2B`, `KDEGMB`, `KDEGEMB`) is an error at its line. Finite differences keep to the box with either step rule, and an optimiser that ignores the bounds (`CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`) has its startup WARNING, which says that a point it proposes outside the box is not evaluated. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst`, `nfp_background.rst`.
- `[core][models][umbrella]` **`EMBF0` or `RHOFORM` without a DM_REAM block is reported once per key and process on standard error, like a deprecated key** (`WARNING: Key RHOFORM is ignored: it applies only to a DM_REAM many-body block, and the potential has none (...)`), wherever the potential or configuration is used (`tadah train`, `predict`, `analysis`, `hpo`), also when only a configuration file given with `-c` sets it; `pair_style tadah` in LAMMPS writes it as a LAMMPS warning on rank 0, in `log.lammps` and on the screen, each time `pair_coeff` loads such a potential (see the entry on the notices `pair_style tadah` writes). It is still accepted and never written into a potential. In Tadah! 1.5.0 an `EMBF0` without a DM_REAM block was a Logger WARNING (`EMBF0 is ignored and not written: the potential has no DM_REAM many-body block.`), which `pair_style tadah` and `VERBOSE 0` hid, and an `EMBF0` in a potential that `tadah predict` loaded was not reported. *Descriptors* (*RHOFORM and EMBF0 without a DM_REAM block*) and *Training* say so.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **OPTIM keys are case-insensitive**: the key is upper-cased when the line is read, so `OPTIM weights` is refused like `OPTIM WEIGHTS`, `OPTIM fixweight` maps through `FIXINDEX`, `OPTIM cgrid2b` arms `BUMPGATE` and bounds the neighbour list, and the labels of `params.tadah` and the journal are upper-case (`OPTIM ampgridmb (1) 0 1` gives `AMPGRIDMB[1]`). A lower-case key was searched under its own spelling and missed every rule keyed on the upper-case name. An index an earlier `OPTIM` line of the same key already selects is an error at the second line (a second box replaced the first silently); lines that select other indices still add to the key. *HPO configuration* (OPTIM) says so.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **OPTIM on `DIMER`, `FIXINDEX`, `MODEL` and on `OALGO (1)`, the training algorithm, is refused at its line**, as on the other structure keys (a searched value was truncated to an integer, broke `FIXINDEX` or moved the data's bond length); `OPTIM OALGO (2)`, the rcond of options 1 and 3, stays searchable.
- `[models][hpo][mlip][umbrella]` **A radial bump width must be > 0 in every `RHOFORM`**: `SGRID2B` of `D2_Blip`/`D2_Blip5`, `SGRIDMB` of a DM_REAM `Blip`/`Blip5` density. `tadah hpo` refuses an OPTIM box whose low bound on such a width is <= 0 at its line and a `-c` potential with such a width at startup (naming each key, bump and value); `tadah refit` refuses an `INIT PHI`/`INIT RHO` anchor with eta <= 0 in every form (it was fitted as its mirror, or failed with "zero entry in SGRIDMB"); `tadah analysis check_bounds` fails such corners; `BUMPGATE` counts a candidate width <= 0 as a violation instead of skipping it. A bump is even in eta, so a negative width evaluated as its mirror and the bump-support checks skipped it. The model still loads a potential with such a width. *HPO configuration* and *Refit* say so.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Compact refit: FUSE's `MAXEVAL` bounds the whole stage**: the held re-run passes after the joint solve share its Ceres iterations, and with none left the held re-run is skipped and the linear re-solve result kept (`FUSE held re-run skipped: MAXEVAL <n> spent` in `refit_opt.log`); each pass had the full budget. *Refit* says so.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Compact refit under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve`: the FUSE refine of each function runs with FUSE's own `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL`**, so curve results change where REFINE's or FUSE's stop keys were set. It ran with REFINE's (an L-BFGS REFINE's evaluation budget read as Ceres iterations), also with REFINE `ENABLED false`, and FUSE's own keys had no effect (FUSE `MAXEVAL 1` and `500` wrote the same potential). phi, rho and F each get FUSE's whole `MAXEVAL`, and the refit log gives each refine's Ceres term (`FUSE[phi] curve score 0.0304 -> 0.0287 (NO_CONVERGENCE(501it, maxiter))`). FUSE reads `GTOL` in both modes (default 1e-14, as before). Without these keys a curve run changes only where a FUSE refine reached REFINE's default 1000 iterations (FUSE's is 2000). REFINE `ALGO NONE` with a stop criterion is an error in curve mode too (*Refitting*).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **BREAKING for bounded gradient searches — finite-difference gradients stay inside the `OPTIM` box, with either step rule**. `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` and `HYBRID`'s finite differences took the central difference at every point, so near a bound one point lay outside the box: evaluated there (in 1.5.0, `DLIB:BFGS` searches over `RCUT2B` in [4, 6] on the Ta data of HPO recorded RCUT2B = 6.000002, outside the box, as their best); Ceres' numeric differentiation (`fd_kernel ceres`, the default under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`) had no bounds at all. One stencil now serves both step rules — the in-house one and Ceres', which Tadah evaluates itself (Ceres' gradient bit for bit inside the box; `fd_kernel ceres` needs no `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES`) — central inside the box and one-sided into it within a step of a bound, (-3 f(u) + 4 f(u+s) - f(u+2s))/2s, second order at the same cost for `NUMERICAL`; `diff_method 1` (FORWARD) is honoured with either rule (it was ignored by the in-house kernel and by `HYBRID`, as `fd_kernel` was by `HYBRID`), `diff_method 2` (RIDDERS) is refused. A point outside the box — only `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` proposes one (`LN_NEWUOA`, which ignored the bounds too, is refused: the BREAKING `LN_NEWUOA` entry above) — is not evaluated, and its gradient is the slope of its score (the BREAKING entry on points outside the `OPTIM` box, above); a coordinate proposed outside its box by no more than rounding (8 units in the last place of the bound's scale, in real or in the optimiser's coordinates) is evaluated on the bound, recorded there and counted in DIAGNOSTICS (`Snapped onto the box`) without a warning — under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` NLopt's own arithmetic rounds points on a bound up to 2 ulps past it (`LN_COBYLA`, `LN_PRAXIS`, `LN_BOBYQA`, the unscaled DIRECT variants), and they were evaluated outside the box and warned about; every point proposed outside the box is counted (a WARNING at the first, a DIAGNOSTICS line), and `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` gets one startup WARNING per run saying what becomes of one. The step is never shrunk: a box too narrow for it is refused at startup (an `fd_step` above 1/3 under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`; under `NONE` checked over the whole box), naming the step's source (`PARAM` or the `NOISE` block's `AUTO`), the largest `fd_step` that fits, and `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO` or a `LOG` box as alternatives; `fd_step`, `fd_kernel` and `diff_method` are refused where they never take effect (an `INNER` block, `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL`, a derivative-free optimiser), and so is the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` where the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser measures no slopes (a derivative-free one or ASA, where it was ignored with a note on stderr, or `ANALYTICAL`: `NOISE AUTO has no effect: NLOPT:LN_SBPLX measures no slopes; remove AUTO from the NOISE block.`), while it now derives `fd_step` for an `MLSL` or `AUGLAG` whose local search takes gradients too, and it is refused beside a `PARAM fd_step` written in the block (`NOISE AUTO has no effect: PARAM fd_step 0.0001 is set; remove one of them.`; the written step was taken, with a note on stderr); ASA's tangent and curvature probes, as ASA computes them, must fit its boxes (`Delta_X`), and the bundled ASA's exit curvature evaluates the points it tests (TADAH-PATCH 8: where both x ± Delta|x| lay in the box it evaluated (1 ± Delta) x, which can round an ulp past a bound; `Curvature_0` 0 or -1 only). `HYBRID` differences every parameter when no analytical derivative can be formed (it returned a zero gradient, so a local search stopped at its start) and takes the loss the optimiser has just evaluated at the point instead of evaluating it again. The ends of a `LOG`/`ASINH` box map to exactly its bounds (`OPTIM X 0.01 10 LOG` recorded X = 10.000000000000002). The journal shows the finite differences of each phase in its `OPTIMISER` section, the first one-sided difference of each dimension, and DIAGNOSTICS counts that are printed only when not zero. Runs whose gradients never come within a step of a bound are unchanged, bit for bit, but for an evaluation at an end of a `LOG`/`ASINH` box (a `GRID` over one) or, rarely, of a linear box under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`, which is now exactly its bound, and an NLopt point within rounding outside the box under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`, now evaluated on the bound. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (*Finite-difference gradients and the OPTIM box*, the `PARAM`, `GRAD_STRATEGY`, CERES and DLIB:BFGS `PARAM` rows, the positive density forms, `NOISE` `AUTO`), `optimizer.rst`, `asa.rst`, `installation.rst` (`TADAH_ENABLE_CERES`).
- `[mlip]` A left-handed cell has a positive volume (`Structure::get_volume` is |a0 · (a1 × a2)|); a left-handed basis, which CASTEP accepts and a POSCAR can hold, flipped the sign of its stress design rows and predictions, gave a negative density and stopped the native relaxer's Iso mode converging. The LAMMPS and VASP exports write such a cell as the right-handed (a0, a1, −a2) — the LAMMPS file from `abc origin` a2, so every atom stays in the box — and refuse a singular cell.
- `[mlip]` `StructureTransformation` moves the lattice vectors (the rows of the cell) with the atoms: `rotate`/`shear` use cell·Mᵀ (they used M·cell), `rotate` and the mirrors also move the forces, `mirrorPlane`/`mirrorHkl` reflect the cell, and `replicate` builds the supercell along the rows, scales the energy, clears the neighbour list and refuses a factor below 1.
- `[core]` A `Matrix3d` no longer converts silently to a `Vec3d` (the conversion took column 0 of the storage, for a cell (a0x, a1x, a2x)), and the matrix/vector scalar operators take numbers only.
- `[mlip]` A neighbour list is refused by the descriptors (and the HPO gradient walkers) when it was built with a cutoff below the model's, instead of being used silently; `NNFinder` refuses invalid input (a non-finite or non-positive cutoff, a non-finite cell or position, a singular cell, a cell vector shorter than 2^-200 Å or longer than 2^200 Å) and a cell so small that the search would span more than 10⁴ image cells (a cube once the cutoff reaches 10 edges, wherever its atoms are stored; before, only beyond 11 edges, and some multi-atom cubes in between got wrong lists). The native relaxer's neighbour-list cache keeps a list exactly while it provably holds every pair (a uniform expansion keeps it, a thin cell squeezed along one axis drops it) and caches nothing with `nn_skin 0`.
- `[hpo]` DIAGNOSTICS count the points proposed outside the `OPTIM` box (`Out-of-box points`) and the finite differences taken without a point of another kind (`Mixed-kind stencils`), above.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **BREAKING for NLopt gradient searches — `MAXEVAL` counts every evaluation of an NLopt search too**, as with `DLIB:BFGS` and the CERES driver: each loss, each finite-difference point and each analytical gradient (`ANALYTICAL`, `HYBRID`; one evaluation each). An NLopt search makes at most its `MAXEVAL` evaluations (a run can make more, as before: each `RESTART` launch, `MULTISTART` start and `PRESTAGE` phase has its own `MAXEVAL` or share of it, and the `NOISE` block's `CALIBRATION` repeats and `ANNEAL_PT`'s `INIT_JITTER AUTO` calibration are charged to no search); a search that the budget ends reports `MAXEVAL_REACHED`, and the journal says `NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted after 60 of MAXEVAL 60 evaluation(s)`. NLopt counted its objective calls, each a loss and a whole gradient, while the gradients' evaluations had a budget of their own: an NLopt `LD_*`/`GD_*` search, and an `MLSL`/`AUGLAG` local search, made up to about twice `MAXEVAL` evaluations (`LD_MMA`: 120 for `MAXEVAL 60`), was given `+inf` for the points it evaluated after that budget, and could end `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged …)` or `SUCCESS`; `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L` and `AUGLAG` made a few evaluations more than `MAXEVAL` (61-75 for 60). With the same `MAXEVAL` an NLopt gradient search does less and can end at a worse loss, and an NLopt `ANALYTICAL` run gets about half as many losses: raise `MAXEVAL` to keep the old totals. An `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL` still counts the NLopt calls of each local search, and the journal's `Inner stop criteria` say so for a gradient-based `INNER` search. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (the `MAXEVAL`, `GRAD_STRATEGY` and NLopt rows, *Stop criteria per backend*, *INNER block*), `optimizer.rst`.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A finite-difference gradient takes the loss the optimiser has just evaluated at its point as its centre**, with `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` as with `HYBRID`: NLopt, the CERES driver and `DLIB:BFGS` evaluate the loss at a point and then ask for its gradient, and `NUMERICAL` evaluated the point a second time first, one evaluation per gradient that counted against `MAXEVAL`. A gradient now costs 2n evaluations (CENTRAL, and one-sided next to a bound) or n (FORWARD) after the loss. Central differences are unchanged bit for bit; one-sided and forward ones use the loss the optimiser was given (under `--hotvar` a second evaluation of a point can differ). A gradient search does more with the same `MAXEVAL` and its path can change. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (*Finite-difference gradients and the OPTIM box*: the cost table, the CERES `diff_method` row).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Every `tadah hpo` optimiser records why its search ended, and the journal trailer says so; `COMPLETED` is no longer written.** The TADAH (`RANDOM`, `GRID`, `ANNEAL`, `ANNEAL_PT`, `ANNEAL_PA`), DLIB (`GFS`, `BFGS`) and CERES (`STEEPEST_DESCENT`) drivers write `<LIB>:<ALGO>: <reason>`, a fixed text (`MAXEVAL reached`, `MAXTIME exceeded`, `STOPVAL reached`, `FTOL_ABS reached`, `FTOL_REL reached`, `XTOL_REL reached`, `tol_gradinf reached`, `max_iter reached`, `T_MIN reached`, `FTOL plateau`, `all grid points evaluated`, `no optimisable dimensions`); the journal's stop line gives the evaluations and a tolerance's value, `(default)` when its key was not set. NLopt and ASA keep their own statuses. These drivers recorded none for most endings and the trailer read `COMPLETED`: `DLIB:BFGS` for a spent `MAXEVAL` and its convergence test (which wrote no line), `DLIB:GFS`, `RANDOM` and `GRID` for a spent `MAXEVAL` and the whole grid, the annealers for every stop, and the CERES driver for every ending but a rejected gradient's, a Ceres `FAILURE` such as `STEEPEST_DESCENT`'s `Numerical failure in line search` included: CERES now reports Ceres' own reason when it converged or ran out of iterations, `MAXEVAL reached` when the budget made it fail, and `STOPPED -- CERES:<ALGO>: FAILURE (<head>)` for any other failure, with the head of Ceres' message, its words before the numbers (`STOPPED -- CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT: FAILURE (Numerical failure in line search)`; the journal's stop line gives the whole message). Serial `ANNEAL` no longer ends `loop completed normally`, with no reason, when `MAXTIME` passes between two of its tests, nor `T_MIN reached` when a `MAXTIME` that passed after its schedule's last test prevented a `REANNEAL` that was due: `MAXTIME exceeded` (with no re-anneal due, the schedule's end stays `T_MIN reached`, as under `REANNEAL OFF`). A normal end without a recorded status is an internal error (`FAILED -- internal error: the optimiser ended without recording why it ended`, exit status 1). *Configuration File*: *How each backend reports its ending*, *Stopping a run*; *Simulated Annealing (ANNEAL)*, *Parallel Tempering (ANNEAL_PT)*. Test: `tests/cli_failed_evaluations` (`gfs_mixed`: `DLIB:GFS: MAXEVAL reached`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND` need `MAXEVAL`**: NLopt's StoGO ignores the forced stop by which `tadah hpo` ends a search (on `Ctrl-C` or another signal, an error, the `RESTART` stagnation stop, or a loss at or below `STOPVAL` that NLopt was not given); once stopped, StoGO calls on, each call refused in a few microseconds, until NLopt's own `MAXEVAL` ends it. Without `MAXEVAL` such a run never ended once it was stopped (`STOPVAL` or an active `RESTART` as its only stop), or waited out the rest of `MAXTIME` at 100% CPU before it reported. `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or an active `RESTART` was accepted as its stop; it is now a startup error (`NLOPT:GD_STOGO needs a stop: … set MAXEVAL (it honours no tolerance)`), in its own block (an `INNER` block does not take StoGO, a global search: see the no-nested-global entry at the top of *Changed*). Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (*Stop criteria per backend*, the `MAXEVAL` row, the `RESTART` block), `optimizer.rst`.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **BREAKING — `tadah refit` writes the many-body term as a `DM_Embed` component**, its starter target names component paths, and a dense Gaussian refit writes `Cut_Poly` (the summary above; Tadah.MLIP module's *Changed*). The refit log says what the potential declares (`the written potential's density component declares FORM SIGNED`; for a component configuration `tadah refit: FORM NONNEG: ...; the density component TA declares FORM NONNEG.`, `REFERENCE ZERO: table F(0) = ...`) and, for a component fit, its embedding window `[0, rhomax]` and remedy (the component's counts and grids). See *Refitting*.
- `[hpo]` **`tadah hpo` checks the `DUMP` and `BDUMP` directories when it reads the target file and creates them right before its first evaluation**, once it has prepared `best_pot.tadah`, not when it reads the target file. A directory it cannot create or write into is refused at its line, before any data is loaded, by `tadah hpo` and `tadah hpo --check` alike: one in a directory that does not exist (`Error on line 10: DUMP directory 'nodir/pots' cannot be created: the directory '<dir>/nodir' does not exist (tadah hpo creates only the last directory of the path)`), in a file or in a directory without write permission, and a path that is a file, a directory without write permission or a symbolic link to no file. One directory inside the directory of the other line, which the run creates first (`DUMP 1 archive` with `BDUMP 1 archive/best`) is accepted in either order of the lines; the paths are taken where the operating system puts them, symbolic links followed and `..` taken from the directory reached (`DUMP 1 ../archive` with `BDUMP 1 ../archive/best` is accepted), but a path that is itself a symbolic link must lead to an existing directory, as the run creates no directory through a link; a trailing `/` or `/.` is taken as `mkdir` takes it (`pots/.` is refused while `pots` does not exist and the other line does not create it; `DUMP 1 archive` with `BDUMP 1 archive/./best` is accepted). A run that stops before its first evaluation creates none. In 1.5.0 reading the target file created them, so a `BDUMP` directory inside a `DUMP` directory that did not exist yet was accepted only after the `DUMP` line, and a file or a directory without write permission was accepted, where no archive could be written. See *Output settings* (*Potential archives*).
- `[hpo]` **`tadah hpo` stops at startup when it cannot write its output files into the working directory**, before any data is loaded, and so does `tadah hpo --check`, with one message. Before the run opens its journal, it checks the working directory (`the working directory '<dir>' is not writable: tadah hpo writes its journal (hpo_run.log), its result files and best_pot.tadah there`) and, when they exist, `hpo_run.log`, the result files (`'loss.tadah' in the working directory '<dir>' cannot be opened: Permission denied (tadah hpo rewrites it)`), the lock of `best_pot.tadah` (`... (tadah hpo opens it to lock best_pot.tadah)`) and `best_pot.tadah`, which may be read-only but no directory; a regular file that exists is opened as the run opens it (the lock to read and write), without creating or truncating it, and refused with the open's own error; a device such as `/dev/null` is accepted, a socket is refused, and a named pipe is not opened, only checked for permission: it is accepted as `hpo_run.log` or a search-space file, which the run opens once, and refused as a history file, which the run reopens for each row (a pipe there would block the run). Once it has read the target file, it checks `hpo_search_space.tsv` and, when a box comes from an `AUTO` rule, `hpo_optim_resolved.hpo`, which it writes only then. Each path is checked where the operating system puts it, symbolic links followed (a link to a file not yet created is accepted when the file can be created there; a loop of links is refused); `best_pot.tadah`, which the run replaces, is checked by its own name, and a symbolic link there is replaced by the file. On several MPI ranks every rank stops when one cannot write its own files. In 1.5.0 such a run started regardless: without its journal, it stopped at the lock of `best_pot.tadah` when the working directory could not be written, and it lost the rows of a result file it could not write. See *Output settings*.
- `[umbrella]` **Docs: the sidebar shows at most two levels under each caption**: the pages, and the sections of the page being read. The subsections below them (the key names on *Configuration File*, the descriptor, cutoff and subcommand names on *Descriptors*, *Cutoffs* and *Command-Line Interface*, the members on the API pages) are on the page only. Under *Nested Fitting* the sidebar lists the pages, not their sections.
- `[umbrella]` **Docs: code lines of up to 80 characters are shown whole on windows 1024 px and wider**, also in a list inside a list; on narrower windows code lines wrap instead of scrolling sideways.

### Deprecated
- `[core]` The configuration keys `MPIWPCKG`, `MBLOCK` and `NBLOCK` of the removed MPI training build. Files that contain them still load and the values are ignored; a newly written potential never contains them. A process that reads such a file writes one warning per key to standard error (`WARNING: Deprecated key MBLOCK is ignored: …`), once per key and process however many files or reads contain it (the rule for every ignored key, `EMBF0` and `RHOFORM` without a DM_REAM block included), whatever `VERBOSE` is; `pair_style tadah` in LAMMPS writes it as a LAMMPS warning on rank 0, in `log.lammps` and on the screen, each time `pair_coeff` loads such a potential (see *Changed*).

### Removed
- `[core][mlip]` C++ API without callers: the per-task API of `Logger` (`setTaskLogLevel`, `setTaskLogFile`, `logTask`), `Scheduler::schedule()`, `Task::serialize()` and `Task::deserialize()`.
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` **BREAKING — task files are removed: the option `--task` (`-t`) of `tadah` and of its commands, and the key `TASK`.** A task file ran several commands from one file: each `TASK <command>` line opened a section of that command's keys, the lines above the first `TASK` line applied to every section, and `tadah --task f` ran the sections one after the other. `--task` is an unknown option of the command line (`The following arguments were not expected: f --task`), and a `TASK` line in a configuration file given with `-c`, in a file it includes with `CONFIG` or in a potential is an unknown key at its line (`Invalid key TASK on line 2 of run.train (closest key: ...)`). Tadah! 1.5.0 split a `-c` file at its `TASK` lines too and used only its first section: the key lines above the first `TASK` line or, in a file that began with a `TASK` line, the key lines of that section. The other sections were dropped without a word, and a `TASK` line naming no command was refused when key lines followed it. It stored the `TASK` line of a file a configuration included as a key, which `tadah train` then wrote into its potential. Conversion: each `TASK` section becomes a command of its own, the commands run one after the other (a shell script): the lines of a section of a command that takes `-c` (`TASK train`, `TASK hpo`, `TASK refit`, `TASK analysis check_bounds`, ...) go into a configuration file given with `-c` (`tadah train -c train.cfg`), but for the options that `-c` needs, which stay on the command line (`--hpotarget` of `tadah hpo`, `--range` and `--type` of `tadah analysis bfunc`, `--outfile` of `tadah analysis dataset_stats`: *Needs* in `tadah <command> -h`), and those of a command without `-c` (`TASK predict`, `TASK data ...`, `TASK properties pairwise`, `TASK analysis descriptor`, `TASK analysis cutoff`) become its options on the command line (`tadah predict -p pot.tadah -d db.tadah -A`); the lines above the first `TASK` line go into each command that takes them, and settings several configuration files share go into a file each includes with `CONFIG`. A potential with a `TASK` line is refused when it loads; deleting the line converts it and changes nothing in the model. The C++ API loses `MLIPUserConfigParser` (the MLIP module's changelog). The *Quick Start* page has no *Task File* section, and its rules of a configuration file are in *Config File*. Tests: CORE `test_blueprint_commands.cpp`, MLIP `test_command_inputs.cpp`, `Subcommand: tadah and its commands have no option --task`.
- `[core][models][mlip][hpo][lammps]` **BREAKING — uncertainty output is removed: `tadah predict --error`, `tadah properties pairwise --error` and `tadah train --uncertainty` are refused.** The errors of Tadah! 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 were not standard deviations, and no calibrated estimate replaces them. Each command refuses the option before it reads or writes anything, with that reason, which `tadah explain predict.error`, `tadah explain properties.pairwise.error` and `tadah explain UNCERTAINTY` give too. Without the option the commands run as before: the predictions and the fit are unaffected. In Tadah! 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 `--error` wrote beside every predicted value an error computed from `SIGMA`, not a standard deviation (negative ones among them), and with `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` or `SWEIGHT` other than 1, or a structure's `eweight`, `fweight` or `sweight` in the predicted data other than 1, it multiplied the predicted energies, forces and stresses by the square root of that value; `--uncertainty` wrote `weights.tadah`, each coefficient beside a diagonal entry of `SIGMA`. `UNCERTAINTY`, a line of every potential that `tadah train` of 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 wrote (`true` or `false`), `ERROR`, the key of `--error` (`tadah train` 1.5.0 wrote the `ERROR` line of a training configuration into its potential, where nothing read it), and `WCOV` and `WNOISE`, which no release wrote, are retired keys: a potential with them loads with one notice (a LAMMPS warning in `pair_style tadah`) and predicts what it predicts without them; a fit (`tadah train`, `tadah hpo` with the potential as `-c`) refuses them, naming each line as converted by deleting it, with the reason, until the line is deleted; `refit --edit` keeps them as they are. `SIGMA` stays deprecated: ignored, with one notice. C++ API: the weight covariance of `LinearRegressor`, `M_BLR` and `M_KRR`, the `predict` overloads with errors and `T_MDMT_diag` are removed (Tadah.CORE, Tadah.MODELS and Tadah.MLIP changelogs). See *Training* and *Quick Start*. Tests: `test_tadah_cli_exit_status.cpp` (`[uncertainty]`), MLIP `test_uncertainty_output_retired.cpp`, CORE `test_uncertainty_keys.cpp`, MODELS `test_oalgo3_fit.cpp`, Tadah.LAMMPS `test_uncertainty_keys_load.cpp`.
- `[core][mlip]` **BREAKING — `tadah data balance --rescale`, `--threshold`, `--force` and `--stress`**: the rescaling replaced the priorities of a dataset by inverse magnitudes, discontinuous at its threshold, and the other options served it only (`rescale` and `threshold` stay options of `tadah analysis bfunc` and `tadah data dedup`). They are retired options of the command: a command line that gives one is refused with the reason. `tadah explain data.balance.rescale` gives the reason. `tadah data balance` shifts energies by a reference energy per element, writes the priorities as they are and, with `--merge`, writes several input files to one.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **The HPO `examples/` directory**: its worked examples, their shared data and scripts, and the reference suite `run_reference_suite.sh` with its reference fingerprints (Tadah.HPO's changelog). The *Worked Examples* page and the README no longer point to it. The data files of the 1.5.0 HPO examples, `examples/common/data/eos_bcc.tadahdb` and `elastic.tadahdb`, store VASP's pressure-signed stress and the energy without entropy, and `elastic.tadahdb` the transposed cells of its 12 sheared structures. Copies of both with the free energy, the Cauchy stress (positive under tension) and the cells as rows, on the same +3.69807734 eV/atom energy reference, are in HPO's `tests/data/ta/common/data`; its `README.md` says what they store and gives their conversion from the VASP OUTCARs (`tadah data convert`, then `tadah data balance --eshift -3.69807734`).
- `[hpo][core][umbrella]` **The experimental `STRATEGY TYPE BASINS` / `BASINSV2` and the `checkpoint.tadah` resume.** Both strategies shipped in 1.3.0-beta.1 and 1.5.0 without user documentation (the HPO examples README called them experimental), and they were the only ones that wrote or read checkpoints: the resume of long sweeps listed among the 1.3.0-beta.1 highlights, and the checkpoint resume the 1.5.0 *Nested Fitting* page described, only ever worked through them, and are withdrawn with them. The stagnation `RESTART` is not affected. A configuration that uses BASINS now fails: `STRATEGY TYPE` accepts `PLAIN`, `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE`; the BASINS knobs, the per-stage `FTOL_LADDER` / `BUDGET_*` keys and the pre-release keywords whose hints pointed at them are unknown keys, and no error message names BASINS (`LOCAL` still points at `INNER`). HPO runs are not resumable: every run starts fresh, and `best_pot.tadah` holds the incumbent at all times. Docs: *Configuration File* no longer describes checkpoint detection or resume, and *Potential archives* says what a stopped run leaves behind. CORE keeps its `Checkpoint` library; only comments that named the removed strategies changed.
- `[cmake-tools][core][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **The legacy MPI training build (`TADAH_BUILD_MPI`, the `tadah_mpi` executable)**, documented as not functional. Configuring any Tadah module with `-DTADAH_BUILD_MPI=ON` stops with an error that points to `TADAH_HPO_MPI`, the MPI-parallel HPO build, which is unchanged; `OFF` is accepted, so older presets still configure. The option, its MPI block and its summary line are gone from `CMakeLists.txt` and `cmake/presets/tadah-linux-defaults.cmake`, and `main.cpp` and the CLI no longer initialise MPI or broadcast for it.
- `[hpo]` `PC_LAMMPS --ncpu`: a line that contains it is rejected at startup with the removal hint and the line number (`Error on line N: --ncpu was removed in 1.6.0 (legacy MPI build); parallelism over PC_LAMMPS scripts is THREADS / PC_ISOLATION. …`); the potential header no longer prints `ncpu=`.
- `[hpo]` The unused header `tadah/hpo/lammps_scripts.h`, which needed a `cmrc` library no build provides and was still installed.
- `[umbrella]` The unmaintained dataset scripts `scripts/create_database.py` (a pymatgen converter that read only the first ionic step of each `vasprun.xml`; it negated the VASP stress, which gives the model's sign, as `tadah data convert` now does), `scripts/build_tadah_db_from_lammps.bash` (it divided every stress by an undefined volume and misread triclinic dumps), `scripts/genearate_lammps_db.lj` and `scripts/old2newDB.bash`. Convert DFT data with `tadah data convert`.
- `[mlip]` `StructureTransformation::alignAxes`, a stub that did nothing.
- `[mlip]` `FastCellFrac` and `FastEnergyEvaluator` (no callers; `FastCellFrac` missed images), and `NNFinder::calc_naive`, `calc_binned`, `deduplicate` and `num_shifts`.

### Fixed
- `[models]` **A fit refuses coefficients that are not finite**, naming the `OALGO` line. Under `OALGO 3` at `LAMBDA 0`, Tadah! 1.5.0 gave coefficients that were not finite whenever the solve kept a singular value whose square underflows to 0 in double precision, that is for any `M` below the ratio of that singular value to the largest (`M` 0 or less, or `M` 1e-300 for a ratio of 1e-200), and `tadah train` wrote `WEIGHTS -inf inf` into a potential no command could read. When such a singular value is the cause, the message names it and says that a `LAMBDA` above 0 keeps its coefficient finite and that an `M` of at least its ratio to the largest leaves it out (the default `M` does for a ratio of at most 1e-8); otherwise it names none. Tests: `OALGO 3: a kept singular direction that gives coefficients that are not finite is refused`, `OALGO 3: coefficients that are not finite for another reason are refused without naming a singular direction`.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah train` with `AUDIT error` returns exit status 1 to the program that runs it**: the refusal ended the process from inside the library (`std::exit`). Test: `Subcommand: train with AUDIT error refuses a FAIL finding with exit status 1, in the process`.
- `[hpo][mlip]` **`tadah hpo` warns about the fixed critical parameters of the searched descriptors alone, under `AUDIT warn` and `error`, each once.** A preview of the audit ran before every run and `--check` whatever `AUDIT` said, read the target file raw (`OPTIMIZER` as a key `IZER`) and warned about a search of `FIXWEIGHT` alone; under `AUDIT warn` each warning came twice. Test: `Subcommand: hpo --check gives the critical-parameter warnings of AUDIT warn once, and none for a search of FIXWEIGHT alone`.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah predict -A`, `tadah analysis suggest_bounds` and `tadah data print` leave the format of standard output as they found it**: a later kernel settings line in the same process read `Kern_RBF | sigma: 1.000000e+00 | gamma: 1.000000e+00`, or `Kern_RBF | sigma: 1.000000 | gamma: 1.000000` after `tadah data print`. Tests: `Subcommand: tadah predict -A leaves the format of standard output as it found it`, `Subcommand: tadah data print leaves the format of standard output as it found it`.
- `[hpo]` **`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` is refused at startup under `M_KRR` implementation 2, naming implementation 1 and not `OALGO 4`, which implementation 2 refuses**: the analytical gradient differentiates the least-squares fit of the training rows (`M_BLR`, `M_KRR` implementation 1), not the solve of implementation 2, (K + `LAMBDA` sigma^2 I) w = t over its basis vectors; `HYBRID` takes finite differences for every parameter of such a setup. Tadah! 1.5.0 stopped such a run at its first gradient, or, with `OALGO 4`, ran it on the gradient of the least-squares fit of the training rows, a fit the run did not make. Test: `startup validation: GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL is refused under M_KRR implementation 2`.
- `[core]` **The example of `tadah analysis cutoff` runs** (`tadah analysis cutoff -o cutoff.txt -r 0 3.14 100 --type Cut_Cos --derivative`); it gave `-t "CutCos"`, `-t` being `--task` (`--range excludes --task`) and `CutCos` no cutoff. Test: `Subcommand: the examples of analysis cutoff run`.
- `[mlip]` **An `M_KRR` implementation other than 1 and 2 is refused naming the `MODEL` line and both implementations** (`MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 3: M_KRR has implementation 1, ...; 3 is neither.`); it said `This KRR implementation does exist: 3`.
- `[models]` **The evidence approximation (`LAMBDA -1`) refuses targets that make alpha or beta not finite and says when it stops at its iteration limit.** Targets that are all zero gave a potential whose weights were all nan; the fit is refused, naming the iteration, the values and why. A fit that stopped after 41 iterations without converging gave no WARNING; a WARNING names the limit, the last relative changes of alpha and beta and the tolerance 5e-12 (an iteration that converges on its 41st step gets none). The evidence approximation writes nothing on standard output: its iterations and how it ended are one INFO message of the log, printed at `VERBOSE 2`. It printed its table of iterations and `Number of steps for convergence` or `Max number of steps reached` on standard output whenever `VERBOSE` was not 0.
- `[mlip]` **C++ API: `M_BLR` and `M_KRR` fit precomputed descriptors with the priorities and error scales of the data, and `predict(StructureDB&)` returns the values of the fit's structures.** `train(StDescriptorsDB&, StructureDB)` fitted the rows unweighted (a structure of priority 0 included) and now gives the `WEIGHTS` of `train(StructureDB, DescriptorsCalc)`; `predict(StructureDB&)` returned the values times the row factors sqrt(weight x `EWEIGHT`/...) and read past the data rows, and now returns the values `predict(Context, StructureDB, DescriptorsCalc)` gives for the structures of the last fit, refusing other data (another number of structures, or structures whose atoms or cells differ from the fit's) and a model without a fit of its own. The fit of precomputed descriptors refuses `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2`, which it fitted as implementation 1.
- `[models]` The API reference of the `DM_mEAD` descriptors (`DM_mRLR`, `DM_mSQRT`, `DM_mBlip`, `DM_mBlipEnv`) gives their Gaussian density as exp(-eta (r_ij - r_s)^2) f_c(r_ij), as the code computes it; it gave exp(eta (r_ij - r_s)). Its configuration examples, and those of `DM_mJoin` and `D2_mJoin`, give their grids with `LIN` and `GEOM` and train. They gave grids such as `-2 7 0.1 10`, which is four values and no generator, and were refused.
- `[mlip]` **The pre-flight audit does not warn about a fixed `CGRIDMB` whose every centre is 0.** Such a basis is largest at the origin and falls off with distance, so its width (`SGRIDMB`), not its centre, decides whether it reaches the neighbour shells; a fixed width and the cutoff are still audited. The audit warned that the centre could miss the neighbour distances of the training data.
- `[hpo]` **A bump centre beyond the low bound of an `OPTIM RCUT2B` or `OPTIM RCUTMB` box is warned about at startup** with a smooth cutoff (`CGRID2B bump 1 centre 4 lies beyond the OPTIM RCUT2B low bound 3.5: a candidate whose RCUT2B is below the centre keeps only the bump's inner shoulder (pathological) ...`). The check read the cutoff of the training configuration only, so a search that tried smaller cutoffs was not warned about.
- `[models]` A single `DM_Poly` descriptor whose channel function reads `CEMBFUNC` and `SEMBFUNC` (`DM_PS_RLR`, `DM_PS_Blip` and their `GPS`, `OW` and `GOW` forms; the `SQRT` forms read `SEMBFUNC`) trains with them, and its line is checked and its grids expanded as any line's (the BREAKING entry on `RCTYPE`, `RCUT` and list values above). `tadah train` removed both keys from the configuration: the `RLR` and `Blip` forms stopped with `Key not found in the Context object: SEMBFUNC`, and the `SQRT` forms trained with every `SEMBFUNC` value 1, which a `SQRT` configuration without `SEMBFUNC` still does. A `DM_mJoin` line gives a descriptor only its share of each list key: the `SQRT` forms take no `SEMBFUNC` there (every value 1, as in 1.5.0), and the `RLR` and `Blip` forms, which cannot run without `CEMBFUNC` and `SEMBFUNC` of their own, are refused, naming them (`TYPEMB DM_PS_RLR (descriptor 2 of 2) reads CEMBFUNC and SEMBFUNC whole and cannot be joined: ...`); in 1.5.0 they stopped with `Key not found`.
- `[models]` `D2_Blip`, `D2_BP` and `D2_Blip5` list their grids under `CGRID2B` and `SGRID2B` in the log of `tadah train -v 2`; they labelled them `CGRIDMB` and `SGRIDMB`, the keys of the many-body grids.
- `[hpo]` **The `TADAH:GRID` messages count the start point apart from the grid points**, and the banner names `1 dimension` for one `OPTIM` dimension. The first `INIT` point (the training configuration's point unless an `INIT` block draws others), evaluated first, is no grid point: `TADAH:GRID finished: the INIT point and all 11 grid points evaluated`, `TADAH:GRID stopped: MAXEVAL reached after 5 of 28 evaluations (the INIT point and 27 grid points)`. They said `1 dimensions.` and `all 12 grid points evaluated` for a grid of 11 points.
- `[hpo]` **The `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` banner of `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` says what the start draw uses**: `INIT_MODE=JITTER AUTO (uniform bound; sigma=proposal sigma per dim, not calibrated: tolerance 0.05 unused)` (the initial V under `CORANA ON`). It said `sigma=AUTO per dim, tol=0.05`, a calibration that `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` does not run.
- `[hpo]` **With `PC_LAMMPS`, the refusal of a gradient-based optimiser names the optimisers that take no gradient and run as written**, among them `DLIB:GFS`, `ASA`, `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` and `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA`, "or DLIB:BFGS, the one gradient-based optimiser it accepts", and says that an NLopt MLSL or AUGLAG algorithm is accepted with a derivative-free `INNER` block. It named `NLOPT GN_*/LN_*` and `TADAH RANDOM/GRID/ANNEAL` only, and gave a wrong reason for the `DLIB:BFGS` exception.
- `[hpo]` **`tadah hpo` removes `best_pot.tadah.lock` when the run ends**, also when a signal or an error ends it, and a lock file it cannot remove is a WARNING in the journal; an empty `best_pot.tadah.lock` stayed in the run directory after every run. The file exists while the run publishes its best potential.
- `[hpo]` **The `best_pot.tadah` header gives the search space in real bounds**, the box of each `OPTIM` line, as the journal does; it gave the optimiser's internal box, the unit box `[0, 1]` under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`. **The header and the journal write a numeric `PARAM` value as it reads**: `GRID_POINTS=11` and `STEP_FRAC=0.15`, where they wrote `1.1000e+01` and `1.5000e-01`.
- `[hpo]` **`DLIB:GFS` refuses a negative `FTOL_ABS` at startup** (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser: DLIB:GFS: FTOL_ABS is the accuracy of dlib's solver and must be >= 0 (got -0.001)`). In 1.5.0 it passed startup, and the search failed inside dlib once the training data had been read (`Failing expression was 0 <= eps`).
- `[hpo]` **A `PRESTAGE` exploration whose optimiser reads `SEED` and that has no seed of its own takes the `STRATEGY`-level `SEED`**, so a run with `SEED` in its `OPTIMIZER` or `STRATEGY` block repeats the optimisers of its exploration and its refinement. The exploration's own seeds come first: the `EXPLORE` block's `SEED` and, for NLopt, its `INIT` block's `SEED`. The refinement draws other random numbers from the same `SEED`, the journal names the exploration's seed (`EXPLORE SEED      : 7 (the STRATEGY-level SEED)`), and a `DLIB:GFS` refinement, which reads no `SEED`, says that it seeds the exploration. Starting points that an `INIT` or `RESTART` block draws repeat only with that block's `SEED`. In 1.5.0 such an exploration was not seeded (NLopt's time seed, or a random device for `TADAH:RANDOM` and the annealers), so the run did not repeat although the journal named a `SEED`.
- `[hpo]` **An NLopt search seeded only by its `INIT` block's `SEED` draws new random numbers in each `RESTART` round and in each `MULTISTART` start**: NLopt takes the `INIT` block's `SEED` when its block has none, and a round (`+ r·S`) and a start (`+ i`) now derive it as they derive `SEED`, so such a run evaluates the points of the run with that `SEED` in its `STRATEGY` block. Every round and start took round 0's seed: a `RESTART` round of `GN_CRS2_LM` drew round 0's population again, every call after its start point answered from the run's record, and `GD_STOGO_RAND` or `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` replayed round 0. The journal names the derived seed (`RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED 6, INIT SEED 8`; `MULTISTART start 2/2 -- running inner optimiser (INIT seed=8)`). Round 0 and the first start are unchanged. See *Configuration File* (*RESTART decorator*).
- `[core][mlip]` **`tadah predict` rescales the data it predicts by the potential's `LSCALE`**, as the docs said. In Tadah! 1.5.0 `tadah predict` had the blueprint default `LSCALE 1.0`, which the merge of the potential and the run's keys put over the potential's, so the data was never rescaled unless `--lscale` was given; the `LSCALE` of `tadah predict` now has no default, `--lscale` replaces the potential's and `--no-lscale` cancels it. With Tadah! 1.5.0 the *Quick Start* configuration trained with `LSCALE 1.1` predicted -144.6 eV/atom for the first structure of `db.predict`, where `--lscale 1.1` gave -8.040. Test: `Subcommand: predict applies the LSCALE of the potential`.
- `[lammps][umbrella]` **A second `pair_coeff * * <file>` in a LAMMPS script loads its file.** `pair_style tadah` read a potential file only when it held no potential, and LAMMPS keeps the pair style when `pair_style tadah` is given again, so a script that switched potentials (a second `pair_coeff`, with or without `pair_style tadah` before it) went on with the first one without a message, under the notices of the new file (a 250-atom Ta cell: -2011.5331 eV with a D2_Knot5 + DM_REAM potential and then with the *Quick Start* potential, whose energy is -2011.4549 eV); a two-species file after a one-species one was refused (`Symbol not in potential file: Nb`). Since 1.0.0. Now every `pair_coeff` with a file loads it, replacing the potential the pair style held, and logs its own notices and fast-path line. `pair_coeff * * EXTERN` adopts a driver's potential only (the one staged for the thread, or one given with `PairTadah::load()`) and is refused, naming what the pair style holds, after a `pair_coeff` with a file or after the driver has cleared the staged slot; `tadah hpo` stages its candidate for every `PC_LAMMPS` script with an `EXTERN` line and is not affected, and a `pair_coeff * * <file>` line of such a script now loads that file (it ran the candidate when no `clear` came between them). A `pair_coeff` that `pair_style tadah` refuses (a file that does not load, an element the potential does not have, a number of elements other than the number of atom types, no element or no file, a refused `EXTERN` line) leaves the pair style without a potential, and `run` then stops (`pair_style tadah has no potential: ...`): a program that drives LAMMPS as a library and goes on after the error ran the potential held before, or died with SIGSEGV. A line LAMMPS refuses before it calls the pair style (`pair_coeff 1 1 ...`) leaves the potential as it was. With it, three defects of a pair style given another potential object are fixed: the per-atom descriptor arrays of the second object stayed empty (SIGSEGV in the many-body kernels, `compute_2b_mb_half` and, for a non-linear model, `compute_2b_mb_full`), the fast path reused the tables of the element an earlier `EXTERN` line had mapped, and a `NULL` type kept the element of the previous mapping. See Tadah.LAMMPS's changelog. *Quick Start* (Tadah! and LAMMPS) and *Nested Fitting Configuration* (`PC_LAMMPS`) say how `pair_coeff` lines combine. Tests: `test_pair_coeff_reload.cpp` (in-process LAMMPS, `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`), Tadah.LAMMPS `test_lammps_tadah_reinit.cpp`.
- `[mlip]` **`TaskFactory::createTasksFromInput` refuses a task name that is not a blueprint task** (C++ API: an `ITaskInput` a program builds; `tadah` passes blueprint names only) with a `std::invalid_argument` naming it, the task it matches but for letter case and the task names (`unknown task 'DATA.SPLIT': task names are case-sensitive, did you mean 'DATA.split'? The tasks: ...`), before any configuration file is read. It died with SIGSEGV, after reading any configuration file.
- `[hpo]` **`DLIB:BFGS` does not evaluate the point it stands on again, and ends a search that no step can move.** At an optimum on a bound, dlib's step held to the box cannot move the point; its line search evaluated the point again, up to 100 times while the loss came back above its first value by rounding (a warm-started `PC_LAMMPS` relaxation), and BFGS took its gradient there again. That line search is not run now (`DLIB:BFGS: the step from RCUT2B = 6 cannot move the point within the OPTIM box: ...`, DIAGNOSTICS `Line searches not run`): under `FTOL_ABS` the search ends `DLIB:BFGS: FTOL_ABS reached`. Under `PARAM tol_gradinf`, which reads the whole gradient, or `FTOL_ABS 0`, such a search ran to `MAXEVAL`, and without `MAXEVAL` never ended; it ends `DLIB:BFGS: no step within the OPTIM box`, a new status, when no step BFGS can take from the point moves it -- a coordinate held at its bound, a step below the rounding of the point, as next to a jump in the loss -- naming the coordinates, or when the line search from the farthest such step comes back to the point. An iteration that asks for no evaluation checks for a signal as an evaluation does.
- `[hpo]` **`DLIB:BFGS` ends a search that an iteration without an evaluation took back to a point where it stood** (`DLIB:BFGS: back at an earlier point`, a new status): such an iteration had every point it asked for answered from the run's record and came back to a point where the search had stood, with the loss it had there. On a plateau of the loss's rounding under `FTOL_ABS 0`, or with a coordinate held at a bound under `PARAM tol_gradinf`, such a search went round points it had, with the record without an evaluation and without end (14 of 3000 searches of a fuzz over random 1-D and 2-D losses), before it evaluated each lap until `MAXEVAL`. dlib's own stop test comes first, and an NLopt search answered from the record still ends on NLopt's `MAXEVAL`, which counts calls. See *Configuration File*, *Points a search asks for again*.
- `[hpo]` **`CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT`, `DOGLEG` and `SUBSPACE_DOGLEG` are refused at the line that closes their block**, with their reason and the optimisers to use instead: `Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT is refused: tadah hpo does not run Ceres' least-squares solver (...): it minimises a sum of squared residuals with their derivatives, and tadah hpo gives an optimiser one scalar loss, over which these methods degenerate. The recommended replacements are CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT, Ceres' line-search solver of a scalar loss that tadah hpo runs, and the bound-constrained gradient searches NLOPT:LD_LBFGS and DLIB:BFGS`. The run stopped with `HPOEngine error: unknown optimizer CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT (not registered in optimizer_caps)`, no line and no alternatives: the deferral message of its own was never reached. They are entries of the caps registry's refusal table, as the refused NLopt algorithms.
- `[hpo]` **An optimiser key inside a `NOISE` block is refused at its line**: `Error on line 7: unknown key 'LIB' inside NOISE block (only AUTO and CALIBRATION are valid)`. 1.5.0 applied it to the main search, as if it stood directly inside `OPTIMIZER`. Move such a line out of the `NOISE` block. See *Optimizer*.
- `[hpo]` **An unknown `LIB:ALGO` is an error at the line that closes its block**, naming what is wrong: ``Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: unknown optimiser NLOPT:LD_FOO: LIB NLOPT has no ALGO LD_FOO; `tadah explain ALGO` lists the optimisers`` (or `there is no LIB FOO (LIB is ASA, CERES, DLIB, NLOPT or TADAH)`), in every phase. It was `unknown optimizer NLOPT:LD_FOO (not registered in optimizer_caps)`, with no line.
- `[hpo]` **The journal line of a failed `PC_LAMMPS` task names every variable at fault**, whether reading it raised a LAMMPS error or its value has no number, in one order: the line's `--varloss` variables, then the `--hotvar`, then the `--failvar` variables, each group in the line's order (`PC_LAMMPS task failed (rb.in, occurrence 1): reading --varloss E after the script: its value 'abc' is not a number; reading --failvar F1 after the script: ERROR on proc 0: Variable F1: Divide by 0 in variable formula ...; reading --failvar F2 after the script: its value 'xyz' is not a number`), alike under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` and `PROCESS`. Only the first was named, so a script with several bad variables was put right one run at a time. See *Configuration File* (*Reading the variables back*).
- `[umbrella]` **`tadah` links without `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`**, the default, and that of the defaults preset the *Installation* page recommends (`cmake/presets/tadah-linux-defaults.cmake`). `main()` called HPO's `PC_ISOLATION` worker entry, `tadah::hpo::pc_worker_main`, in every build, and only a build with NFP has HPO: linking `tadah` failed (`undefined reference to tadah::hpo::pc_worker_main(int, char**)`), so a build without NFP had no `tadah`, and its `tadah_version_matches_blueprint`, `tadah_cli_example_*` and `tadah_quickstart_reproduces_pot` failed. Without NFP, `--tadah-pc-worker` is an unknown argument.
- `[hpo]` **A build with `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES=OFF` compiles**: HPO fetches and links Eigen whatever the option (it did only with Ceres, and 13 sources of `tadah.hpo` stopped at `Eigen/Dense: No such file or directory`). Such a build refuses `LIB CERES` and `REFIT_MODE compact`, as documented.
