- `[umbrella]` **The CMake-script tests declare no more than the CMake the tests accept** (3.16, above; HPO's 3.24 makes this a matter of builds without NFP). `tadah_header_check_*` need CMake 3.19 (`string(JSON)`) and are Skipped, with a line that says so, on CMake 3.16-3.18 (they failed there: on 3.16-3.17 at the 3.18 they declared, on 3.18-3.23 at `cmake --fresh`, `ctest --test-dir` or `string(JSON)`, which that CMake does not have); they, `tadah_removed_option_*`, `tadah_catch_skip_marker` and `tadah_version_reader_*` no longer use `cmake --fresh` (CMake 3.24) or `ctest --test-dir` (3.20). The scripts of `tadah_removed_option_*`, `tadah_git_branch_*`, `tadah_doxygen_input_*` and `tadah_catch_discovery_script`, `_module` and `tadah_catch_skip_marker` declared 3.18, and stopped on 3.16-3.17 (`CMake 3.18 or higher is required`): they declare 3.15, or 3.16 where they run cmake-tools' test discovery or CTest's skip; `tadah_catch_discovery_script` reads a name's bytes with `file(READ ... HEX)` (it used `string(HEX)`, 3.18) and `tadah_catch_skip_marker` runs `ctest` without `--no-tests=error` (3.17; its status checks name every case). `tadah_cmake_minimum_features` checks this statically, against its table of commands, options and test properties.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` **A `tadah` built without `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP` refuses `tadah hpo` naming the build option**, and its help says so. `tadah hpo` stops before it runs with `ERROR: tadah hpo needs the Nested Fitting Procedure, which this tadah was built without; reconfigure with -DTADAH_ENABLE_NFP=ON (see Installation)` (exit 1); the hpo line of `tadah -h` and `tadah hpo -h` end with that sentence, and `tadah explain hpo` prints it after the command's text. Such a build has no HPO engine: `tadah hpo` said `ERROR: EngineFactory::create: No engine found for HPO` (`EngineFactory::registerNotBuilt`). v1.1.1 and v1.3.0-beta.1 built without NFP said the same; in 1.5.0 no `tadah` linked without NFP (above).
- `[core][models]` **An assignment to a column of an `fd_type` or `aeds_type2` writes into the container** (C++ API): `fd[k] = v` made the column own a copy, so `fd(i, k)` kept its old values (`D2_Base::calc_fd_approx` wrote nothing into its `fd_type`); a vector of another size is refused, and moving a column out copies it (it left the column null). A copy of an `aed_type` (the copy constructor, an assignment into an owner, a move out of a view, `aed_type(const double*, size, stride)`) holds its `size()` values one after another, read through the source's stride: a copy of a strided view such as `Matrix::row(r)`, or the copy `const Matrix::row(r)` returns, copied `size() * stride` doubles from the row's first value, past the end of the matrix (a heap-buffer-overflow), and kept the stride; an owner assigned a view of its own values read its freed buffer. No Tadah module copies a strided view.
- `[models][hpo]` **A fit LAPACK cannot make says why and what to do.** `OALGO 2` (DGELS) and `OALGO 4` (Cholesky) refuse, before LAPACK runs, a design matrix with fewer non-zero data rows than weights (with `LAMBDA > 0`, fewer than intercept columns; a data row that is zero in every column adds nothing to the rank (to rounding: no value above 1e-10 of the largest of its column): a row of priority 0, such as the energy of a structure of energy priority 0, a row the descriptors make zero, such as the y and z force rows of a dimer along x or, with `BIAS false`, the energy of an atom without neighbours, and with `FIXINDEX` a row whose values are all in the pinned columns; the message counts the others and names these causes: `2 non-zero data rows of 3 (a data row that is zero in every column adds nothing to the rank: a row of priority 0, or a row the descriptors make zero, such as the y and z force rows of a linear molecule along x) for 3 weights`), and stop where LAPACK cannot complete the factorisation (an exactly zero diagonal element of R under `OALGO 2`, such as a column that is zero for every structure; a pivot that is not positive under `OALGO 4`), naming the data rows and weights, the column where LAPACK stopped, and the remedies that apply to the fit's `LAMBDA` (with `LAMBDA 0`: `OALGO 1` or `3`, `LAMBDA > 0`, more data; with `LAMBDA > 0`: `OALGO 1`, for `OALGO 4` also `OALGO 2`, `OALGO 3` only without `BIAS` intercepts, which it refuses with `LAMBDA != 0`, a larger `LAMBDA`, more data); with `FIXINDEX` (M_BLR and M_KRR) they count the free weights and the pinned ones and name the weight of a column (`these 4 free weights (FIXINDEX pins 1 of the 5 weights)`, `its column 1 (weight 2)`); they said `Error in DGELS: info = 3` and `Error in DPOTRF: tadah::core::Matrix is not positive definite; info = 3`, and `OALGO 4` fitted fewer data rows than weights whenever the rounded Phi^T Phi could be factored (2 energies for the 3 weights of `D2_BP 3 3` gave weights of 1e5). A column that depends on others without being exactly a combination of them in floating point is still fitted by both options, with very large weights. A design matrix or target with NaN or an infinity is refused under every `OALGO`, naming its row and column, instead of stopping in LAPACK (`Error in DGELSD: info = -4`) or, for an infinite energy, force or stress target, fitting it. The `OALGO` key text says so. `LinearRegressor::train` and `train_with_factor` refuse a layout that does not describe the design matrix (`std::logic_error`, C++ API) before they write the `LAMBDA` ridge rows into it, which they wrote at a row that wraps for a matrix with fewer rows than columns. In `tadah hpo` a candidate whose target is not finite (an intercept pinned by `FIXINDEX` so large that its share of an energy overflows) is an evaluation that threw, journalled with that message (see Tadah.HPO); it was fitted with NaN weights and its loss clamped to `FAIL_SCORE`.
- `[models]` **A malformed setfl file is refused, naming the file, the line and the field** (`SETFL` of D2_EAM and DM_EAM, `EAM` of `tadah refit`): a table of fewer than 3 points (the spline read past its end), a `drho`, `dr` or `rcut` that is not a positive finite number, a header line with a word too many or too few (a fifth word on the `Z mass a lattice` line shifted every table by one value without an error), a value that is not a finite decimal number, a short table, and a table whose last line holds the start of the next (LAMMPS drops it). The file is read as LAMMPS `pair_style eam/alloy` reads it, so `#` comments and blank lines after line 3 are skipped (they were refused). The other defects failed as `EAMSetflIO: malformed setfl header in: <file>` or `truncated tabulated data in: <file>`. The setfl files LAMMPS ships read as before. The `SETFL` key text and the `EAM` row of *Refitting tabulated EAM potentials* say how the file is read.
- `[models]` **A setfl file whose element record is split after its mass is refused, as LAMMPS refuses it** (`SETFL` of D2_EAM and DM_EAM, `EAM` of `tadah refit`). LAMMPS' `pair eam/alloy` reads `Z mass a lattice` up to the end of the line that holds the mass and the F(rho) table from the next line, so `73 180.95` followed by `3.304 bcc` fails there (`Not a valid floating-point number: 'bcc'`), while tadah joined lines until it had the 4 words and read the file. tadah now names the line and its words (`EAMSetflIO: setfl file 'Ta.eam.alloy', line 6: the element record is 'Z mass a lattice', it has 2 words: LAMMPS (pair eam/alloy) reads the record to the end of the line of its mass, and the F(rho) table from the next line`); a record split before its mass reads, as in LAMMPS. The `SETFL` key text says where the record's words go.
- `[hpo]` **The `PC_LAMMPS` analytical gradient chain adds the direct θ term** (`compute_sims_grad_dWchain`): a descriptor parameter changes a relaxed LAMMPS observable through the energy and the forces at the same weights, as well as through the refitted weights. The chain had only the part through the weights, as the `PC_OBSERVABLE` chain had before 1.6.0. Without the direct term in its input, the chain sends every parameter to finite differences. The path is not reachable, as `PC_LAMMPS` refuses every `GRAD_STRATEGY`, and its provider does not compute that term yet, so it takes finite differences for every parameter.
- `[hpo]` **The box block of the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint Hessian comes from the pair virial** (`LammpsHessianAssembler`): its diagonal was the energy's second difference over a strain step of 1e-5, whose round-off moved it by up to 1.5e-4 relative and the lattice-constant gradient with it (1.1e-4, against its test's 1e-4 tolerance, with an HPO-trained Ta potential); the gradient's box part now matches finite differences to 4e-7. On an orthogonal box the shear rows are exactly 0. Not reachable either; what remains of that gradient's error comes from pair_tadah's adjoint snapshot `dF/dW`, which is not the derivative of the forces.
- `[hpo]` **Every `tadah hpo` evaluation trains a model built from its own candidate.** A worker thread used to keep the model of its first evaluation and rewrite only the searched keys for the next ones, so what the model, its kernel and its design matrix read when they were built kept the first evaluation's value. `OPTIM EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT` (every model) and `OPTIM MPARAMS` (M_KRR kernels) were not applied after a worker's first evaluation, while every potential recorded the candidate's values; `OALGO 3` potentials gained one `SIGMA` per evaluation, and `tadah predict --error` on such a `best_pot.tadah` corrupted the heap; an M_KRR `-c` potential with `WEIGHTS` failed the first evaluation; and a second run in one process (a program's second `hpo_run()`) trained the first run's model (its `LAMBDA`, `FWEIGHT`, `FORCE`, ...). **A search that used `OPTIM EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT`, `SWEIGHT` or `MPARAMS` needs a new run:** its reported optimum was never applied (`OPTIM ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA` and `SSIGMA` search the error scales in place of the retired weights). The environment variable `TADAH_HPO_FORCE_REBUILD` is gone. Still carried over from a process's earlier runs: the evaluation counter (a second run's `ITERATION` numbers and `DUMP` names follow the first run's; *Nested Fitting*, *Two runs in one process*); the worker pool has each run's own `THREADS` (the entry on worker pools below), and neither an earlier run's `WARM` seed values nor its journal tallies carry over (the *Fixed* entry on a second run in one process).
- `[hpo]` **Every `tadah hpo` run starts its `PC_LAMMPS --hotvar` values from their `<initial>` value or its `WARM` seed** (and not from an earlier run's `WARM` seed: the *Fixed* entry on a second run in one process). The values each evaluation hands to the next were kept for the process, so the second run in one process started each hot script from the value the first run's last evaluation converged to, and did not reproduce the same run made alone (HPO's `tests/data/ta/target_files/dlib_bfgs.hpo` at `MAXEVAL 3`: `TOTAL_LOSS/N` 1.043183703093665e-01 against 1.043183703095260e-01); its files are now those of the run alone but for the evaluation numbers. See *Nested Fitting*, *Two runs in one process*.
- `[models]` **`tadah train -c` with an M_KRR potential trains it** (exit 1, "This object is already trained!", when the potential had `WEIGHTS`). An M_KRR potential is written without `DBFILE`; add it to train from one.
- `[core][models]` **An integer read from a number-typed value is exact.** Where tadah reads an integer from a value the blueprint types as a number, such as the `Kern_Polynomial` degree (`MPARAMS (1)`), `2.0` or `2e0` is 2 and `2.7` or `2abc` is refused, naming the key and the value; it read the integer prefix (`MPARAMS 2.7` trained the degree 2). Keys typed as integers (`SBASIS`, `OALGO`, `VERBOSE`, `--uniform`, ...) take plain digits only and refuse `2.0` or `1e3` when the configuration or command line is read, as before. For C++ callers, every integer read of a `Context` (`get<int>`, `get<size_t>`, integer containers) takes an exact in-range integer in any spelling and refuses a fraction, trailing text or an out-of-range value (Tadah.CORE). `Kern_RBF` refuses sigma 0.
- `[core]` **A number read from a configuration value is the whole token.** A floating read (`get<double>`, `get<float>`, the n-th value, a container) takes a decimal number with a finite value (`1`, `.5`, `5.`, `-1e-3`) and refuses trailing text (`1.5abc`), hex numbers (`0x10`), `inf`, `nan` and `1e400`, naming the key and the value (`Conversion failed for key=MPARAMS, value="1.5abc": a finite decimal number is required`); a bool read takes `true` or `false` whole. They read the numeric prefix: `1.5abc` was 1.5. A configuration line is checked against the key's type when it is read, but a list key's check took a comma (or a `+`) between values, which the parser does not split at, and a line of 2048 characters or more skips the check: on a line of any length, a list value such as `FIXWEIGHT 0.1,0.2`, `MPARAMS 1.0,2.0` or `SGRID2B 1.0,2.0 3.0` was read as the first number of each comma token (0.1, 1.0, 1.0) without an error; such a line is now refused when the file is read, naming the separator (see *A comma or a `+` between two values* below). Write a list with blanks between the values. `1.5abc` or `0x10` reached the reads only from programs that put values in a `Context` and from lines of 2048 characters or more.
- `[core][mlip]` **BREAKING — a comma or a `+` between two values of a configuration line is refused when the file is read, naming the line, the key and the separator.** A line is split into values at blanks, but the check of a list key's values took a comma or a `+` between two of them (and a line of 2048 characters or more, commas): `FIXWEIGHT 0.1,0.2`, `MPARAMS 1.0+2.0`, `CGRID2B 1.0,2.0,3.0` or `FIXINDEX 13+14` passed it and were one value per word, which a later read refused (1.5.0 read `0.1,0.2` as 0.1 without an error). They are refused now with the line to write: `Invalid value for key FIXWEIGHT on line 3 of config.train: '0.1,0.2' separates values with ',', which a configuration line is not split at (values are separated by blanks: FIXWEIGHT 0.1 0.2)`, and for the values of a command-line option (`Invalid key-value pair: percent -> 80,20 ('80,20' separates values with ',', which is not a value separator: give them as separate words, 80 20)`). A word with a comma or a `+` stays one value where the key takes a path or an option string (`DBFILE a,b.tadah`, `--set 'CGRID2B(3)=2.1,2.4'`), and the index lists (`FIXINDEX`, `--index`) keep their commas (`FIXINDEX 13,14` is `FIXINDEX 13 14`). A key whose values are names (`ATOM`, `--atompair`, `RCTYPE2B`, `RCTYPEMB`, `TYPE2B`, `TYPEMB`, `MODEL`, `WATOM`) refuses a comma or a `+` in a name the same way (`ATOM Ta,Nb`: write `ATOM Ta Nb`); it kept `Ta,Nb` as one name, and `tadah train` stopped later at an error that did not name the separator (`Mismatch between elements in datasets and ATOM ...`, `Type "Cut_Cos,Cut_Tanh" ... is not registered`). A `+` is an exponent's sign only in a number (`1e+5`): `TYPE2B D2_Blip 4 4 Fe+Fe` is refused naming the `+` (it got `Invalid schema for key: TYPE2B`). *Configuration File* says how values are separated.
- `[core]` **`aed_type` iterates through its stride, and `Matrix::row()`/`col()` return views without relying on NRVO** (C++ API): a range-for over `Matrix::row(r)` visited the column-major neighbours of its first value (no module iterates a strided `aed_type`); without NRVO the non-const `row()` and `col()` returned copies, whose writes were lost. The adopting `aed_type(arr, size, stride, true)` refuses a null buffer of values.
- `[core]` **A configuration line is checked in time linear in its length, a long one by the rules of a short one.** The check of a line against its key's types took time exponential in the number of values to refuse one (seconds at 12 to 24 values, a hang beyond: a `{DOUBLE}` list 2 blanks apart that ends in a typo, a `TYPE2B` line with an odd element list), and a line of 2048 characters or more was checked by other rules (a line of numbers was taken for any key: `.5` or `inf` in a list of numbers, `1.5` in a list of integers; a list of words was split at commas too, so `x1,2` was refused for its part `2`). Lines of any length are now checked word by word, with the same rules, in a time that grows with the line only.
- `[models]` **`DC_Selector::reinit` lays the selector out as one built from the new context** (C++ API; no tadah command calls it): the intercept columns of its `BIAS` and `ATOM`, the two-body block after them, the many-body block after it, the density array, and the descriptor and density sizes. It moved no block and updated the sizes only when a descriptor reported a new size, so the many-body values were written onto the two-body block (or past the descriptor after a shrink), a `D2_mJoin` moved onto the intercept columns with `BIAS true`, a `DM_mJoin` child that grew left the selector's sizes short and wrote past them, and a `DM_Blip` or `DM_EAD` that changed size wrote F'(rho) over its density or past the density array.
- `[core]` `Context::get(key, container)` refuses a key with more values than the container holds (it wrote past a `std::vector`).
- `[core][umbrella]` **A configuration key's example is shown with the key once.** A refused value listed the key's examples with the key in front, and 64 examples of 36 keys began with the key already (`BETA   BETA 0.0001`); the examples are now values of their key, one per line for an example of several lines, and both the refusal and the *Configuration File* page (*Supported KEYS*) show each line as `<KEY>   <value>` (the page showed an example as written: most without their key, such as SBASIS's `10`, those 64 with it, such as BETA's `BETA 0.0001`). The CLI pages and `tadah explain` show the values (`dbfile`'s examples no longer repeat `dbfile`). The `tadah refit` blocks INIT, EXPLORE, REFINE, FUSE and OPTIMIZER have no examples; their long descriptions show the layout.
- `[umbrella]` **The *Command Line Interface* page keeps the layout of a long description.** `generate_cli.py` stripped every line of a key's long description, so nested lines stood at the indentation of the text (`--rescale`'s bullets, and the `tadah refit` block layouts of INIT, EXPLORE, REFINE, FUSE and OPTIMIZER, which also showed RST's `for example::`). Their lines now keep their indentation relative to the least indented one, and a paragraph's closing `::`, RST's marker of the literal block after it, is shown as RST shows it (`for example:`), as the *Configuration File* page does, and as `tadah explain` prints it (below).
- `[models]` `dm_mEAD.hpp` and `dm_poly.hpp` (`descriptors/dm`) have include guards, and `[hpo]` `hpo_host_impl.hpp` includes `hpo_host.h`: included before their `.h`, the first two defined everything twice and the third did not compile. Code that includes the `.h` files, as Tadah does, was not affected.
- `[core]` **A `CONFIG` line with an inline comment** (`CONFIG config.train   # nested config`) includes the file it names; `tadah train -c` and every other reader stopped with `Failed to open config file: config.train   `, the blanks before the `#` kept in the path. Comments on the other keys were not affected.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah data print`, `tadah analysis descriptor` and `tadah analysis bfunc` take `-i` strictly**, as `data sample`, `balance` and `write` do: 1-based indices checked against the structures of the DBFILE (for `bfunc`, the basis functions of the descriptor) before anything is read, an index outside them, `0`, a descending range and a malformed entry refused naming it (`tadah data print: index 9 (in '4-9') is outside 1-5 (the dataset has 5 structures)`), and several values one list (`-i 1 3`). `analysis descriptor -i` refused every index above 1 since 2025-04 (every release from 1.0.0), the help's own example `--index 1,3,5` included, and crashed (`-i 5-2`, `-i 1 3`) or hung (`-i 0`); `data print -i` dropped indices past the end (`-i 4-9` printed two structures, exit 0) and did not finish in two minutes on `-i 1-3000000`; `analysis bfunc -i` dropped an index past the last function with a WARNING and printed a function from outside the grid for `-i 0`. Tests: the `Subcommand: data print -i`, `analysis descriptor -i` and `analysis bfunc -i` cases (test_tadah), `test_dataset_tools.cpp` (MLIP).
- `[mlip]` **The cell and the stress of a dataset structure are three lines of three numbers each, for every reader** (training, `count`, the `-i` selections, the element scan). A structure with another layout, or a file that ends inside the blocks, is refused, naming the file, the structure and the line (`cell line 1, '3.3 0 0 0 3.3 0 0 0 3.3', has 9 fields: ...`). Reading took 18 numbers across any lines while counting skipped six lines: a cell written on one line was read, but counted with two atoms fewer, and a fourth number on a stress line was dropped. Every dataset the docs, the examples and the tests ship has this layout.
- `[mlip]` **A structure without atoms counts as a structure for `-i`** (its blank line straight after the stress lines, as `tadah data print -d` and `data sample` read it): `StructureDB::count` stopped at the first such structure, so `tadah data write -i` refused every later index with a false count (`index 3 is outside 1-1 (the dataset has 1 structures)`). `data print -i` and `analysis descriptor -i`, which take their bound from it too, read the selected structures in one pass and parse only those, so a structure elsewhere in the file whose values are not valid does not stop them (its cell and stress lines are still checked for their layout, three lines of three numbers, as every reader of a dataset checks them). Tests: `Subcommand: data print, analysis descriptor and data write -i count a structure without atoms` and `... -i read only the selected structures` (test_tadah), `test_dataset_tools.cpp` (MLIP).
- `[mlip]` **`tadah data convert` names the file and the line in every error of the VASP OUTCAR, `vasprun.xml` and CASTEP `.castep` readers, and refuses a value it cannot read**: an unknown element, an unreadable `NIONS`, ions-per-type, lattice, position, force, energy, temperature or iteration line, a `vasprun.xml` varray with a row too many or too few, XML that is not well-formed. They printed a message on standard error that named no file and stored the step with what they had read (a partial cell, an atom less, a zero force); a 4-row `vasprun.xml` stress crashed the reader. An unreadable stress or force row (`*****`) of `vasprun.xml` gives that priority 0 with a WARNING, as an unreadable OUTCAR `in kB` line and `.castep` row do (it was stored as 0 with priority 1), and a `vasprun.xml` calculation without forces has force priority 0. A file that ends inside a step keeps the steps before it, with one WARNING naming the file and the step (and the block it ends inside, if any): an OUTCAR or `.castep` file that ends during a step's SCF or between its blocks (a run stopped, `WARNING: VASP OUTCAR OUTCAR: the file ends inside ionic step 15 (line 266439), which was skipped (it has no 'free  energy   TOTEN' line); 14 structure(s) read.`) dropped that step without a word. This now holds for `vasprun.xml` too, which was read as nothing when VASP had not finished writing it; one that ends after a complete calculation, without its closing tag, is read whole and says that nothing was skipped. An OUTCAR `EKIN_LAT` temperature of 10000 K or more (`(temperature10147.82 K)`) is read: such a step was stored together with the steps after it (a 6580 K MD run of 5000 steps of 54 atoms gave 4939 structures, one of 3348 atoms); re-convert such runs. The *Dataset Format* section says so.
- `[mlip]` **Every structure reader names its source when an element is unknown**: a POSCAR or CASTEP `.cell` file (every error of those two readers names the file now), an AFLOW entry (and the species), a NOMAD entry or a Materials Project material (and the site); the AFLOW, NOMAD and MP readers let the periodic table's `Unknown symbol: <s>` escape, naming no entry or material, and the POSCAR and `.cell` messages named the line or the label but not the file. A refused POSCAR, `.cell`, NOMAD or MP read keeps the previous structure, and a reused reader no longer appends the atoms of each read to the last ones. A POSCAR atom count is digits after at most one `+` (`+2` is read, as before): a count such as `2abc`, `2.5` or `-2`, of which only the leading digits or the sign were read, is refused, naming the file and the line. Tests: MLIP `test_vasp_readers_values.cpp`, `test_castep_readers_values.cpp`, `test_aflow_reader_typed.cpp`, `test_online_reader_fetch_failure.cpp`.
- `[core]` `fd_type`'s `+`, `-` and `*` with a vector throw `std::invalid_argument` for operands of another size, and `==` and `isApprox` are false for them; they read past the smaller buffer, and two fd_types of zeros of different sizes compared equal. The CORE test `fd_type out-of-range checks`, which asserted nothing, is replaced by tests that do.
- `[core]` A default-constructed or moved-from `fd_type` has three empty columns: `fd[k]` and `fd(k)` dereferenced a null pointer on it (a segmentation fault).
- `[core][models][mlip][hpo]` **`FIXINDEX` is read strictly, by one reader everywhere** (CORE `parse_fixindex`): `tadah train` (M_BLR and M_KRR implementation 1), the weight provisioning and the `BIAS` composition check (with the fit's weight count, before the fit), and `tadah hpo` (`OPTIM FIXWEIGHT`, startup). **M_KRR implementation 2 (`MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2`), whose solve fits every weight at once, refuses `FIXINDEX` and `FIXWEIGHT`** (`MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2 cannot pin weights: ...`; `tadah train`, `tadah hpo` at startup): it read neither, so `tadah train` exited 0 with the pins ignored and copied the keys into the potential, whatever their values. `FIXINDEX` given as separate values (`FIXINDEX 13 14`, which the key's format allows, and which the *Mixed mode* example of *Nested Fitting Configuration* uses) is the comma list `13,14`; it was dropped as one malformed segment, and `tadah train` exited 1 with `FIXWEIGHT and FIXINDEX differ in size: 2 and 0`, `tadah hpo` with `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT (14): weight 14 is not pinned by FIXINDEX (13 14)` or `FIXINDEX names 0 weights`. A descending range (`2-1`, which named no weight and gave the same messages), index 0, a weight past the model's and a weight named twice are refused, each with one message naming the text (`FIXINDEX: index '2-1' is a descending range`, `FIXINDEX: index 7 is outside 1-4 (the model has 4 weights)`, `FIXINDEX 13,13,14 names weight 13 twice; ...`), and `FIXINDEX` must name as many weights as `FIXWEIGHT` has values, counted before a range is expanded. **BREAKING — a configuration with a repeated `FIXINDEX` weight is now refused**: the repeat was removed with a warning on standard error, so `FIXINDEX 1,1` with one `FIXWEIGHT` value trained and `13,13,14` with two ran an hpo search; both are now refused naming the repeated weight (`FIXINDEX 1,1 names weight 1 twice; ...`), as is any list that names no more distinct weights than `FIXWEIGHT` has values. CORE's lenient `parse_indices` is removed. Docs: the `FIXINDEX` key, *Nested Fitting Configuration* (*Pinning and searching linear coefficients*). Tests: CORE `test_fixindex.cpp`, MODELS `test_fixed_weights.cpp`, MLIP `test_weight_provisioning.cpp` and `test_descriptors_calc_bias.cpp`, HPO `test_optim_key_rules.cpp` and `test_startup_validation.cpp`, `test_tadah` (`Subcommand: train reads FIXINDEX given as separate values as the comma list`, `Subcommand: train refuses FIXINDEX with M_KRR implementation 2`).
- `[core]` `parse_one_based_indices(values, total, who, counted)`, the strict 1-based index list behind `parse_dataset_indices`, names what the indices count in its bound message; the `INDEX` help texts state the strict rules, with their own text for `data print`, `analysis descriptor` and `analysis bfunc` (basis functions, not dataset indices).
- `[models]` **A descriptor whose list keys are not set is refused, naming them**: `tadah train` (and `tadah hpo`, `tadah analysis bfunc`, a `D2_mJoin`/`DM_mJoin` model) with `TYPE2B D2_BP 5 5 * *` and no `CGRID2B`/`SGRID2B` ended in a segmentation fault, and so did every descriptor with list keys (`CGRID*`, `SGRID*`, `AMPGRIDMB`, `CEMBFUNC`, `SEMBFUNC`, `KDEG*`); it now stops with `TYPE2B D2_BP takes 5 CGRID2B values and 5 SGRID2B values, but CGRID2B and SGRID2B are not set: TYPE2B D2_BP 5 5 * *`. A list key with too few values names the line too (on a joined line, which descriptor and what is left of the key), instead of `Not enough data for key 'CGRID2B' in context. Expected 2 but got 3. Token: D2_BP`. A `TYPE2B`/`TYPEMB` line without its parameters (a crash), with a SIZE parameter that is not an integer (`stoi`), and a joined model with fewer `RCTYPE*`/`RCUT*` values than descriptors (`std::bad_alloc`) are refused with a message that names them. Tests: `test_descriptor_list_keys.cpp` (MODELS) and `Subcommand: train refuses a TYPE2B descriptor without its grid keys, naming them` (test_tadah).
- `[hpo]` **The `HEALTH_LOG` monitor of `tadah hpo` checks the weights of every evaluation.** It read a design matrix that `tadah hpo` never builds and reported nothing, its NaN/Inf warning included. It now warns, on standard output, when a weight of the evaluation's model is NaN or infinite, when every weight of a block (the bias, the two-body or the many-body descriptor's columns; one block for a non-linear basis function or kernel) is exactly zero, when fewer than 5 % of a block's weights exceed 1e-15 in magnitude (a block that holds a NaN or infinite weight is checked for neither: the NaN/Inf warning describes it), and when the model has not as many weights as its design matrix has columns (no block check then). Concurrent evaluations (`ANNEAL_PT`/`ANNEAL_PA`, `DLIB:GFS`) record into it safely. The docs no longer say it reports "exploding blocks", which it never checked.
- `[hpo]` **Every `tadah hpo` run has worker pools of its own and stops them when it ends, also when it fails.** The second run of a process (a program's second `hpo_run()`) ran on the pool of the first run's `THREADS` while its journal reported its own; under `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` a second run with a smaller `THREADS` failed its evaluations (`PcWorkerPool::execute: bad slot`), and under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` it ran a `PC_LAMMPS` script of the same file name and LAMMPS options on the first run's LAMMPS instance, as the first run had read it (a script rewritten between the runs was not read again). A program that called `hpo_run()` again after a run that threw found the first run's `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker processes still running (`pc_worker_pool_init: pool already initialized`). After a run, a later training in the process (a program that trains after `hpo_run()`) ran its training kernels on the run's pool instead of OpenMP. The evaluation counter still carries over from one run to the next (see *Two runs in one process*); an earlier run's `WARM` hot values and the journal's tallies no longer do (the *Fixed* entry on a second run in one process).
- `[hpo]` **A signal that one rank of a multi-rank `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` run (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) receives during the search ends every rank as a signal stop**: every rank reports `SHUTDOWN` and exits with 128 + the signal number; a rank that did not receive it names the rank that did (`SHUTDOWN (SIGTERM on MPI rank 0)`). Such a rank ended with an error, status 1 (`FAILED -- … rank 0 aborted during … calibration`, `… worker failed on another MPI rank …`). A signal or an error on one rank during the `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` calibration hung the job: that rank and the others waited in different collectives, none reached the exit barrier, and the job ran until it was killed from outside; such an error now ends every rank with status 1. `NOISE_CALIBRATION`, which each rank runs on its own before the search, has no such agreement: when only some ranks see a signal or an error there, the job now ends through the exit barrier (the failed rank aborts it after 30 s); before, Open MPI 5 aborted it at once on the mismatched collectives (`MPI_ERR_TRUNCATE`). Docs: *Nested Fitting: configuration* (*Stopping a run*, *Multi-node runs*).
- `[hpo]` In a multi-rank run each rank's startup LAMMPS instance, which reads a `PC_LAMMPS` script's variables, writes the line's `-log` / `-screen` file with the rank's `.r<rank>` suffix (every rank wrote the one file); an MPI build journals a `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` request (`PC_ISOLATION: THREAD (requested; the only mode of the MPI build)`).
- `[umbrella]` *Installation*, step 2 of the Nested Fitting build: a static LAMMPS library built with OpenMP (`BUILD_OMP`, on when the LAMMPS configure finds OpenMP) needs `-DTADAH_ENABLE_OPENMP=ON`, which the defaults preset leaves `OFF`: without it linking `tadah` fails on `omp_get_num_threads`, `GOMP_parallel` and the like. The page did not say so, and its MPI build line (*MPI-Parallel Hyperparameter Optimisation*) had the preset's `OFF`; that section also names the libraries a static LAMMPS with COMPRESS, GRAPHICS or VORONOI needs in `TADAH_LAMMPS_LIBS`, and the launcher to set as `MPIEXEC_EXECUTABLE` when another MPI's `mpiexec` comes first on `PATH` (CMake takes the first one there).
- `[mlip][hpo]` **BREAKING — a potential has no `DBFILE` key line, so the documented retrain fits the dataset once, whichever the model.** (its training record names the datasets; see the training-record entry under *Changed*) No potential that `tadah train`, `tadah hpo` or `tadah refit` writes has a `DBFILE` key line (*Training*, *Retraining from a potential*). An `M_BLR` potential kept the `DBFILE` it was trained on (an `M_KRR` one did not), so `tadah train -c pot.tadah` with the documented `DBFILE` line added listed its dataset twice: every structure was fitted twice, a fixed `LAMBDA` acted at half strength and an `OALGO 3` `SIGMA` was that of twice the data, without a word. An `M_BLR` potential written before keeps its `DBFILE` key line (`tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` do not read it): remove it before adding the `DBFILE` of a retrain. A potential trained with `WDBFILE` lists it in its training record, not as a key line, and a retrain does not take it from the potential (the dataset-keys entry under *Changed*), so the retrain may list any datasets; an `M_BLR` potential with its `DBFILE` lines retrained as it was.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **A training `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice is refused**, naming both entries (the same path, two spellings of it or a link to it): by `tadah train` before any file is read, and by `tadah hpo` at startup. Each entry was a dataset, so the file's structures were fitted twice; `WDBFILE` weights a dataset.
- `[models][mlip]` **`MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2` potentials predict what their fit fitted.** The weights are the `DSIZE` descriptor weights of the basis vectors (with `NORM true` converted to the raw descriptors), the same model, since the kernel is linear (*Regressors*). The potential had the `SBASIS` coefficients of the basis vectors as `WEIGHTS`, which `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` evaluated as descriptor weights, so the energies were wrong (the check of a potential's fit, `require_weights_fit` of the BREAKING `BIAS` entry under *Changed*, refuses such a potential unless `SBASIS` equals `DSIZE`). Retrain such potentials.
- `[mlip][hpo]` **`SBASIS` is checked before the training data is read, for `MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2` too.** A missing `SBASIS`, or one below the intercept vectors plus one (2 with one species or `BIAS false`), is refused naming the model, `M_KRR Kern_Linear 2: SBASIS 1: the fit needs at least 2 basis vectors ...`, by `tadah train` before it reads the datasets and by `tadah hpo` at startup. Implementation 2 draws basis vectors for its solve with every kernel. `tadah hpo` passed `Kern_Linear 2` with `SBASIS 1` or none, and every evaluation threw; `tadah train` read the data first and stopped with `Key not found in the Context object: SBASIS` (every M_KRR fit that draws a basis) or, for a small value, called `Kern_Linear 2` `a non-linear M_KRR model`.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah train` warns when `FIXINDEX` and `FIXWEIGHT` pin every weight, so the fit changes none**: `WARNING: tadah train: FIXINDEX and FIXWEIGHT pin every weight (all 84 of them), so the fit changes none: the WEIGHTS of the potential written are the FIXWEIGHT values. tadah refit and refit --edit write FIXINDEX and FIXWEIGHT for every weight of their potentials, ...: to fit the weights to the DBFILE data, delete both lines from the training configuration (...)`. A potential of `tadah refit` or `refit --edit` (and the `best_pot.tadah` of a `tadah hpo` run that pins every weight) has these lines for every weight, the seed of a `tadah hpo` coefficient search, so its documented retrain (a copy with a `DBFILE` line) exited 0 with the input weights and nothing said that none was fitted. A warning, not a refusal: such a fit can be deliberate. Docs: *Training* (*Retraining from a potential*: delete the two lines from the copy).
- `[mlip][hpo]` **`tadah train -c pot.tadah` without a `DBFILE` line says how to retrain**: `The training configuration has no DBFILE: tadah train fits the datasets it lists. A potential has no DBFILE line (no potential tadah writes one; its training record, the comment block above its key lines, lists the datasets its weights were fitted to in a '# DBFILE' line when there were any: a refit has none, and a potential of an earlier version has no record): to train from one, add a DBFILE line to a copy of it; a potential of tadah refit or refit --edit also pins every weight with its FIXINDEX and FIXWEIGHT lines, the seed of a tadah hpo coefficient search, so delete them from the copy for the fit to change the weights (the Training page of the documentation, Retraining from a potential).` It stopped with `Key not found in the Context object: DBFILE`, now the likely mistake on the documented retrain route (no potential has a `DBFILE` key line). The refusals of `tadah hpo` for a `-c` configuration without a training `DBFILE` end with the same advice where the weights cannot be determined (no `FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT`, pins that do not cover every weight, a basis drawn from the training data) and where `ATOM` is missing. With every weight pinned, `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` is still refused for the missing `DBFILE` advising `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID` only: with a `DBFILE` line the pins are refused next.
- `[hpo]` **A validation `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice is refused**, naming the `--validation` file and both entries (the same path, two spellings of it or a link to it), as a training one is: every validation metric averages over the datasets, and the file counted twice. **`--validation` takes one file**: a second value is refused, naming both (the run read the first and did not read the others).
- `[hpo]` The startup refusal of a `-c` configuration whose model does not build ends the model's reason with one full stop (`... Set SBASIS to 2 or more. Every evaluation would fail; ...`; it read `..` after every reason that ends in a full stop).
- `[lammps][umbrella]` **The per-atom energies of `pair_style tadah` with a `DIMER` potential sum to its energy.** `compute pe/atom` gives each atom the sum of its energies in the molecule pairs its molecule forms with every molecule within the centre-of-mass cutoff (Tadah.LAMMPS `compute_dimers`); each pair set it in turn, so an atom kept only the energy of the last pair evaluated (27 N2 molecules with *Tadah!N2*: sum(pe/atom) -0.036475 eV against pe -0.61539 eV), on one rank or many. The energy, the forces and the virials were not affected. Tests: `test_dimer_pe_atom.cpp` (the sum, and each atom's pe/atom in three molecules against its pe/atom in the two molecule pairs it belongs to). *Trained MLIPs* (*N2*) says so.
- `[models]` `OLS::solveWithPseudoinverse` reads Vᵀ with leading dimension min(m, n) (it used n: wrong for a matrix with fewer rows than columns, which the design matrix never is).
- `[cmake-tools][umbrella]` **cmake-tools' version reader takes the `[TADAH]` table of a blueprint whose text has square brackets or semicolons** (a key description `a number in (0, 1]`): `tadah_read_version_from_blueprint` reads the file as text and the table from its header line to the next line starting with `[`, as before. It split the file into a CMake list of lines, in which an unbalanced bracket joined the lines up to the next bracket and a semicolon split a line, so such text before `[TADAH]` stopped the configure step with `'version = "..."' not found in [TADAH]`. Test: `tadah_version_reader_brackets`.
- `[models][mlip]` **`FIXINDEX` with `LAMBDA > 0`: the ridge penalises every free weight but the model's free intercepts.** `M_BLR` left the first N free columns unpenalised after removing the pinned ones, so pinning an intercept penalised another intercept and left a descriptor weight unpenalised, with one species too (`FIXINDEX 1` freed the first descriptor weight; on Ta-Nb data of one composition with the Nb constant pinned and `LAMBDA 1e6`, the Ta constant came out 0.03 eV, and -7.646 eV now, as `M_KRR` gives with every `OALGO`); `M_KRR` kept the pinned columns as zero columns, so a pinned intercept made `OALGO 2` and `OALGO 4` fail (`Error in DGELS: info = 1`, `DPOTRF: ... not positive definite`). Both now fit the free columns, leaving out of the penalty exactly the function's unpinned intercepts. `BF_Polynomial2` with `BIAS true` on several species leaves every species' constant (k,k) out of the penalty (it left column 0 alone). Tests: MODELS `test_intercept_ridge.cpp`, `test_intercept_columns.cpp`, `test_fixed_weights.cpp`.
- `[mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **A potential used as a training configuration retrains from its own file, also a non-linear `M_KRR` one** (`tadah train -c pot.tadah` with a `DBFILE`, a `tadah hpo -c` seed): the model to be fitted is built from the configuration without what a fit writes (`WEIGHTS`, `BASIS`, `NMEAN`, `NSTDEV`) and without the deprecated `SIGMA`. An `OALGO 3` potential's `SIGMA` was kept by a retrain with another `OALGO`, and an `OALGO 3` retrain appended its own to it (`predict --error` read the old one), as in 1.5.0 and earlier. Tests: `test_tadah_cli_exit_status.cpp` (`[retrain]`), MLIP `test_descriptors_calc_bias.cpp`, HPO `test_multispecies_bias.cpp`.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo`'s journal describes the starting points and the `RESTART` block as configured**: the run policy prints the `RESTART` block's `METHOD` chain (or `(default -- CONFIG only)`), `K_TOTAL`, `SEED` and `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`, of which it printed only the chain; `K_TOTAL` and `SEED` print under those names (they read `INIT_K` and `INIT_SEED`, the retired keys); `Starting point:` counts the INIT chain's points by origin (`3 points from the INIT chain (3 LHS)`, where it read `user-supplied (3 seeds)`), an `EXPLORE` without `INIT` reads `(the OUTER INIT points, shared)`, and the `PRESTAGE.EXPLORE.INNER init strategy:` line is gone (an `INNER` block takes no `INIT` block). *Configuration File* (density gates: the `INIT` chains whose seeds are checked are `OUTER`'s and `PRESTAGE`'s).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A `RESTART` block's `WARM_ON_MISMATCH` governs the `WARM` seeds of its `METHOD` chain** (`error`: a startup error, `skip`: the seed dropped), at startup and at each restart; it was accepted and ignored, and the `INIT` block's applied to that chain. A restart whose chain produces no point warns that the round starts from the previous round's points. *Configuration File* (`INIT` and `RESTART` keys, diagnostic markers).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`ASA:ASA`'s stop line names the stop that ended the run**: `shutdown requested (SIGINT)`, `RESTART stagnation stop (...)` or `an evaluation raised an exception (<message>)`, where it read `exception raised during evaluation`, and its count includes the evaluation that raised the stagnation stop or an error; a `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or signal stop inside the reanneal tangents reads `IMMEDIATE_EXIT requested during the reanneal tangents (asa_out: INVALID_COST_FUNCTION_DERIV)`, where it gave the code of a failed evaluation there. *ASA* (*Journal output*, `Reanneal_Parameters`, *Troubleshooting*).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo`'s new-best breakdown in `hpo_run.log` names an indexed `PC_ERMSE` or `PC_FRMSE` per dataset** (`top: ERMSE[2]=1.78e-03(56.3%)  ERMSE[1]=1.38e-03(43.7%)`), each shown like every part of the line as its contribution to the run's `loss=` (`L(ERMSE)[i]` / datasets / loss terms); it labelled the average of the dataset losses `ERMSE_global` / `FRMSE_global`, the name of the global form. *Configuration File* (per-dataset targets).
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` **`tadah explain` prints a long description as text**, as the *Command Line Interface* page shows it: a paragraph that closes with RST's `::` ends with `:` (`Written inside a stage block, for example:`) and its literal block follows, indented, as in the 1.6.0 descriptions of the `tadah refit` `OPTIMIZER`, `INIT`, `EXPLORE`, `REFINE` and `FUSE` keys. An option that names no command, or no option of its command and no configuration key (below), is an error naming what was not found (`tadah explain: no command 'bogus'` for `bogus.key`, `tadah explain: refit has no option 'nosuchkey', and it is no configuration key`): `tadah explain bogus.key` ended in a segmentation fault, `refit.nosuchkey` printed nothing and exited 0, and so did the help's own example `tadah explain task`, now `tadah explain train`.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah explain <command>.<key>` prints the key's description when the key has no long description**, then its examples: 85 of the 150 configuration keys printed an empty line and their examples, the `tadah refit` numbers among them, whose ranges `tadah refit --help` says `tadah explain refit.<key>` gives.
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` **`tadah explain` reaches every configuration key**: a name that is no command, subcommand or option of its command is looked up among the configuration keys, so `tadah explain SETFL`, `tadah explain TYPE2B` (its examples of several lines each indented) and `tadah explain refit.rhoform` print the key's text. They printed nothing and exited 0, although `tadah refit --help` named `RHOFORM`, `EMBF0`, `NUMERIC`, `RCUT2B`, `RCUTMB` and `AMPGRIDMB` and sent the reader to `tadah explain refit.<key>` for "the full description of any key". Every name is read in any letter case, and a command's own subcommand or option comes first: `refit.hpofileout` is refit's text, `tadah explain data.MERGE` the merge subcommand, not the `MERGE` key (a subcommand was matched in lower case only: `data.MERGE` and `data.SPLIT` printed nothing). `tadah refit --help` writes `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY`, `REFIT_COMPACT_INSERT_TOPK` and `REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE` in full and limits its pointer to the configuration keys: `tadah explain refit.optimizer` describes the keys of an `OPTIMIZER` block (`ALGO`, `LIB`, the stopping criteria) and `tadah explain refit.init` the placements of `INIT`, since the long descriptions of the five stage blocks now hold the block's text before its layout (`tadah explain refit.optimizer` printed the layout only). A name that is none of these is an error: `tadah explain: no command or configuration key 'bogus'`, `tadah explain: refit has no option 'nosuchkey', and it is no configuration key`. *Introduction*, `tadah explain --help`, `tadah refit --help`.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`NLOPT:NLOPT_LD_LBFGS_NOCEDAL` is refused at startup**, in every phase, naming `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS`: NLopt 2.10 names it but does not implement it, so every `tadah hpo` run of it ended `FAILED -- nlopt invalid argument` once its setup was done. *Configuration File* (the `ALGO` row, the `NLOPT` row of *Algorithms by library*, `MULTISTART`), *Optimizer*.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo`'s `DLIB:GFS` takes a `MAXTIME` of any length**: one beyond about 292 years, the nanoseconds dlib counts its run time in, ended the search after its `INIT` points, reported as a spent `MAXEVAL`; dlib now gets at most its own no-limit, 200 years. *Configuration File* (*DLIB:BFGS PARAM keys*, the `DLIB:GFS` paragraph).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **The CERES driver of `tadah hpo` refuses at startup the settings Ceres cannot take**, naming the value: `FTOL_REL` or `XTOL_REL` below 0, `PARAM max_iter` not an integer from 0 to 2147483647, `max_ls_iter` not one from 1. Ceres refused the negative values and `max_ls_iter 0` when the search started, before any evaluation, and the run took that for the search's ending; a fraction was cut to an integer (`max_ls_iter 2.5` ran as 2). *Configuration File* (the CERES `PARAM` keys).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` Docs and the `ALGO` help: `DLIB:BFGS`'s `PARAM tol_gradinf` tests the L2 norm of the gradient (dlib's `length(g)`); they called it the infinity norm (*Configuration File*, *DLIB:BFGS PARAM keys*).
- `[mlip][models][hpo][lammps][core][umbrella]` **Non-linear models train, predict and run in `tadah hpo` and LAMMPS again:** M_KRR with `Kern_RBF`, `Kern_Polynomial`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ` or `Kern_Sigmoid`, and M_BLR with `BF_Polynomial2` (in LAMMPS with `BIAS true` on several species too, now that tadah trains one intercept component per species). Since 1.3.0-beta.1 `tadah train` refused every one ("ColumnLayout: total_cols=3 disagrees with design-matrix width=5"), and so did every `tadah hpo` evaluation (FAILSCORE, no best potential) and `tadah predict --error`: the design matrix's layout check compared the descriptor's columns with the design-matrix width, which only a linear model's equals. The layout is now the function's. Behind that check: the KRR basis is drawn by a fixed, portable draw (e0, then `SBASIS-1` training descriptors, or with `BIAS true` one intercept vector per species, then `SBASIS-N`; `std::mt19937_64`, default seed 5489), so `tadah train` writes the same potential every time and an HPO loss no longer depends on a random basis (it used `std::random_device`); `SBASIS` must be n + 1 to the number of training atoms plus n, n the intercept vectors (1, or N with `BIAS true` on N species; training structures plus n for implementation 2, `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2`; `SBASIS 0` crashed); M_KRR no longer rotates its columns (EKM): in HPO's reused trainer the basis grew by `SBASIS` vectors with every evaluation (the extra ones zero), and a basis vector with K(b, b) <= 0 (an isolated atom under `Kern_Quadratic`) corrupted the heap; the ridge leaves out the columns each function declares as intercepts (with `BIAS true` on N species: the products (k,k) of `BF_Polynomial2`, columns 0 .. N-1 of the dot-product kernels, none for `Kern_RBF`, which `OALGO 3` with `LAMBDA != 0` now trains); a KRR potential whose basis does not fit is refused, naming the keys, by `tadah` when it predicts and by `pair_style tadah` when it loads it (one without `BASIS` predicted E = 0; vectors of another length corrupted memory), and so is, by `pair_style tadah`, a `BF_Polynomial2` potential without D(D+1)/2 `WEIGHTS` for its D descriptor components (the one check of a potential's fit, see the `BIAS` entry); `tadah hpo` checks `FIXINDEX` of a kernel against `SBASIS`, and `SBASIS` against its lower bound n + 1, at startup. See *Regressors*, *Non-linear models*. (`OPTIM MPARAMS`, and for every model `OPTIM EWEIGHT`/`FWEIGHT`/`SWEIGHT`, were not applied after a worker's first evaluation: the entry on `tadah hpo` evaluations that train a model built from their own candidate.)
- `[lammps]` **`pair_style tadah` with `BIAS true` sets the bias component only for the atoms LAMMPS has** (local and ghost), in `compute_2b_mb_full` (every non-linear model) and `compute_2b_mb_half` (a linear model with a many-body descriptor). The loops ran over all `atom->nmax` columns of the descriptor array and read `atom->type` past the atoms, where LAMMPS does not set it, so `run 0` in a second LAMMPS instance of one process, as `tadah hpo` creates for `PC_LAMMPS` evaluations, died with SIGSEGV, and with one species or several a column past the atoms could get a 1 or the loop crash, depending on the memory there. LAMMPS must be rebuilt. Tests: HPO `test_pair_tadah_nonlinear.cpp`, `test_multispecies_bias.cpp` (two species; with `atom->type` past the atoms set to a valid type no column past them is set, a deterministic check).
- `[mlip][core]` **`tadah data sample`:** `--uniform N` started at the second structure, took every 2N-th for odd N, and for some dataset sizes wrote a garbage structure read past the end; `--uniform 0` crashed (SIGFPE). It now keeps every N-th structure from the first, and refuses 0. `--random N` always returned the first N structures; it now draws from the whole dataset, each structure with the same chance, in dataset order. `-i` refused the last structure and did not check index 0; it dropped a malformed entry with an `Error:` line but exited 0, dropped a descending range (`5-3`) without a word, and read the last entry of one `-i` value and the first of the next as one malformed entry. It now parses the indices as one list, strictly against the dataset size, refusing what it cannot use. Exactly one of `--uniform`, `--random` and `--index` is required (`tadah data sample needs one selection: --uniform N (every N-th structure), --random N (N structures at random) or --index <indices>`; none wrote an empty dataset). `tadah data balance -i` and `data write -i` take their indices the same way (balance refused the last structure and wrote out of bounds for index 0; write dropped two `-i` values as one malformed entry).
- `[mlip]` **A `tadah data` command whose output file cannot be written exits 1**, naming the file; it printed an error and exited 0 (`data write` checked only that the file opened, not a full disk). `data split`, `dedup`, `balance` and `write`, which write several files, check them all before writing any (`data write` checks every selected structure too: an index beyond the dataset or a singular cell for VASP or LAMMPS): a second output in a missing directory left the first written, and an `--append` rerun appended it twice.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah predict` no longer re-applies the dataset priorities of a potential** (`WDBFILE`, and the retired `WDBFILE_AUTO` and `EWEIGHT_TEMP`, which an earlier version wrote as key lines; the dataset-keys entry under *Changed*).
- `[mlip][lammps][hpo][umbrella]` **Two atoms at the same position are refused**, naming the structure, the atoms, the position and the image shift: `tadah train`, `predict` and `hpo` (at startup) stop with that message, and `pair_style tadah` stops naming the atom IDs. Every force was nan: `predict` printed nan (exit 0), `train` failed with a LAPACK error naming no structure or, fitting energies only, fitted the structure, `hpo` spent its budget on failed evaluations, and LAMMPS ran on with nan forces. A pair 1e-12 Å apart is accepted. **`tadah analysis dataset_stats`** counts such a pair in r_min (it hid it) and lists every pair closer than `--close-contact <A>`; `suggest_bounds` and `check_bounds` refuse such data, and `check_bounds` stops when the dataset's statistics cannot be computed (it audited against empty statistics and reported every corner safe).
- `[mlip]` **CIF:** a symmetry record gemmi could not read is no longer silently P 1 (the asymmetric unit): a space-group number alone gives the full cell in its standard setting (origin choice 2, hexagonal or rhombohedral axes from the cell angles), with a WARNING when the cell could be in another ITA setting of the group, and only without a symbol or an operator loop that forms a group, and from 1 to 230; H-M symbols written `P2_1/c`, `P2(1)/c`, `P 21/c (No. 14)`, in full (`I 4/m -3 2/m`, as the CIF dictionary asks and FINDSYM writes) or with ICSD's ' S'/' Z' suffix in either case are read, a CIF that gives no cell or not its three lengths is refused (gemmi kept a 1 Å cube; an angle left out is 90°), with or without a loop, `Ccce` gets origin choice 2 like `C c c a` (it got origin choice 1), an unknown or blank value of one tag no longer hides the other tag of its pair (the operator loop, the H-M and Hall symbols, the number; a number that is not an integer refused the CIF even when unused), and a Hall symbol in no tabulated setting expands with its own operators; a symbol that cannot be read is refused, quoting it, when it is used (also when an operator loop that forms no group falls back to it; next to a loop that forms a group it gives a WARNING); a monoclinic group in a setting of gemmi's table, or with the rotations of one, must fit the cell however it is given (the angles between its unique axis and the other axes are 90°; a short symbol or a number means unique axis b, except the B-centred `B 2`, `B m`, `B b`, `B n`, `B 2/m`, `B 2/b` and `B 2/n`), or the CIF is refused, naming the group and the angles, as is a trigonal or hexagonal group on axes the cell does not have, however it is given (`R -3 m:H` or ICSD's `R -3 m H` in a rhombohedral cell gave 36 atoms 0.78 Å apart; translations alone fix no axes); a CIF gemmi cannot parse is refused naming the file; an inconsistent symmetry record warns; a partly occupied site, an occupancy above 1 or one that is not a number is refused, naming the site (a disordered site became coincident atoms). **AFLOW:** entries are fetched from `<aurl>/aflowlib.json` (the old `?format=json` answers 404) and read in the typed form the service uses (every live entry failed); a malformed field is refused, naming it.
- `[mlip][core]` **A failed fetch from the Materials Project, COD, AFLOW or NOMAD names the ID and the service's reason**, cut to 300 characters, e.g. `tadah data print -s cod-1534932` with no network: `CODReader: HTTP 500 for COD ID '1534932': CODDataProvider: cURL error: Could not resolve hostname`. The message holds no request URL, header or API key. The readers gave only the HTTP status and the ID.
- `[mlip][core]` **The label of a CIF or COD structure names the space group its cell is built with** (`tadah data print -s si.cif`: `CIF file: si.cif | Symmetry: F d -3 m:2 | Volume: 160.191478`; `P 1` without a symmetry record), not the CIF's H-M symbol, which left the field empty for a group given by an operator loop, a Hall symbol or a number, and could name another setting or group. Labels written to datasets from CIF and COD structures change. The errors, WARNINGs and label of a COD entry name it (`COD entry 1534932: site Fe1 has occupancy 0.5; ...`), not the CIF data block; its label had an empty field (`COD entry: cod-1534932 |  | Symmetry: ...`). A CIF reader used again no longer accumulates atoms, and a refused CIF leaves its last structure as it was.
- `[mlip]` **A CIF or COD structure with a site of an element Tadah does not know is refused naming the file or COD entry and the site**: deuterium, which COD's neutron-diffraction entries use (`COD entry <id>: site D1 has element 'D', which Tadah does not know: its elements are H to Og.`), or a symbol that is no element (`site Q1: 'Q' is not an element symbol.`). The message was `Unknown symbol: D` (`Unknown symbol: X` for `Q`), naming neither.
- `[hpo]` **`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy`** hands on a0 × the relax scale factor (`cell(0,0)/nx` was one ulp off for a 3×3×3 supercell); the default 4×4×4 is unchanged.
- `[hpo]` **A LAMMPS error while `tadah hpo` reads a `PC_LAMMPS` variable back no longer ends the run.** HPO reads each `--varloss`, `--outvar`, `--hotvar` and `--failvar` variable after the script by evaluating its formula, and that can raise a LAMMPS error: a thermo keyword the `thermo_style` does not compute (`variable Pend equal press` under `thermo_style custom step pe`), a divide by zero for one candidate. Under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` (the only mode of the MPI build) the run died with SIGSEGV; under `PROCESS` the whole script failed, a diagnostic `--outvar` included, with a journal line that did not name the variable; and a `--hotvar` or `--failvar` that could not be read was taken as absent. Now, alike in both modes, an unreadable `--varloss`, `--hotvar` or `--failvar` fails its simulation as a failed run does (`PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): reading --varloss E after the script: <LAMMPS message>`), so does an unreadable `S` of the `PC_FAILPROBE` line (a failed probe: `reading PC_FAILPROBE --outvar S ...`), and an unreadable diagnostic (`--outvar` only) is `NaN` in `outvar.tadah` while the evaluation keeps its loss (`PC_LAMMPS --outvar is NaN (...)`); a variable that reads an unreadable one (`variable Pdiff equal v_Pend-1.0`) gives that one's LAMMPS message. A LAMMPS error inside the script is journalled with its message too (`PC_LAMMPS task failed (...): ERROR: Unknown command: ...`); such evaluations failed with nothing in the journal. The line was `pc-worker task failed (...)`. See *Reading the variables back* in the nested-fitting configuration.
- `[hpo]` **A hot value LAMMPS refuses to inject before a `PC_LAMMPS` script fails that script**, in both isolation modes, journalled with the LAMMPS message (``PC_LAMMPS task failed (cold.in, occurrence 1): injecting `variable hot_a0 equal 3.25` before the script: ERROR: Cannot redefine variable hot_a0 with a different style``): LAMMPS refuses it when the name is already another style, e.g. `--invar hot_a0 3.3` on the cold line. The error was swallowed and the script ran on the value it had, with a feasible loss and nothing in the journal. A cold script is injected the hot values it reads (`${name}` or `v_name` in its text) and no other, so a value it does not read cannot fail it; every hot value went to every cold script that read any. A cold script that reads a hot value its hot script did not produce (that script's LAMMPS instance could not be created) does not run; it ran without the value, or in a cached instance on an earlier evaluation's. A `string`-style hot readback fails the next evaluation's injection, and so that evaluation (the warm-start text said the injected value was silently ignored). See `--hotvar` and the warm-start protocol in the nested-fitting configuration.
- `[hpo]` **A `PC_LAMMPS` variable read back with no number for its value fails its simulation**, as one that cannot be read does, in both isolation modes: a `--varloss`, `--hotvar` or `--failvar` variable (a failvar also when inf or nan) or the `PC_FAILPROBE` `S` that is not defined after the script, has no single value or is text (`PC_LAMMPS task failed (...): reading --varloss F after the script: no value (the variable is not defined, or has no single value)`); a diagnostic `--outvar` is `NaN` with a journal line. A `--varloss` one used to add `FAILSCORE` to the mean of the evaluation's terms, which could fall below `0.999*FAILSCORE` and count as feasible; a hot value was skipped, a failvar counted as no violation, a diagnostic was `NaN` silently. A hot or fail value is a number only when the whole value is one (`3.25abc` was read as 3.25). See *Reading the variables back* in the nested-fitting configuration.
- `[hpo]` **An evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation scores `FAILSCORE` whatever loss its variables are measured with** (`FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`; `ON` grades it, as before) -- a script error, a timeout, a variable that cannot be read back or has no number, a hot value refused or outside its window, a failed `PC_FAILPROBE`, an instance that cannot be created. It scored the mean of its terms, the failed line's loss of its `--failscore` value among them, which reached `0.999*FAILSCORE` only when that loss carried the value past `FAILSCORE` (`L2` with the default `--failscore`): under `L1`, the bounded `TUKEY` or a small `--failscore` a failed evaluation counted as feasible and could become the best, and a run all of whose evaluations failed ended as a completed one. The failed line's terms in `loss.tadah` are still its loss of the `--failscore` value. Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF` the startup warning for a `PC_FAILPROBE` line says that its `S` neither grades nor gates an evaluation and that a probe that fails fails its evaluation (it said that the probe ran "as a plain diagnostic sim"). See `--failscore` and `FAILSCORE_GRADING` in the nested-fitting configuration.
- `[hpo]` **An evaluation with a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is a failed evaluation**: `FAILSCORE` under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`, under `ON` a graded band-B failure measured by the failed fraction of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms. A term that cannot be computed (a relaxed lattice constant outside its `--hotvar a0` window, a divergent relaxation, a value that is not finite) is `FAILSCORE`, and the evaluation scored the mean of it and its other terms, about `FAILSCORE/n` for one failed term of n >= 2: below `0.999*FAILSCORE`, so it counted as usable under either setting -- it could become the best and reach `STOPVAL` -- and under the default `FAILSCORE` the other terms vanished in it, so a gradient search that started where the term fails converged there (`DLIB:BFGS` `FTOL_ABS reached`, `LD_LBFGS` `SUCCESS`, `CERES:LBFGS` `tol_gradinf reached`, status 0, `best_pot.tadah` at 8.988e+307); an evaluation with no usable term (a target whose only term is the observable), or one whose failed terms summed past the largest double, scored `FAILSCORE` already. Such a search now stops at its first gradient under either setting -- under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF` at once (`no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point`), under `ON` when every point of its finite-difference stencil fails with the same failed fraction (`... every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`), and under `ANALYTICAL`, the default with `PC_OBSERVABLE`, at once as well (next entry) --, a run whose every evaluation has a failed term ends without a usable loss (status 1), and the term stays `FAILSCORE` in `loss.tadah` (`NaN` in `outvar.tadah`). Under a `FAILSCORE` that does not absorb the other terms (`1e16` next to terms of about 1e5) such a search used to end with status 0 and a `best_pot.tadah` under either setting, most backends following the gradient of the other terms out of the region (`DLIB:BFGS` `FTOL_ABS reached` with the best 7.7476e+04, `LD_LBFGS` `SUCCESS`); now, graded or not, it stops at its first gradient with status 1 and no `best_pot.tadah`. The journal's band-B count reads `B=n (script or observable failed)`. See *Typed observables (PC_OBSERVABLE)*, *Failure handling — graded FAILSCORE* and *Points without a usable gradient* in the nested-fitting configuration, and the loss in *Background*.
- `[mlip][models]` **`tadah refit` refuses a `REFIT_BASIS` that names no family, a `REFIT_LEVEL` or `REFIT_PLACEMENT` that is none of its values, and `REFIT_CONSTRAIN_F0` under `EMBF0 ZERO`**, before the EAM file is read (`REFIT_LEVEL` and `REFIT_PLACEMENT` in any letter case; `BLIP` is Blip). A typo of the basis fitted Blip while it warned of the experimental Gaussian basis, another level the `balanced` preset, another placement the window one, and `REFIT_CONSTRAIN_F0` had no effect. See *Refitting*.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah refit --edit` and `--retarget` write the file an explicit `-o` / `--hpofileout` names, also `pot.tadah` or `refit.hpotarget`**: a name equal to a refit's default counted as unset, and the edited or retargeted default names were written instead, without a word. See *Refitting*.
- `[hpo]` **`refit_opt.log`'s curve-mode note no longer advises `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` for a Blip basis**, which refuses it; it names `REFINE`'s `MAXEVAL` and `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` there.
- `[mlip]` **`tadah train` refuses `FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT` with `NORM true`**, as `tadah hpo` did: it fitted the pins to the normalised descriptors, and the potential did not hold the `FIXWEIGHT` values it recorded (a pinned Nb intercept of -7.6 was written as -7.649). See *The bias term* in *Descriptors*.
- `[hpo]` **A second `tadah hpo` run in one process (a program that calls `hpo_run()` twice) has a journal of its own run, and starts its `--hotvar` values without an earlier run's `WARM` seed.** The second run's `hpo_run.log` counted both runs (`Total evaluations`, `Unusable losses` and the verdict), timed the first run's evaluations, reported the first run's best in RESULT (and a `best_pot.tadah` it had not written) and, with a worse best, had no BEST line; its hot-variable `rejecting` warnings were rate-limited by the process's count. A run without `WARM` after one that `WARM`-started from `# HOT` lines started its hot scripts from that seed. Only the evaluation numbers carry over now, as documented. See *Nested Fitting*, *Two runs in one process*.
- `[hpo]` **A failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation or `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is never reported as a NaN/Inf clamping.** Under `LOSS L2` with the default `FAILSCORE` (the largest double), the substituted values of a failed simulation, or two failed observable terms, overflowed the mean of the loss terms, and the evaluation was journalled `evaluation produced NaN/Inf -- loss clamped to FAIL_SCORE` with bound hints and counted under `NaN/Inf clampings` (under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` with such a `FAILSCORE`, also under `Graded failures`). The failed task or term now decides: a failed evaluation with its own journal line, and no NaN warning. See `FAILSCORE` in the nested-fitting configuration.
- `[hpo]` **At a graded failure a gradient search is given no analytical gradient** (`FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`). A graded failure's loss is set by its failure band's measure, not by the loss terms, and the analytical gradient there is that of the terms that did not fail. It was kept, so a search that started where a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term fails reported convergence inside the failure region under the default `GRAD_STRATEGY`, which is `ANALYTICAL` with `PC_OBSERVABLE` (a trained `OALGO 4` model whose observable fails over the whole box: `DLIB:BFGS` `FTOL_ABS reached` after 4 evaluations, `LD_LBFGS` `algorithm converged` after 12, `CERES:LBFGS` leaving the box; `HYBRID` the same). Now `ANALYTICAL` stops there at once (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point (the loss at RCUT2B = 5.2 is 9.99375e+15: a graded failure, whose grading the analytical gradient does not see; GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID takes finite differences there)`, one evaluation, status 1), and `HYBRID` differences every parameter there, which gives the graded slope or a flat failure region. A gate-rejected candidate under `ON` gets this cause too and no longer costs an evaluation for its analytical pass, so an NLopt search through gate rejects can end earlier on NLopt's own failure after a rejected candidate. See *Points without a usable gradient* and *Failure handling — graded FAILSCORE* in the nested-fitting configuration.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **The journal says why a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term failed**: `PC_OBSERVABLE term failed (cohesive_energy[1], eval 2, occurrence 1): the relaxed lattice parameter a0 = 3.3532 is outside its --hotvar window [3, 3.35]`, a `WARNING` at the first, second, fourth, ... failed term of the run, as for a failed `PC_LAMMPS` task; a failed term left only `FAILSCORE` in `loss.tadah` and `NaN` in `outvar.tadah`. Docs: *Configuration File* (`PC_OBSERVABLE`).
- `[hpo]` **A `PC_LAMMPS` task that fails before its script runs says why in the journal**, in both isolation modes: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (...): creating the LAMMPS instance: <LAMMPS message>` or `... rendering the script to a temporary file: <reason>`. Such runs failed every evaluation with nothing in the journal.
- `[hpo]` **Every row of `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah` and `stats.tadah` (and their `best_*` twins) has its header's columns**: a cell an evaluation did not compute -- the `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` columns of one stopped by `--skip-above`/`--skip-rel`, the metric columns of a candidate a feasibility gate rejects before training, the columns of one ended by an error -- is `nan` (`loss.tadah`, `stats.tadah`) or `NaN` (`outvar.tadah`). Such rows held only the cells the evaluation had, so `TOTAL_LOSS/N` fell under another column, and a gate reject showed its metric averages as `0`.
- `[core]` **`tadah data merge -d a.tadah b.tadah -o merged.tadah`** (its help example) exited 106 (`Exactly 1 option from [-t,--task,-d,--dbfile] is required`): `-d` was registered twice. It merges.
- `[mlip]` **The dataset scanners read blank lines as the dataset reader does:** `StructureDB::find_unique_elements` (what `tadah train` and `tadah hpo` read first) read a separator line of spaces or a CRLF blank line as an atom line; `count` and `add(file, first, N)` read a separator of spaces as an atom line and stopped at an extra blank line.
- `[hpo][mlip]` **HPO builds without a system Boost** (the SOBOL init strategy's headers are vendored), and a test checks that every Boost header HPO includes, and every one those include, comes from the vendored tree.
- `[mlip]` **Readers and data tools:**
  - the first frame of every CASTEP `.md`/`.geom` file was dropped;
  - a `.castep` (L)BFGS revert paired the reverted geometry with the trial SCF's results; each SCF now pairs with the last geometry printed before it, line- and quadratic-minimisation steps included;
  - a fixed-cell `.castep` geometry optimisation with the finite basis correction lost its iteration 0, and an MD run with the correction its step 0;
  - a fixed-occupancy (insulator) `.castep` run stored energy 0, or an earlier structure's energy and temperature; a structure without an energy of its own is now refused;
  - the stress a `.castep` geometry optimisation prints for its final configuration, or an MD run after it finishes, was dropped;
  - a constrained `.castep` optimisation stored the optimiser's `Constrained` stress and forces instead of the physical ones, and a single point with stress off and `iprint` 2 or more stored one term of the force breakdown (`Ewald forces`, …) as its forces; only the physical blocks are read;
  - an MD step's stress printed after its MD Data (CASTEP samples it every few steps) was dropped;
  - a run of another type (a phonon calculation, say) with the finite basis correction stored a `CASTEP FBSC` structure;
  - a dispersion-corrected (DFT-D) `.castep` run stored the energy without the correction its forces and stress include;
  - a `.castep` run's type could come from a parameter line, and its state carried into the next run in the file;
  - `.castep` atoms had Z = 0;
  - CASTEP `El:tag` species, VASP 6 POTCAR names on POSCAR line 6 and padded `vasprun.xml` element names were refused;
  - the CASTEP `.cell` writer fused columns;
  - `tadah data convert` crashed on an unrecognised or missing file;
  - `tadah data print -s` and `data write -s` failed;
  - steps of VASP's machine-learned force field in an OUTCAR (`ML_MODE = train`) were stored as DFT data, with energy 0 in an MD run; they are skipped with one warning, an MD step without its free energy is refused, and a last MD step the file ends inside after its free energy is skipped with a warning (it was skipped silently);
  - `tadah data dedup` removed every copy of a structure present three times;
  - every tool reading a dataset spun forever on a `nan`, `inf` or out-of-range cell entry, and an element symbol longer than two characters overflowed a buffer;
  - the predicted stress held only its upper triangle.
- `[hpo][mlip][core][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo` with `PC_FRMSE` and `FORCE false` (or no `FORCE` key) stopped with a segmentation fault** at its first evaluation; so did `MODEL M_KRR` with `PC_FRMSE` and `FORCE true`, and `PC_SRMSE` reported RMS(reference) for every candidate with `STRESS false` and, for `MODEL M_KRR`, also with `STRESS true`. The journal and `best_pot.tadah` headers labelled `ErRMSE` (a relative error times 1000) `meV/atom` and `SRMSE` (eV/Å³) `GPa`; they say `per mille`, and `SRMSE (GPa)` for `PC_SRMSE_GPA`, the stress metric in GPa (the BREAKING entry of the error scales). Validation now predicts forces for `PC_FRMSE` and stresses for `PC_SRMSE_GPA` whatever training fitted (see *Changed*), and the force metrics refuse a prediction without forces instead of reading it out of bounds.
- `[hpo]` The journal said `PC_ISOLATION: PROCESS requested but no PC_LAMMPS sims are configured` when `PROCESS` was only the default (e.g. a run with a `PC_OBSERVABLE` line and no `PC_LAMMPS` line); it now says `PC_ISOLATION: PROCESS (default) -- no PC_LAMMPS sims are configured, nothing to isolate`.
- `[hpo]` A bare `LOSS HUBER` / `LOSS TUKEY` used a zero parameter (Huber then scored every potential as 0). Root `LOSS`, `--varloss` and `PC_OBSERVABLE --loss` now share one set of defaults (Huber 50, Tukey 4.685) and reject non-positive, non-numeric or surplus parameters.
- `[hpo]` Misleading error messages: MULTISTART with a single-seed global backend, PRESTAGE without `EXPLORE` (it referenced removed syntax), and the CERES `STEP` hint (it named a `PARAM` the adapter rejects).
- `[hpo]` A configuration without `OPTIM` lines is rejected at startup for NLOPT, DLIB and CERES; negative numbers after `PC_LAMMPS` options are accepted as values; `PC_LAMMPS` lines keep their configuration order (the `--ncpu` sort that permuted them is removed with `--ncpu`).
- `[cmake-tools][core][mlip][hpo]` **About 205 unit tests never ran under CTest, and 13 CTest entries matched no test case yet passed.** The Catch discovery script split the test listing through a CMake list: CORE's `[low, high)` test name merged itself and the next 198 names into one entry, and names containing `;` (one in MLIP, five in HPO) were split in two. Discovery now reads names verbatim, gives each entry a quoted Catch test spec that selects exactly its case, passes `--warn NoTests` so that an entry matching nothing fails (and refuses `EXTRA_ARGS` with a second `--warn`, which Catch rejects), checks the listing against Catch's own count (which wraps modulo 256), and stops the build on a name Catch cannot select on its own (a leading or trailing `*`, a blank name, names differing only in case or surrounding whitespace, hidden test cases included). All 1,494 non-hidden test cases of the module suites are now registered exactly once, and all pass. `catch_discover_tests()` also passes its options exactly (`cmake -D` stripped a trailing space from `TEST_PREFIX`) and re-discovers the tests when the discovery script changes.
- `[lammps][core][umbrella]` **The `EAMFAST` fast path of `pair_style tadah` agrees with the generic kernel to 1e-9 in the range its log line states and exactly below it.** Each collapse table is certified at load down to the smallest distance above which every table segment meets a relative tolerance of 1e-9 for energy and force, the error of each segment bounded from 31 check points so that error peaks next to basis knots and switching-cutoff junctions are not missed; closer pairs are evaluated analytically. The log line printed a meaningless `parity dphi=3.071e+42` for the `D2_LJ` quickstart potential of 1.5.0 (the *Quick Start* potential of this release runs the generic kernel: the *Quick Start* entry under *Changed*) and now reads `Tadah! pair-collapse fast path ON (2b-only, nr=8192): table certified for r in [2.80125, 6.5] (rel tol 1e-9: energy 3e-13, force 1e-9); analytic below r=2.80125`. The tables were used at every distance, off by up to a factor of 130 at the shortest distances, and pairs within 8·10⁻⁴ Å below a `Cut_Dummy` cutoff got forces of about 20 eV/Å (both fast kernels). A table that misses the tolerance already in its top segment has no certified range; the log says so and the generic kernel is used. `ctest -R "fast path"` compares both fast kernels with the generic ones end to end (the EAM kernel had no such test).
