Docs: the *Quick Start* explains the log line, and the `EAMFAST` entry of the configuration keys describes the certified range and gives measured speed-ups (1.6x to 4.5x in 250-atom MD runs) instead of "typically 5-10x".
- `[mlip][umbrella]` `tadah` exits non-zero when a command fails.
- `[mlip]` A configuration file given with `-c` that holds only blank and comment lines, or nothing, runs on the command line's options; Tadah! 1.5.0 crashed on it (segmentation fault, exit status 139), with `tadah train -c` and `tadah analysis dataset_stats -c` alike.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo` stopped by Ctrl-C, `SIGTERM` or `SIGUSR2` exits with 128 + the signal number (130, 143, 140) instead of 0**, after writing the queued history rows and the `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)` journal trailer, with every optimiser and strategy. NLopt reported the signal as convergence, `MULTISTART` ran its remaining starts, `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` failed with an error when it arrived during their calibration, a signal during `NOISE_CALIBRATION` lost the queued rows and the trailer, and `DLIB:GFS` with `THREADS` > 1 aborted (exit 134, core dump). `PC_ISOLATION` worker processes ignore `SIGUSR2` as they did `SIGINT` and `SIGTERM`, so a signal sent to the whole job step no longer kills the evaluations in flight. An error keeps status 1. `ctest -L shutdown` signals DLIB BFGS and GFS, NLopt SBPLX, CERES, ASA, `ANNEAL_PT`, `ANNEAL_PA`, `MULTISTART`, `PRESTAGE` and `NOISE_CALIBRATION` runs, and the `tadah` binary itself.
- `[hpo]` **`RESTART` fires with NLopt backends, with `DLIB:GFS` at `THREADS` > 1, under `MULTISTART`, and under `PRESTAGE` with `TOPK` > 1**; it did not. A restart re-runs the whole strategy from the fresh points: the `MULTISTART` from its first start, or both `PRESTAGE` phases — an exploration without its own `INIT` block replayed its original points and never used the restart's. When the `RESTART` budget ends a run, its journal trailer says so (`STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in W evals after MAX restart(s)`), and a trailer no longer repeats a status an earlier phase left behind. A failure to save `best_pot.tadah` ends NLopt and `MULTISTART` runs as an error, and a concurrent signal or stagnation stop no longer hides it; an NLopt round-off-limited end is a graceful stop, and the final flush writes results behind a missing evaluation instead of dropping them.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A `PRESTAGE` refinement starts only from the exploration's usable losses.** A failed evaluation (`FAILSCORE`, a graded failure) stayed among the `TOPK` candidates while fewer usable ones existed, so a refinement seed could start at a failed point, and an exploration whose every evaluation failed so was refined from failed points only. With fewer usable candidates than `TOPK` the refinement runs from those and the journal says so (`PRESTAGE: 4 usable candidate(s) for TOPK 6 (the exploration made 6 evaluation(s), 2 without a usable loss); refining 4`); with none the refinement runs from the OPTIMIZER-level `INIT` points, as it did for an exploration without a candidate (one whose every evaluation a gate rejected before training: such an evaluation never was a candidate), and the journal says why (`PRESTAGE: no evaluation of the exploration produced a usable loss (9 evaluation(s)); refining from the OPTIMIZER-level INIT point(s) instead`, a `WARNING`). Docs: *Configuration File* (`STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A `PRESTAGE` exploration that asked for no point ends the run as an error** (`FAILED -- PRESTAGE: the exploration (EXPLORE block) LIB:ALGO returned without evaluating a point, so it has no candidate to refine (it asked for no point: ...)`, status 1), where the refinement ran from the `INIT` points after a journal `WARNING` (`PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`). Every exploration a configuration can set asks for at least one point (every optimiser makes its first call whatever `MAXTIME`, the next entry), so this is an internal error the refinement hid; an exploration whose every evaluation failed is refined from the `INIT` points as before. An exploration whose every call is answered from the run's record (*Changed*: a point the run has evaluated is answered from its evaluation) makes no evaluation and is no error: its recorded points are its candidates (a `RESTART` round that retraces an earlier one; under `NOISE CALIBRATION` an exploration whose one call, `MAXEVAL 1`, is the calibrated seed point). This entry said that every exploration evaluates at least one point, so one that made none ends `FAILED`, which the run's record made false. Docs: *Configuration File* (`STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Every optimiser makes its first evaluation whatever `MAXTIME`**, which keeps the later ones from starting. `ASA`, and `DLIB:GFS` without an `INIT` point, made none when `MAXTIME` had passed before the first (`MAXTIME 1e-9`, shorter than the optimiser's setup): a `PLAIN` run ended `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss` (status 1), and a `PRESTAGE` exploration had nothing to refine (the refinement ran from the `INIT` points after the `WARNING` `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`). The other optimisers made their first evaluation already. Docs: *Configuration File* (the `MAXTIME` row, `STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE`), *ASA* (stops).
- `[hpo]` **Each `RESTART` round draws new random numbers.** Restart *r* runs every optimiser that has a `SEED` with a seed derived from it — the `STRATEGY`-level one with `SEED + r·S`, *S* being the number of starts the strategy runs from `SEED` (`N_STARTS` under `MULTISTART`, `TOPK` under `PRESTAGE`, 1 under `PLAIN`), so no two starts of a run share a seed, and an `EXPLORE` one with `SEED + r` — and the journal's `RESTART r/MAX` line lists them. A restart kept every `SEED`, so a seeded stochastic optimiser or exploration repeated the first round's random sequence from its new point. The first round keeps `SEED`, so runs are unchanged up to their first restart. Docs: *Configuration File* (`RESTART`, `MULTISTART`, `PRESTAGE`, `SEED`).
- `[hpo]` **NLopt takes its random seed from the optimiser's `SEED`, and from the `INIT` block's `SEED` only when the optimiser has none.** The `INIT` `SEED` took precedence, so with both keys set the `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE`-refinement starts and the `RESTART` rounds of an NLopt optimiser all drew the same random numbers. The `INIT` `SEED` still draws the `INIT` points; the first round changes only for NLopt optimisers that set both keys. Docs: *Configuration File* (the two `SEED` rows).
- `[hpo]` **`SEED` inside an `INNER` block is a startup error that names the line** and the block to move it to (`… NLopt draws the INNER search from the enclosing run's random stream; set SEED on the enclosing STRATEGY block`). MLSL and AUGLAG run their `INNER` search inside the enclosing block's NLopt run, which has one random stream; an `INNER` `SEED` reseeded it in place of the enclosing block's seed, so the `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE`-refinement starts and the `RESTART` rounds all drew the same random numbers — every start of a `MULTISTART` `AUGLAG` with `SEED 20` on its `GN_CRS2_LM` `INNER` (a global search, which an `INNER` block no longer takes) drew the population of seed 20. A configuration that sets `SEED` in an `INNER` block now fails to load: move the `SEED` to the enclosing `STRATEGY`, `EXPLORE` or `REFINE` block, whose start and restart seeds then reach the `INNER` search. Docs: *Configuration File* (`INNER` block, `SEED`, `RESTART`).
- `[hpo]` **A multi-rank HPO launch (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) with `RESTART` fails at startup** (`multi-rank HPO does not support RESTART (…): stagnation is tracked per rank; run on one rank or remove RESTART`). Each rank tracked stagnation on its own, so at the first stagnation one rank restarted and waited in a collective while the others failed, and the job hung until the scheduler killed it. One rank keeps `RESTART`. Docs: *Configuration File* (multi-node runs and `RESTART`).
- `[umbrella]` **CLI examples 1 and 2 (*Examples*) run again.** Their `config.train` set `OALGO 3` with `BIAS true` and `LAMBDA 1e-7`, which training refuses (`OALGO=3 … does not support intercept exclusion from regularisation`): example 1 stopped with that error, and every evaluation of example 2's `tadah hpo` run failed, so it found no potential. They use `OALGO 1`. `ctest -L examples` runs every CLI example the documentation includes, as its README says (`tadah_cli_example_<name>`).
- `[cmake-tools][umbrella]` `tadah_get_git_branch` skips a CI or Read the Docs variable that is set but empty, as the docs build does; it took the first one set, even empty, and the modules were fetched at an empty ref. Both lookups now say so in their messages, and every `tadah_git_branch_*` case checks that they name the same ref.
- `[umbrella]` Docs: in a build without `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`, such as the Read the Docs one, the Doxygen input of the `docs` target ended in a bare `/include` (HPO's empty source directory); it now lists the `include/` directories of the configured modules only, and the HPO API is documented in a build with NFP (`cmake/doxygen_input.cmake`, `ctest -R tadah_doxygen_input`).
- `[umbrella]` Docs build: the ANNEAL key tables no longer fall back silently to HPO's `develop` branch on Read the Docs, and the GitLab `docs_sphinx` job finds HPO (it looked only for a sibling checkout). Missing Doxygen XML is one clear error instead of a warning per API directive, and the page generators run with Sphinx's own Python interpreter.
- `[umbrella]` **The published potentials `Tadah!Kr1`, `Tadah!Kr2` and `Tadah!N2` (*Trained MLIPs*) load again.** They were written in an older file format, which `tadah` and `pair_style tadah` reject (`Invalid key: ATOMS`, `Invalid key: CHECKPRESS`). The three files are re-issued in the current format with their weights and hyperparameters unchanged: `ATOMS` and `WATOMS` become `ATOM` and `WATOM`; `INIT3B`, `OUTPREC`, `MPIWPCKG`, `MBLOCK`, `NBLOCK`, `CHECKPRESS` and the empty covariance `SIGMA 0` are dropped; the Kr `D2_mJoin` descriptor takes three `TYPE2B` lines, as a newly trained potential writes it; the N2 descriptors state their grid sizes, `Lmax` and element pairs (`TYPE2B D2_Blip 4 4 * *`, `TYPEMB DM_Blip 1 4 4 * *`), and N2 gains `ATOM N`, which `pair_coeff` needs. `ctest -R "Published potentials"` loads the three files and checks that the Kr dimer energies and forces reproduce every row of the published tables `Tadah.Kr1.tab` and `Tadah.Kr2.tab`, and that N2 gives the energies `pair_style tadah` gives for two N2 molecules at 3.5 and 4.5 Å.
- `[mlip]` **`DIMER` models were trained and predicted with every interatomic distance set to zero** (`tadah train`, `tadah predict`), so every configuration had the same descriptors. `pair_style tadah` was not affected.
- `[models]` **A `TYPE2B` or `TYPEMB` line without the parameters its descriptor takes is an error that names the line**, in `tadah` and `pair_style tadah`. A line with too few tokens, such as `TYPE2B D2_Blip` (the form of older potential files), crashed (segmentation fault or abort): `TYPE2B D2_Blip takes 2 parameters before its element pairs; the line has 0: …`. A line with element pairs where the grid sizes belong, such as `TYPE2B D2_Blip * *` or `TYPEMB DM_Blip 1 N N`, applied the descriptor to no pair of elements, so the potential predicted wrong energies without an error: `… parameter 1, the number of CGRID2B values, must be a non-negative integer, not "*": …`.
- `[models][core]` **A DM_EAM model can be built.** DM_EAM reads its setfl file from `SETFL`, as documented and as D2_EAM does, so one `SETFL` serves the usual `D2_EAM` + `DM_EAM` pair. It took the path from its `TYPEMB` parameter, which the `TYPEMB` format admits only as an unsigned integer, so no configuration or potential file could build a DM_EAM model (`TYPEMB DM_EAM 0 Ta Ta` with `SETFL`: `EAMSetflIO: setfl file does not exist: 0`). The one `TYPE2B D2_EAM` / `TYPEMB DM_EAM` parameter is a placeholder, written 0; another value is reported on standard error once per process (`WARNING: TYPE2B D2_EAM parameter '12345' is ignored: …`; D2_EAM dropped it without a word). The `SETFL` key text and the D2_EAM and DM_EAM entries of *Descriptors* say so and give a configuration with both.
- `[models][core]` **D2_EAM and DM_EAM refuse an `RCUT2B` / `RCUTMB` other than the cutoff of their setfl file**, naming both: `D2_EAM: RCUT2B 3 differs from the cutoff 5.58190742024 of the setfl file 'Ta.eam.alloy' (SETFL); set RCUT2B to the file's cutoff, 5.58190742024.` They replaced the value with the file's cutoff and printed a WARNING (`Enforcing SETFL file cutoff: ...`), but `tadah train` fitted the weights with the descriptor evaluated at the configured cutoff while the potential file recorded the file's, so `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` evaluated a model that was never fitted, with no error; a cutoff above the file's failed with a neighbour-list error. A D2_EAM potential of 1.5.0 records the file's cutoff and still loads. A potential records `RCUT2B`, `RCUTMB` and the grids exactly whatever `NUMERIC` is (*Numbers keep full precision, and potential files are exact* under *Changed*). The D2_EAM and DM_EAM entries of *Descriptors* said that the descriptor enforces the file's cutoff and that setting it suppresses a warning; they and the `SETFL` key text say that `RCUT2B` and `RCUTMB` must be the file's cutoff.
- `[hpo]` **`STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART` with a single-seed global optimiser (NLopt `G*`, `TADAH:RANDOM`/`GRID`/`ANNEAL*`, `ASA`), `TYPE PRESTAGE` with `TOPK` > 1 and such a refinement optimiser, and `TYPE PRESTAGE` without `EXPLORE` are startup errors**, raised before any data is loaded. They surfaced once the run was under way — under `PRESTAGE` after the whole exploration, and only when it had found more than one candidate. `PRESTAGE` has a message of its own (`… or TOPK 1 to refine the best candidate once`), and the listed alternatives are sorted. These rules are checked as soon as the configuration is read, before the worker pool is sized and before any `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker is started, and the journal records a startup error on an `ERROR` line (`ERROR     startup: <message>`); it went to stderr only.
- `[hpo]` **An optimiser without `LIB` or `ALGO` (the `STRATEGY`-level one, the `PRESTAGE` refinement, an `EXPLORE` or `INNER` block) is an error naming the block**, reported at the line that closes it (`ENDINNER` for an `INNER` block, which the message places: `the INNER block inside STRATEGY names no optimiser: ALGO is not set; …`); it failed at run time with `unknown optimizer NOT SET:NOT SET`, and an `INNER` block without `ALGO` inside NLopt. This is the rule of `tadah hpo`; the `tadah refit` stage blocks need `ALGO`, and `LIB` only where the `ALGO` does not name the optimiser (*Refitting*).
- `[hpo]` **An `INNER` block needs an optimiser that runs one (NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG), and `LIB NLOPT`**: both are startup errors naming the optimiser the block belongs to (`DLIB:BFGS does not accept an INNER sub-optimiser (the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser): …`). The first was reported only once the host had loaded the data; an `INNER` block with another `LIB` failed inside NLopt once the run had started (`wrong algorithm string`).
- `[hpo]` **With `PC_LAMMPS`, `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`, `LD_AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG_EQ` with a derivative-free `INNER` block are accepted**: an MLSL or AUGLAG is judged by its local search, which takes no gradient there; they were refused as gradient-based for the `D` of their name. Without an `INNER` block (NLopt's default `LD_MMA`) they are still refused, naming it. *Optimizer* documents it.
- `[hpo]` **The `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` gradient rules hold in the `PRESTAGE` `EXPLORE` block and its `INNER` block too**: with `PC_LAMMPS`, a gradient-based optimiser other than `DLIB:BFGS` or a `GRAD_STRATEGY` there is an error at startup (`--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS but EXPLORE's optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS is gradient-based. …`), and so is `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` there with `PC_OBSERVABLE`. They were checked at the `STRATEGY` level only: `NLOPT LD_LBFGS` in `EXPLORE` failed at once inside NLopt and the run reported success after a wasted exploration, and a `GRAD_STRATEGY` there failed after the first evaluation.
- `[hpo]` **`N_STARTS`, `PARALLEL`, `BUDGET_PER_START` outside `TYPE MULTISTART` and `TOPK` outside `TYPE PRESTAGE` are errors naming their line**; they were silently ignored. `TYPE` may come before or after them in the `STRATEGY` block. A `TYPE` line after an `EXPLORE` or `REFINE` block, which in 1.5.0 took it out of `PRESTAGE` (the run silently became `PLAIN`), is refused as a second `TYPE` line (a key of the target file that takes one value is set once), and a second `STRATEGY` block is an error at its line; it reopened the first and could change its `TYPE`.
- `[hpo]` **Numbers in the hpotarget file are parsed strictly**: an integer key takes decimal digits only (no sign, fraction or exponent) within its range, a real one a whole finite number, and every key exactly one value; anything else is an error naming the line (`Error on line 6: MAXEVAL must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647, got '1e5'`), and a value outside its key's range names the value (`PC_EMAE <weight> must be a number >= 0, got '-1'`). Prefixes were read (`5x` as 5), non-numbers stored 0 (`FAILSCORE abc` made every failure score 0, `MAX abc` switched `RESTART` off), negative values wrapped, missing values left the default or read an uninitialised variable, and extra tokens were ignored. This covers the root, `STRATEGY`, `RESTART`, `INIT` and `NOISE` keys, the optimiser attributes, the `OPTIM` bounds and the `PC_LAMMPS` / `PC_FAILPROBE` options; an unparsable `--hotvar` initial or `--failvar` scale is no longer passed on to LAMMPS. `THREADS` must be at least 1 in every optimiser block: `THREADS 0` was accepted, and the pool quietly ran one worker. `MAXEVAL` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block (below), and so are `BUDGET_PER_START` and `tadah refit`'s `MAXITER`: NLopt counts evaluations in an `int`, and a larger `MAXEVAL` wrapped (`MAXEVAL 4294967299` ran 3 evaluations). `INIT METHOD LHS` and `SOBOL` take `K` from 1, in the same message. The integer flags of `PC_OBSERVABLE` (`--supercell`, `--maxiter`, `--maxeval`) take no sign either: `--maxiter -0` was 0. `MAXTIME` in an optimiser block must be a positive number of seconds (`MAXTIME must be a positive number of seconds, got '-5'`); leave it out for no limit. 0 or a negative value meant no limit to NLopt and `TADAH` and a startup error to ASA and the annealers. `STEP` values and numeric `PARAM` values must be finite numbers at their line (`STEP inf` and `PARAM fd_step inf` failed only once the run had started), and a `STEP <KEY>(indices)` override takes the strict index grammar of the `OPTIM` lines (`STEP CGRID2B(3x) 0.3` applied to every index). `tadah refit`'s `EXPLORE MAX_ADD` is an integer ≥ 0 (a negative value silently meant no cap; leave it out for no cap). Every real number has one grammar, the decimal one LAMMPS reads (an optional sign, digits with at most one decimal point, an optional exponent), for every key, the `PC_LAMMPS`, `PC_FAILPROBE` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` options and the `tadah refit` blocks included: a hexadecimal float is an error (`FAILSCORE must be a finite number, got '0x1p40'`; it loaded as 1.0995·10¹², and `STEP 0x1p-3` as 0.125, while `PC_<METRIC>` rejected it), and so is a number below the smallest normal double (`1e-320`, which `PC_<METRIC>` accepted, as LAMMPS does), also where denormals-are-zero is on. Configurations that relied on the lenient reading now fail at startup.
- `[hpo]` **A `PC_LAMMPS` option takes only its own values**: a token after them (`--maxtime 1:30 00`) is an error naming the option (`--maxtime takes a single value (unexpected '00')`); it was passed to LAMMPS as a command-line argument, and every evaluation failed on it. The LAMMPS variable `maxtime` is reserved for the script timeout (`-var maxtime`, `-v maxtime`, `--invar maxtime` are errors); such a value replaced the limit unchecked while the journal reported `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT`.
- `[hpo]` **`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` / `MAXTIME` / `--maxtime` are LAMMPS time specs** (`off`, `unlimited`, `[[HH:]MM:]SS`: a strict subset of what `timer timeout` reads, to the same seconds), checked at startup, and the `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker deadline derived from them uses the parsed seconds: `2:00:00` gave a 122 s deadline and `00:05:00` none. `unlimited` means no limit, like `off`; it made every multi-section script fail as timed out. A limit of 0 or less is an error at its line (`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT must be a positive time, 'off' or 'unlimited' (a limit <= 0 stops every script), got '0'`); it made the evaluations score `FAILSCORE`. A worker deadline above 2³¹ − 1 ms (about 24.8 days) is kept: it wrapped, so the worker was killed early or not at all; one that does not fit in 64-bit milliseconds (a script timeout above about 3.07·10¹⁵ s, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` above about 9.2·10¹⁵ s) is an error at its line.
- `[hpo]` **A `tadah refit` stage's `OPTIMIZER` block rejects the keys the stage does not use**, naming the keys it reads (`REFINE: THREADS is not used by a tadah refit stage (it reads LIB, ALGO, MAXEVAL/MAXITER, FTOL_REL, FTOL_ABS, GTOL)`); `THREADS`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `POPULATION`, `STEP`, `PARAM` and an `INNER` block in `REFINE`/`FUSE` were accepted and did nothing (FUSE's `GTOL`, accepted and ignored before, is read now). `SEED` is rejected with a hint to `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED`. Every stage `OPTIMIZER` block and `INNER` block must set `ALGO` (`NONE` to switch a search off): an `INNER` block without `ALGO` silently ran no local search. Every error of a stage's block names it (`REFINE: FTOL_REL must be a finite number, got '0x1p-40'`); a malformed number named no block. *Refitting* lists the keys of each stage.
- `[hpo]` **`MAXEVAL 0` is an error in every optimiser block**, and in `tadah refit` `MAXEVAL 0` and `MAXITER 0`: `MAXEVAL` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 (`MAXEVAL must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647, got '0'`). It meant no limit to NLopt, no evaluation to `TADAH:RANDOM`, the annealers and ASA, the first starting point to `DLIB:GFS`, an error when `DLIB:BFGS` and `CERES` started, nothing to `TADAH:GRID`, and no iteration to the refit's Ceres stages. Leave the key out for no evaluation limit; *Configuration File* says how each backend then stops.
- `[hpo]` **A backend with no convergence test and no finite schedule needs a stop**: `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, a tolerance it honours set above 0, or an active `RESTART` (whose stagnation stop ends the run once its `MAX` restarts are used; `GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND` need `MAXEVAL`, see *Changed*), a startup error otherwise that names the backend and the stops it honours (`NLOPT:GN_DIRECT needs a stop: … set MAXEVAL, MAXTIME, STOPVAL or a positive tolerance it honours (FTOL_REL, FTOL_ABS, XTOL_REL, XTOL_ABS); DIRECT's XTOL_REL and XTOL_ABS end it only once every rectangle is that small, after roughly (1/XTOL_REL)^n evaluations in n dimensions`). A tolerance of 0 or below ends nothing (NLopt switches it off), so `FTOL_REL 0` is no stop. Run without one, NLopt's global searches (the DIRECT family, `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ISRES`, `GN_ESCH`, `GN_AGS`, StoGO, the MLSL family), `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ`, `LD_SLSQP`, `ANNEAL_PT`, `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` ran until they were killed; they were accepted, and `RANDOM` and `GFS` needed `MAXEVAL` itself. An `INNER` search takes the enclosing block's `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME` and `STOPVAL` (NLopt replaces an `INNER` block's own `STOPVAL`, so `STOPVAL` inside `INNER` is an error at its line, below). *Configuration File* has the table of each backend's stops.
- `[hpo]` **`TADAH:RANDOM`, `TADAH:GRID` and `DLIB:GFS` honour `MAXTIME` and `STOPVAL`**, which they accepted and ignored: no evaluation starts after `MAXTIME`, and the first loss at or below `STOPVAL` ends the search. The journal says which stop ended it, and the stop is the run's status (`TADAH:RANDOM: STOPVAL reached`); a search whose last evaluation spends `MAXEVAL` or the grid ends on that (`TADAH:GRID: MAXEVAL reached`, `TADAH:GRID: all grid points evaluated`; see *Changed*), even when `MAXTIME` has passed by then. They reject a `PARAM` they do not read, naming what they take (`TADAH:RANDOM: unknown PARAM 'FOO' (it takes none)`), and `GFS` rejects `FTOL_REL`, `XTOL_REL` and `XTOL_ABS`.
- `[hpo]` **`tadah hpo` refuses at startup an optimiser key the backend never reads**, naming the phase and the stops the backend honours or the backends that read the key: a tolerance its run never tests (`TADAH:RANDOM`, `TADAH:GRID`, NLopt `GN_ESCH`, `GN_AGS`, `GD_STOGO*`, all but `XTOL_REL` of `GN_ORIG_DIRECT*`, and MLSL or AUGLAG with an `INNER` block), `POPULATION` or `VECTOR_ARR` on a backend without them, `SEED` on `TADAH:GRID` and `CERES`, `STOPVAL` inside an `INNER` block (at its line), and `GRAD_STRATEGY` on a phase whose optimiser takes no gradient. They were accepted and did nothing. `DLIB:GFS`'s `SEED` keeps its warning, and NLopt takes `SEED` on every algorithm. A tolerance refused on MLSL or AUGLAG with an `INNER` block is sent into that block only when its algorithm tests it; otherwise the message names the stops that search honours. *Configuration File* (the `OPTIMIZER` keys, *Stop criteria per backend*, the `INNER` block) and *Optimizer* say so.
- `[hpo]` **NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take a gradient only when their local search does**: the `D` of `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`, `LD_AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG_EQ` only picks NLopt's default local optimiser, `LD_MMA`, which an `INNER` block replaces. With a derivative-free `INNER` block they were treated as gradient-based: `GRAD_STRATEGY` and `PARAM fd_step`, `fd_kernel` and `diff_method` were accepted and never used, `NOISE` `AUTO` derived a step nothing used, the journal listed them, and with `PC_OBSERVABLE` an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` resolved to `ANALYTICAL`, so startup required the analytical gradient for a run that takes none. Such a phase is now derivative-free: those settings are refused at startup, naming the search that takes no gradient (`NLOPT:GD_MLSL_LDS takes no gradient: NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take a gradient only for their local search, and the INNER block's NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA takes none`). *Configuration File* (`GRAD_STRATEGY`, the finite-difference `PARAM`s, `PC_OBSERVABLE`) says so.
- `[hpo]` **NLopt `AUGLAG`, `AUGLAG_EQ`, `G_MLSL` and `G_MLSL_LDS` without an `INNER` block, or named in an `INNER` block, are refused at startup** (`NLOPT:AUGLAG needs an INNER block (the PRESTAGE refinement): NLopt has no default local optimiser for it; ...`); NLopt refused them once the run had started, under `PRESTAGE` after the whole exploration (`local optimizer must be specified for AUGLAG`), or inside `INNER` with only `nlopt invalid argument`. *Configuration File* (the `INNER` block) documents it.
- `[hpo]` **A single-dash option LAMMPS refuses on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line is an error at its line**, with LAMMPS' message (`PC_LAMMPS: LAMMPS cannot start with this line's command-line options: ERROR: Invalid command-line argument: -sff (...)`; also an option the build cannot honour, such as `-sf omp` without the `OPENMP` package). The line was accepted and every evaluation failed to create its LAMMPS instance. *Configuration File* (`PC_LAMMPS` options) documents it.
- `[hpo]` **An unknown `--` option on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line is an error at its line**, naming the options the line takes and the closest one (`PC_LAMMPS: unknown option '--hotvars' (did you mean '--hotvar'?): ...`; a `PC_FAILPROBE` line's leave out the `--varloss`, `--hotvar` and `--failvar` it refuses). LAMMPS has no `--` option: it was passed to LAMMPS, every evaluation failed (`Invalid command-line argument: --hotvars`) and the run spent its whole budget. Single-dash options still go to LAMMPS. *Configuration File* (`PC_LAMMPS` options) documents it.
- `[hpo]` **BREAKING — `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` is refused** in every phase (the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, `MULTISTART`, the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, `INNER`): at `ENDOPTIMIZER` for the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, whether the `STRATEGY` block, a `REFINE` block or the `OPTIMIZER` block itself names it, at `ENDEXPLORE` for the exploration and at `ENDINNER` for an `INNER` block, naming the phase and `LN_BOBYQA` (`LN_NEWUOA` is refused too: the BREAKING `LN_NEWUOA` entry under *Changed*) instead (`Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND is refused: NLopt 2.10 plans each LN_NEWUOA_BOUND step with a nested LD_MMA run that has no time limit and sees no stop request, ...`). It was accepted, and NLopt 2.10 stalls in it once a non-finite value reaches its model: each step then takes about 10–60 s without evaluating the loss. That happened after every stop `tadah hpo` makes through the objective from a run's (2n+1)-th evaluation on, n being the number of `OPTIM` dimensions, as the objective then returns `HUGE_VAL`, NLopt's convention for a forced stop (NEWUOA first evaluates 2n+1 starting points, and a stop on one of the first 2n returned at once; Ctrl-C waited about 10 s; with `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION` each stagnation stop was followed by about 20 s without an evaluation), and on a flat loss without `XTOL` or near a minimum (a run made 300 evaluations in 5 s, then 20 in 590 s, and `MAXTIME 20` ended it after 31 s). `LN_BOBYQA` and `LN_NEWUOA` return at once after the same stop. A study moved to `LN_BOBYQA` takes another trajectory (`STEP` rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius). The `MULTISTART` alternatives list one backend fewer. *Configuration File* (the `ALGO` key, the `NLOPT` row of *Algorithms by library*) and *Optimizer* (*Scaling, steps and tolerances by backend*) document it; HPO's `docs/issues/nlopt_newuoa_bound_stall.md` has the analysis and the NLopt-level repro.
- `[hpo]` **`BUDGET_PER_START` with `DLIB:GFS` under `MULTISTART` is an error** (`BUDGET_PER_START has no effect with DLIB:GFS, which runs every start in one optimiser; use MAXEVAL`); it was ignored.
- `[hpo]` **`TADAH:GRID` honours `MAXEVAL`**: it stops after `MAXEVAL` points in grid order (the `INIT` point, then the grid nodes) and says so in the journal (`GRID: MAXEVAL 5 < 28 grid points (the INIT point, then 27 = 3^3 grid nodes); evaluating the first 5 in grid order`); it ignored `MAXEVAL` and evaluated the whole grid.
- `[hpo]` **Each backend's attribute and `PARAM` checks run at startup**, before any data is loaded or any evaluation runs, on the settings each phase runs with: the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, each `MULTISTART` start (with its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL`), the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, and every `INNER` block (`HPOEngine error: the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not support MAXTIME`). They ran when that optimiser started: after the data were loaded, for a `PRESTAGE` refinement after the whole exploration, and under `MULTISTART` each start failed with a warning and the run ended without an evaluation. The annealers, ASA, `DLIB:BFGS`, CERES and `TADAH:GRID` reject a `PARAM` key they do not read, and a word where `TADAH:GRID` takes a number (`PARAM GRID_POINTS AUTO`) is now an error too (it was ignored); `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` reject every `PARAM`, and NLopt passes every `PARAM` to NLopt.
- `[hpo]` **A `tadah refit` stage block that runs an optimiser names the library of its `ALGO` in `LIB`** (`NLOPT`, `DLIB` or `CERES`), as in `tadah hpo`: a missing `LIB` (`REFINE: LIB is not set; set LIB NLOPT (ALGO LD_LBFGS is an NLopt algorithm)`) and one that does not match the `ALGO` are errors, which also name the stage's algorithms of the `LIB` given when it has any (`REFINE: LIB NLOPT does not match ALGO LBFGS, a dlib algorithm; set LIB DLIB, or with LIB NLOPT set ALGO LD_LBFGS`); `LIB` could be left out where the `ALGO` named the optimiser, and a mismatched one was ignored. The `ALGO` names stay the refit's own (`GLOBAL` for dlib's global search, `LBFGS` for dlib's L-BFGS). With `ALGO NONE` or `UNIFORM`, which run no optimiser, `LIB` is an error, and so is a stop criterion that no stage reads (`REFINE: MAXITER has no effect with ALGO NONE (the stage is skipped); remove MAXITER`); `UNIFORM` took any `LIB`, and those keys were ignored. *Refitting* has the table of each stage's `ALGO` and `LIB`.
- `[hpo]` **`tadah refit`'s EXPLORE optimiser refuses `ALGO GN_MLSL`, `GN_DIRECT` and `GN_CRS2`**, naming the algorithm it runs (`EXPLORE: ALGO GN_MLSL is not run by tadah refit (NLopt's MLSL with pseudo-random starts); its MLSL is GN_MLSL_LDS: set ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS`). They ran `GN_MLSL_LDS`, `GN_DIRECT_L` and `GN_CRS2_LM`, while `tadah hpo` runs `GN_MLSL` and `GN_DIRECT` as named and `GN_CRS2` is no NLopt algorithm. The short names `MLSL`, `DIRECT` and `CRS2` stay (*Refitting*).
- `[mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **The numbers of a `tadah refit` configuration are read strictly, with their ranges, before the EAM file is read** (one reader for both modes, MLIP's `read_refit_numbers`): the number grammar of the hpotarget file or decimal digits, and `REFIT_TOL_AMBIENT`/`REFIT_TOL_COMPRESSED`, `REFIT_COMPACT_SHARE`, `OUTPUT_GRID_SCALE`, `OPTIM_PCT` (also with `--edit` and `--retarget`) and `REFIT_REACH_MARGIN` > 0, `REFIT_REACH_SMIN` from 0.1 to 1 (`0.01` took about a minute, `1e-6` did not finish), `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` in (0, 1], `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC` in (0, 1), `REFIT_CURVE_DERIV_WEIGHT` >= 0, the compact counts from 1 (`REFIT_COMPACT_JITTERS` 0 to 2147483646), `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED` 0 to 4294967295, `N2B`/`NRHO_BASIS`/`NF_BASIS` 1 to 1024 and `REFIT_MAX_ESCALATIONS` from 0: `tadah refit: REFIT_TOL_AMBIENT must be a positive number, got '0'` (*Refitting*). An `OUTPUT_GRID_SCALE` whose `refit.eam` grid a setfl table cannot have (fewer than 3 or more than 2147483647 points) is refused once the table is read, before any fitting. They were read unchecked: `REFIT_TOL_AMBIENT 0` made the energy metric infinite, `OUTPUT_GRID_SCALE 0`, `0.0001` or `1e7` wrote a 2-point `refit.eam` (exit 0), `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY 0` and `INSERT_TOPK 0` silently became 1, `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED 3000000000`, a valid seed, failed converting, `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` went through `std::stoi`, and a `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` above 536870911 overflowed the sample-grid size (now refused: the grid of 4 × NMAX points must fit an int). A configuration file still passes the type check of every configuration key first, which refuses `.5` in every number and a sign (`+3`) in an integer.
- `[core][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah refit` refuses a key that its `REFIT_MODE` does not read**, before it reads the EAM file and before the numbers, naming the key, the mode in effect and the mode that reads it (`tadah refit: N2B has no effect under REFIT_MODE compact (the default): only REFIT_MODE dense reads it; remove N2B, or set REFIT_MODE dense`): under `REFIT_MODE dense` every `REFIT_COMPACT_*` key, `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING`, `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX`, `REFIT_CURVE_DERIV_WEIGHT`, `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve` and the `INIT`/`EXPLORE`/`REFINE`/`FUSE` blocks; under `REFIT_MODE compact` `REFIT_LEVEL`, `N2B`, `NRHO_BASIS`, `NF_BASIS`, `REFIT_MAX_ESCALATIONS`, `REFIT_PLACEMENT` and `REFIT_CONSTRAIN_F0`; and under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy` `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING`, as its help said, `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` and `REFIT_CURVE_DERIV_WEIGHT`, which `curve` alone reads, and `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` with `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING false` (`tadah refit: REFIT_CURVE_RELAX has no effect under REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING false: only REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING true (the default) reads it; ...`). A default `tadah` stores for a key the configuration does not set is not refused. These keys were ignored: a dense run never read its stage blocks (a `REFINE` block with `THREADS 4` and `GTOL -1`, which compact mode refuses, ran), a compact run never read the dense keys. A top-level `OPTIMIZER` block, which neither mode reads, is refused first in either mode, before the EAM file is read, with its migration text (`tadah refit: a top-level OPTIMIZER block is no longer supported. ...`; under dense it ends `Under REFIT_MODE dense, which reads no stage block, remove the OPTIMIZER block.`); dense mode ignored it, and the compact fitter refused it after reading the table. `REFIT_MODE` is read in any letter case and another value is an error (it ran the dense fit after a WARNING, `Dense` and `COMPACT` included). **BREAKING for builds without the compact fitter:** a `tadah` built without the compact fitter (without `TADAH_ENABLE_NFP`, or with it and `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` off) refuses `REFIT_MODE compact`, written or the default (`tadah refit: REFIT_MODE compact (the default) needs the compact fitter, which this tadah was built without ...; set REFIT_MODE dense`), where it fitted the table in dense mode after a WARNING: such a build needs `REFIT_MODE dense` in every refit configuration. After a compact fit the log no longer gives the dense fit's advice (`raise REFIT_MAX_ESCALATIONS, pin a larger N2B/NRHO_BASIS/NF_BASIS`; `REFIT_PLACEMENT`): it names `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` or `EXPLORE MAX_ADD`. The `F` row of `refit_range_report.dat` no longer describes the windowed dense fit's uniform tail basis after every fit: after a compact fit it says that `refit.eam` carries the original table above the embedding window, after a dense fit with `REFIT_PLACEMENT uniform` that one uniform basis is fitted on the full range. The `tadah refit --help` text and the `OPTIMIZER` key's examples no longer point to `HPO_REFIT_2B_EAM/REDESIGN/configs`, which is not shipped, and neither does the migration text of a top-level `OPTIMIZER` block. *Refitting* ("Keys of the other mode") describes the rule; the configuration keys state their mode.
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` **`tadah refit --edit` and `--retarget` refuse a key of a refit of an EAM file**, which they never read, before a file is read or written, naming the key and what reads it (`tadah refit --edit: N2B has no effect under --edit: only a refit of an EAM file in REFIT_MODE dense reads it; remove N2B`): the keys of either `REFIT_MODE` (`REFIT_MODE`, `REFIT_BASIS`, `REFIT_TOL_*`, `REFIT_REACH_*`, `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC`, `REFIT_OBJECTIVE`, `OUTPUT_GRID_SCALE`, `SETFL_OUT`, `REFIT_LOG`), of one mode (`N2B`, `REFIT_COMPACT_*`, ...), the stage blocks and a top-level `OPTIMIZER` block; under `--retarget` also `HPOFILEOUT` (`--hpofileout`), which `--edit` reads, and `NUMERIC`, the precision of the potential a refit and `--edit` write, as `--retarget` writes only an hpotarget, whose bounds are printed `%.8e` whatever `NUMERIC` is. The defaults `tadah` stores for the keys a configuration does not set are not refused. They were ignored: `tadah refit --retarget refit.hpotarget --hpofileout new.hpotarget` wrote `refit_edited.hpotarget`, and an `--edit` configuration with `N2B 50` edited the potential as without it. *Refitting* (`--edit`, `--retarget`).
- `[models][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **`refit_range_report.dat` gives the window each fit used**: the `phi` and `rho` rows' `fit_window_lo` is the compact fit's `r_lo` (max(0.8 r_reach, `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC` rcut, dr), the row's `full_lo`) or the dense fit's window (from the edge of a steep wall in the table, at most 0.8 r_reach and never below `r_lo`: `r_lo` for a table without one), and `refit_curve_{phi,rho,F}.dat` flag `in_window` on the same windows. Both gave 0.9 r_reach after every windowed fit, neither fit's window (on the test table 1.718 A, where the compact fit starts at 1.527 A and the dense fit at 1 A, or at 1.75 A with `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC 0.35`), and under `REFIT_PLACEMENT uniform` the curve files flagged every row, from 0.15 rcut (below the fit's `r_lo`, 0.2 rcut with the default `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC`). `EAMRefit::fit_window_r_lo()` and `fit_window_rho_hi()` (Tadah.MODELS) give the windows. *Refitting* (*Output files*).
- `[core][mlip][umbrella]` **BREAKING — `tadah refit --retarget` writes `<input>_retargeted.hpotarget` by default**, a name no default output of `--edit` or of a refit has. It wrote `<input>_edited.hpotarget`, which for `refit.hpotarget`, the hpotarget every refit writes, is `refit_edited.hpotarget`, the hpotarget `--edit` writes by default: of an `--edit` and a `--retarget refit.hpotarget`, two commands, the second overwrote the first one's file without a word, and the two are searches around different potentials (the edited one and the input). `-o` and `--in-place` are unchanged. *Refitting* (`--retarget`).
- `[core][mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **`REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE`, `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` and `REFIT_COMPACT_INSERT_TOPK` are refused under `REFIT_BASIS Blip` or `Blip5`**, before the EAM file is read, naming the key and the basis (`tadah refit: REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE has no effect under REFIT_BASIS Blip5: only the knot families (REFIT_BASIS Knot5, the compact default, Knot3 or KnotMix) read it; remove REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE, or set REFIT_BASIS Knot5`): the compact fitter applies them to the knot families only, and they were accepted with no effect (a Blip fit has no coalescence guard, optimises every rung and inserts at the curve peak). The default `REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE auto` is not refused, and `refit_opt.log`'s schedule line says `merge_guard=off` for a Blip fit (it said `on` under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy`); the fits are unchanged. *Refitting* (*Compact ladder*, *Keys of the other mode*).
- `[models][umbrella]` **The dense `tadah refit`'s `RHOFORM NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` table pre-check reads the fit window**, never below its inner bound, below which no fit samples the table (with a steep wall the window starts above the bound, and the table between, which the fit solves in a separate tail block, is not checked, as before): with 0.8 r_reach below `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC` × rcut (a small `REFIT_REACH_SMIN`, a large `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC`) it read table nodes below that bound, which no fit samples, and refused a table that is negative or rises only there (`RHOFORM MONOTONE cannot fit this table: ... inside the fit window [1.527351, 5.000000] A` for a table the fit follows on [1.75, 5] A). The compact fit's check is unchanged. *Refitting* (*Density form*).
- `[core][models][umbrella]` **The text of `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC` and of `EAMRefit::phi_setfl`/`z2_setfl` says what `refit.eam` carries below the inner fit bound**: the original table's phi and rho, each shifted by the constant that joins it to the fit there (rho clamped at 0 under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE`), as the WARNING of `tadah refit` says; they said a smooth C2-matched repulsive (exponential) continuation, which `tadah refit` does not write. *Refitting* (*Output files*, `REFIT_R_LO_FRAC`) describes it, the bound (at least the table's dr; max(0.8 r_reach, the bound) in compact mode) and that `pot.tadah` is valid from it out only.
- `[hpo]` **A `tadah refit` stop criterion that its block's `ALGO` does not read is an error** naming the `ALGO` and what it reads (`REFINE: GTOL has no effect with ALGO LD_LBFGS (it reads MAXEVAL, FTOL_REL); remove GTOL`); `XTOL_REL` with an `INNER` dlib `LBFGS` was accepted and had no effect (but under `GN_MLSL_LDS`), and `GTOL` with an L-BFGS REFINE had none on REFINE, but under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve` it was the FUSE curve refine's gradient tolerance (see the curve-mode entry). `MAXITER` is Ceres' (`LM`, `DOGLEG`) only; with an L-BFGS REFINE it set the evaluation budget and now names `MAXEVAL`. dlib's `LBFGS` reads `FTOL_ABS`, the absolute change of the objective it stops at (as `tadah hpo`'s `DLIB:BFGS`), and `FTOL_REL` there, which it took for that change, is an error naming `FTOL_ABS`; under `GN_MLSL_LDS` an `INNER` dlib `LBFGS` block's `FTOL_REL` still sets MLSL's local `LN_SBPLX` searches, and only them, so such a run without `FTOL_ABS` gives different results. REFINE or FUSE with `ENABLED false` and an `OPTIMIZER` block is an error; the block was ignored with a warning. The refit log's `optimiser:` line gives only what each `ALGO` reads (*Refitting*).
- `[hpo]` **`tadah refit` `MAXEVAL` counts objective evaluations exactly, for every NLopt and dlib algorithm, so refit results change** with an explicit `MAXEVAL` for EXPLORE dlib `GLOBAL` (it took `max(500, MAXEVAL/20)` calls), dlib `LBFGS` in EXPLORE `INNER` and REFINE (it capped L-BFGS iterations, 50 × `MAXEVAL` of them in REFINE) and REFINE `LD_LBFGS` (it took 50 × `MAXEVAL` evaluations, computed in an `int` that overflowed above `MAXEVAL 42949672`). Ceres' `LM` and `DOGLEG` count iterations, as `MAXITER` says. Without `MAXEVAL`, `GLOBAL` and REFINE `LD_LBFGS` keep their budgets (3000 and 50000 evaluations); dlib `LBFGS` keeps the numbers of its old iteration caps (20000 in EXPLORE `INNER`, 50000 in REFINE), now counted in evaluations, so a default run whose cap binds stops sooner. *Refitting* has the table of what each stage counts.
- `[hpo]` The journal prints a `PRESTAGE` `EXPLORE` or refinement block without `MAXEVAL` as `MAXEVAL=none` (no evaluation limit); it printed `MAXEVAL=0`.
- `[hpo]` **An ASA run no longer stalls, deaf to Ctrl-C and `SIGTERM`, when a periodic test follows a new best.** ASA resized its block of generated states to 0 there and then looped at full CPU without evaluating: neither `MAXEVAL` nor a signal could end a serial run, which only `kill -9` did, and a block-parallel run (`THREADS` or `Gener_Block_Max` above 1) ended only at its `MAXTIME`. How often a run stalled depends on the objective; at the default options 6 of 30 seeds on the HPO termination-test fixture stalled at their first periodic test (after 100 acceptances). The block now keeps at least one state, and runs that did not stall are unchanged. *Adaptive Simulated Annealing (ASA)* no longer says that `Gener_Block_Max` equal to `Gener_Block` freezes the block size: the resizing can shrink the block below `Gener_Block`, which can leave block-parallel workers idle.
- `[hpo]` *Adaptive Simulated Annealing (ASA)* says where ASA starts: from the first `INIT` point (without an `INIT` block, the configuration's values), and only when the `INIT` chain yields no point from a random state around the OPTIM box centre (`User_Initial_Parameters 0`). It said that without an `INIT` block the box centre was used.
- `[hpo]` **A stop ends an ASA run while ASA samples its cost temperature or a random start.** After `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, a signal or an error raised by an evaluation, ASA's sampling of the initial cost temperature (`Number_Cost_Samples`), of a random start (`User_Initial_Parameters 0`, the default without an `INIT` seed) and of the `Reanneal_Cost` resampling (above 1 or below -1) drew states that were not evaluated until `Limit_Invalid_Generated_States` ran out (1000 by default, then `TOO_MANY_INVALID_STATES`); with `PARAM Limit_Invalid_Generated_States -1` it spun for ever, deaf to Ctrl-C, until `kill -9`. The run now ends at the stop with `IMMEDIATE_EXIT`, as it does in ASA's main loop; *Adaptive Simulated Annealing (ASA)* says so under *Stops honoured*.
- `[hpo]` `PC_LAMMPS` runs no longer leave `tadah_pc_<pid>_<n>.in` files in the temporary directory when a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker is killed at its hard deadline, crashes inside LAMMPS or is killed with its parent: a rendered script is unlinked as soon as it is created and read through `/proc/self/fd`.
- `[hpo]` **The analytical gradient of a `PC_OBSERVABLE` run matches the loss.** It left out the observable's own dependence on the descriptor parameters at fixed weights and summed the observable terms instead of averaging them with the validation metrics, so on the Ta model of the HPO tests it pointed away from the optimum (dL/dRCUT2B 61.8 against −0.0097 from central differences); it now agrees with central differences to about 0.1 %. A `PC_OBSERVABLE` run without `--validation` has an analytical gradient too.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`DLIB:GFS` no longer crashes (SIGSEGV) when its evaluations fail.** dlib was handed each failed evaluation's `FAILSCORE`, by default the largest double, and its trust-region step crashed once the first *dims* + 2 evaluations had all failed, whether or not later ones would have produced usable losses (CLI example 2 with its old `OALGO 3`, from `MAXEVAL` 12). dlib is now given a finite stand-in for a failed evaluation: the worst usable loss of the search so far plus a margin, or, before any usable loss, `FAILSCORE` capped at 1e100 (*Configuration File*: `FAILSCORE`, and `DLIB:GFS` under *DLIB:BFGS PARAM keys*). A stand-in is not revised, so graded failures keep their order only among the stand-ins handed over in the same state of the search. `loss.tadah`, the journal and `best_pot.tadah` keep the loss the evaluation reported, and the journal notes the stand-in once per `DLIB:GFS` search.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo` exits with status 1 when no evaluation produced a usable loss**, with `[HPO] ERROR: no evaluation produced a usable loss; no best_pot.tadah was written` on stderr and in the journal, and the trailer `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss` (*Configuration File*, *Stopping a run*). It exited with 0 and `COMPLETED`, although no `best_pot.tadah` was written. A signal (128 + N) or an error still decides the status first; runs with a usable loss end as before. In a multi-rank run (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) the run has a usable loss when any rank produced one, and every rank reaches that agreement however its run ends, so a rank whose final flush fails leaves no other rank waiting.
- `[hpo]` The journal's DIAGNOSTICS count every evaluation without a usable loss (`Unusable losses`), and the verdict follows that count: it printed `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss` whenever no failure was graded, right after `100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss` or for a run whose every loss was a positive `FAILSCORE`. It now says `[!] No evaluation produced a usable loss.` when none did. In a multi-rank run each rank's journal counts that rank's evaluations, and its verdict names the rank (`[!] No evaluation on MPI rank 1 (of 2) produced a usable loss.`). A `FAILSCORE` of 0 or below, which left no failure band, is refused at startup (*Changed*).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A gradient-based search that a point without a usable gradient ended says so**: `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil` in the journal trailer, and a warning that names the backend and the failed evaluation (the stencil point, or the loss at the point). A failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil fails the gradient (next entry); before, with the default `FAILSCORE`, it made the gradient not finite; `DLIB:BFGS` reported `MAXEVAL=<n> reached` and `COMPLETED`, NLopt `LD_*` success or `roundoff-limited`, the CERES drivers `COMPLETED`, `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` (and `LD_AUGLAG`'s default local search) asked for the same point until `MAXEVAL`, and `AUGLAG` with an `INNER` `LD_LBFGS` reported `FTOL_REACHED`. `DLIB:BFGS` ends at such a gradient; NLopt and Ceres still reject such a point and step back, and the status applies when that ends the search: at the starting point of a local NLopt search (an `LD_*` algorithm, or an `AUGLAG` whose local search is one), or when the search ends right after the rejection with neither `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME` nor `STOPVAL` met (a tolerance reached right after it counts as the rejection's ending; `MLSL` and `GD_STOGO` go on past a rejection). The run exits with 0 when an evaluation produced a usable loss, and with 1 (no usable loss) otherwise; a signal or an error decides first (*Configuration File*: *Points without a usable gradient*, *Stopping a run*). Test: `tests/cli_failed_evaluations` (`fd_straddle`, `failscore_zero`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A setting of the `OPTIMIZER` block that `tadah hpo` refuses at startup is named with the line of its block**, as a parse error is: `HPOEngine error: Error on line 17: the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not support MAXTIME` and `Line: ENDOPTIMIZER` (`ENDEXPLORE` for the `EXPLORE` block, `ENDINNER` for an `INNER` block), for every backend's checks (the CERES settings Ceres cannot take, `DLIB:BFGS`, the annealers, a backend that needs a stop, ...), the `STRATEGY` rules and a phase's finite differences or a `GRAD_STRATEGY` on a phase that takes no gradient (for the check of the `ANALYTICAL` setup and the other refusals that still named none, see the next entry). They named the phase and no line. Docs: *Configuration File* (the startup checks, stops, `MULTISTART`, the `INNER` block, the finite-difference box check: the quoted refusals with their line), *ASA*.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **More refusals of an `OPTIMIZER` block's settings name the line of their block**: the check of the `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` setup (`HPOEngine error: Error on line 20: OPTIMIZER block: GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL ... needs the analytical gradient of the loss, ...` and `Line: ENDOPTIMIZER`; `EXPLORE block: ...` at `ENDEXPLORE`), the `STEP` rules (`DLIB:BFGS (OUTER) does not use STEP; ...`, a `STEP` outside `(0, 0.5]`, overrides that cover only some dimensions), the `PC_OBSERVABLE` and `PC_LAMMPS` gradient rules (`--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS but EXPLORE's optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS is gradient-based. ...`) and the refusal of a multi-rank launch (`multi-rank HPO supports only ...`). A search without an `OPTIM` line is refused for every phase whose optimiser is NLopt, DLIB or CERES (`Error on line 14: the PRESTAGE exploration (EXPLORE block): NLOPT:GN_DIRECT cannot optimise a zero-dimensional search space ...`); only the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser was checked, without a line, so an `EXPLORE` block's NLopt optimiser or `DLIB:GFS` passed startup and failed when it started (`NULL args to nlopt_optimize_`, dlib's `The vectors can't be empty`). Docs: *Configuration File* (the startup checks, `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL`, the multi-rank launch, the journal's message table).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A refusal of an `INIT` or `RESTART` block's `METHOD` line names that line**: `HPOEngine error: Error on line 3: WARM: cannot open seed file 'missing.tadah'. ...` and `Line:     METHOD WARM missing.tadah`, also for an unreadable `WARM` file, a `WARM` seed whose `RHOFORM` the run refuses, a `WARM` seed that `WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` refuses (`WARM: 1 parameter(s) not found in seed.tadah: RCUT2B`) and a `SOBOL` block beyond the sequence; they named no line. Refusals that still name no line of the file: a starting point outside its `OPTIM` box or not finite, `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` without a finite positive `FAILSCORE`, and the bump-support and density gates (they name their keys). Docs: *Configuration File* (the startup checks).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A failed evaluation fails a gradient, whatever `FAILSCORE` is, aware of `FAILSCORE_GRADING`**, in every gradient backend (`DLIB:BFGS`, NLopt `LD_*` and the local search of `MLSL`/`AUGLAG`, the CERES driver) and under every `GRAD_STRATEGY`. Only a loss that was not finite, or a difference that overflowed, failed a finite-difference gradient, the analytical strategies did not read the loss, and `tadah hpo` clamps every failure to a finite `FAILSCORE`: the difference of two failures was an exact 0, and the analytical gradient at a failed point is that of the loss before the failure, small but not 0, so a start inside the failure region looked converged (from 1.25 in a one-dimensional search failing below about 1.47, `PARAM fd_step 0.2`, before this change: `DLIB:BFGS` 8 evaluations and no warning, `LD_LBFGS` `SUCCESS -- generic success` after 4, `LD_MMA` the same point and its stencil until `MAXEVAL`, the CERES drivers `CONVERGENCE` at their first iteration, a `MULTISTART` start drawn there ending without a word and leaving the trailer `COMPLETED`; under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` — the default under `PC_OBSERVABLE` — or `HYBRID` with nothing differenced, the same endings at `FAILSCORE`), and under a finite `FAILSCORE` a failed stencil point gave a slope as steep as the jump (`FAILSCORE 1e9` from 1.6: `DLIB:BFGS` evaluated the bound 28 times in a row, 60 evaluations in all). Now the loss at the point decides: at a usable loss a failed stencil evaluation is not differenced -- the gradient takes the other side, and fails when no side is usable (the BREAKING entry on differences of one kind, above); at a failed loss (`FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`, `FAILSCORE` itself, a loss that is not finite) the gradient fails at once, and the journal names the loss (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: … (the loss at CGRID2B[2] = 1.25 is 1.797693135e+308)`: one evaluation); at a graded failure under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` the differences take graded failures only, a direction out of the failure region, unless every one is 0 (a flat failure region). `ANALYTICAL`, and `HYBRID` before its analytical pass, read the loss the optimiser gives too: a failed one fails the gradient at once (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point (the loss at CGRID2B[2] = 1.25 is 1.797693135e+308)`, the trailer `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point`), and so does a graded failure under `ANALYTICAL`, while `HYBRID` differences every parameter there (the entry on a graded failure's analytical gradient). From 1.25 a local search (`DLIB:BFGS`, `LD_*`, an `AUGLAG` whose local search is one, a CERES driver) stops after one evaluation; `MLSL` and `GD_*` reject the start and go on. The `TADAH_FAILSCORE_GRADING` override reaches the gradient too. A search that touched a failure from a usable point takes the other side of the stencil (from 1.6 under `FAILSCORE 1e9`: 1.6, 1.8, 1.4, then 2.0, as under the default), and only a point whose stencil fails on both sides has no gradient. An evaluation with a failed term (a `PC_LAMMPS` simulation, a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term) is a failed one (entries above), not a usable mean of its terms below `0.999 × FAILSCORE`. At a graded failure next to the usable side the difference takes the graded side only (the same entry): under `FAILSCORE 1e9` with `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` from 1.4, `DLIB:BFGS` reaches the bound 2 in 6 evaluations, `FTOL_ABS reached`, `LD_SLSQP` after 6, roundoff-limited, `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` `FTOL_REL reached` after 30 at 1.9854. Docs: *Configuration File* (*Points without a usable gradient*, the `FAILSCORE` and `GRAD_STRATEGY` rows, *Failure handling — graded FAILSCORE*), *Optimizer*. Tests: `tests/cli_failed_evaluations` (`fd_inside`); HPO's `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (the straddle problem from 1.4).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` a candidate a gate rejects before training is a point without a usable gradient, not an error.** Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` a candidate the bump-support gate (`BUMPGATE CANDIDATE`) or a density gate rejects scores a graded failure, and an `ANALYTICAL` gradient search that reached one ended the run `FAILED -- GRAD_STRATEGY=ANALYTICAL: no analytical gradient for 2 parameters at this point ...` (exit 1; NLopt `LD_*`, `CERES:LBFGS`, `WOLFE`, `NLCG`). Such a point now has no usable gradient (`the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`): NLopt and Ceres step back from it and go on, as from any point without a usable gradient. `HYBRID` takes finite differences there, as before. Docs: *Configuration File* (graded failures, gradients at failed evaluations).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` a point whose fit the analytical gradient cannot differentiate is a point without a usable gradient, not an error.** Where the normal equations of the fit (Φᵀ Φ + `LAMBDA` I over the training rows) are not positive definite in the gradient's implicit-function solve — the fit's own factorisation of them succeeded: the two differ at rounding level on a numerically singular matrix, with a small `LAMBDA` — an `ANALYTICAL` run ended `FAILED -- GRAD_STRATEGY=ANALYTICAL: no analytical gradient for N parameters at this point ...`, and nothing said why. Such a point now has no usable gradient (`the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`), as a candidate rejected before training has, and the journal says why in a `WARNING` at the first such point and then at the 2nd, 4th, 8th, … (`Analytical gradient could not be formed at this point (occurrence 1): the implicit-function solve has no solution at this point: the normal equations of the fit (Phi^T Phi + LAMBDA I over the training rows, LAMBDA = 0) are not positive definite ...`); an error in the gradient's computation is reported the same way (it was written to the journal and to stderr at every gradient). `HYBRID` takes finite differences there, as before. Docs: *Configuration File* (`GRAD_STRATEGY`, gradients at failed evaluations).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` draw random numbers of their own for their `SEED`.** They drew the generator the starting-point methods `RANDOM` and `LHS` draw, seeded the same way, so equal seeds in two blocks gave the same numbers: with `INIT { METHOD RANDOM 1, SEED 2 }` and `TADAH:RANDOM` with `SEED 2` the first two evaluations were the same point (likewise in a `PRESTAGE` exploration with its own `INIT` block), and with `SEED 5` in the `RESTART` block (whose `SEED` is new in this release) and on `TADAH:RANDOM` every restart round evaluated its fresh point twice (the documentation advised different values). `TADAH:RANDOM`'s and `ASA`'s random numbers now come from a stream derived from their `SEED` and a salt of their own, as the `TADAH` annealers' did; NLopt keeps its own generator, seeded by `nlopt::srand`; the `INIT` and `RESTART` blocks' points are unchanged. A `TADAH:RANDOM` or `ASA` run with a given `SEED` therefore takes another path than before. Docs: *Configuration File* (the `SEED` row, the seeds of a restart round), *ASA* (seeding).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A `PRESTAGE` refinement draws random numbers of its own, not its exploration's.** Its optimiser drew the exploration's numbers for an equal `SEED`: with `TADAH:RANDOM` and `SEED 7` in both blocks the refinement evaluated the exploration's points again, and under `RESTART` the seeds derived for a round met for `SEED`s that differ (`SEED 7` and `EXPLORE SEED 8`: round 1's refinement ran with round 0's exploration seed). The refinement now seeds every random stream of its optimiser (`TADAH:RANDOM`, `ASA`, the `TADAH` annealers, NLopt's generator) with a key derived from its `SEED`; the journal still names the `SEED`, and every other run draws as before. Docs: *Configuration File* (the `SEED` row, the seeds of a restart round).
- `[hpo]` `PARAM fd_kernel inhouse` selects the in-house finite-difference kernel under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`, as documented; it was ignored there.
- `[lammps][models][umbrella]` **LAMMPS now warns when a DM_REAM potential has no `EMBF0` line (BUG-08).** Such a potential is evaluated with the default `EMBF0 ZERO`. For a potential written by tadah 1.5.0 or earlier that moves every atomic energy by -F(0), and for a non-linear model forces change as well. Every `pair_coeff` that loads such a potential prints a LAMMPS `WARNING`, on screen and in log.lammps, with the same text as the tadah notice (add `EMBF0 FREE` to reproduce the old energies), and the banner says `key absent`. Rebuild `lmp` and `tadah`. Docs: `descriptors.rst` (DM_REAM).
- `[models][hpo][umbrella]` **Knot-channel RCUT is set exactly to the largest knot** in train, predict, `refit --edit` and every hpo evaluation (a value within 1e-9 relative was kept, so train/predict and hpo could run slightly different cutoffs). `pair_style tadah` reads the stated value: for a knot potential written by <= 1.5.0 under a smooth cutoff a WARNING says LAMMPS differs (~1e-13 relative) until the potential is re-written (train, `refit --edit` or hpo).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **`tadah hpo` evaluates optimiser start points exactly.** CONFIG and WARM values and MULTISTART/RESTART/PRESTAGE seeds are evaluated at exactly their loaded doubles; the real → internal → real map of `OPTIM` dimensions rounded them (3e-11 relative for a small weight in a wide box), so even a `MAXEVAL 1` WARM rescoring was not exact. NLopt `BOBYQA`/`COBYLA`/`PRAXIS`, which round the start before evaluating it, are covered by a window of 4 ulp of the internal box around each seed, except `PRAXIS` under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` on a box reaching 2^24 (1.7e7): its first evaluation can land a few ulp of the box off a seed of smaller magnitude than the box. `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` runs are therefore no longer byte-identical to 1.5.0. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (search-space scaling: starting points).
- `[mlip]` **`Structure::save` writes every column exactly.** Its z force column held the y component, and weights, energy, cell, stress, positions and forces had 6 digits; every double now has 17 significant digits, so `save()` → `read()` round-trips bit-exactly, and `save(path)` throws when the file cannot be written. Public API only (no CLI caller).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A best potential that cannot be saved fails `tadah hpo` with a non-zero exit code; transient filesystem errors are retried first.** Under NLopt such a failure ended as `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged; ...)`, exit code 0, with a stale `best_pot.tadah`. Every optimiser and policy now propagates it: `Optimiser status : FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <error>`, and RESULT names the lost candidate and what `best_pot.tadah` holds. Transient errors (EBUSY, EAGAIN, ESTALE, ETXTBSY, EIO, ENOLCK, ETIMEDOUT, EDEADLK; EACCES on network filesystems) are retried with backoff for up to 30 s for the best potential, and 10 s per writer drain for the per-evaluation logs and archives (not fatal: lost rows are reported); after a shutdown signal for at most 2 s. Once a save has failed for good, waiting candidates get a single attempt each. Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (*Potential archives*, *Stopping a run*).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **Compact refit: the joint refinement (FUSE) keeps every bump inside the hard cutoff, in every density form.** It optimises the radial bumps in their outer support edges mu + w/eta, bounded at the cutoff, with a width floor, so no bump ends past the cutoff or with a negative width and the post-FUSE projection no longer undoes the energy–volume fit (a synthetic table: `SIGNED` went from a failed crystal self-test to 3.3 / 8.3 meV/atom with the density within 4e-3 of the table). Compact `SIGNED` refits can differ where the joint refinement used to leave a bump past the cutoff. Docs: `refit.rst` ("Compact mode").
- `[mlip]` `refit --retarget` recovers a lower-bound-clamped line with its own group's percentage, so repeating a per-key retarget (`--pct 5 SGRIDMB=50`) is a fixed point.
- `[hpo]` Test infrastructure: `HPO_Host::reset_globals_for_test()` rewinds the result-row writer and `HPOLog::reset_for_test()` resets the gate counters, timing and fan-out statistics, so e2e cases no longer depend on what ran before them in the process.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` `tadah refit --retarget`: a per-key percentage on an `RCUT2B`/`RCUTMB` line is recorded in a group comment above the line, so `--pct 5 RCUT2B=50` run twice is byte-identical (the second run read the line's centre with the file's 5 % and gave `[3.571, 10.714]` for `[2.5, 7.5]`). `--pct` and `--set` numbers are read with the hpotarget number grammar (`5x`, `0x10`, `inf`, `nan` and `1e-320` are errors; they were read as 5, 16, inf, nan and a subnormal). `--pct KEY=N` for a key without an `OPTIM` line is an error (it was ignored). A `--set` needs finite bounds with `lo < hi`, and its `lo` must satisfy the file's density form (`--set 'AMPGRIDMB(2)=nan,0.2'` passed the `lo < 0` test). The error for a `RHOFORM` header naming no density form gives its line.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` `tadah analysis check_bounds` requires `RHOFORM` for a DM_REAM seed (a seed without it was audited without the density rules), applies the Gaussian `SGRIDMB` rule under `NONNEG`, and audits each corner in the context `tadah hpo` evaluates: `OPTIM CGRIDMB (3)` on a five-entry grid set only entry 3, where it replaced the grid by three copies of the corner value (and `(1)` by one).
- `[mlip][umbrella]` `tadah train`, `tadah refit` and `refit --edit` stop with an error naming the file, and a non-zero exit status, when the potential or the hpotarget cannot be written; `tadah train -o no_such_dir/pot.tadah` logged an ERROR and exited 0.
- `[mlip][umbrella]` `tadah analysis bfunc` refuses an `EMBF0` of its configuration file that differs from that of the potential its `POTENTIAL` line names (`EMBF0 conflict: pot.tadah declares EMBF0 ZERO and the configuration file sets EMBF0 FREE. ...`), as `tadah refit --edit` does: `EMBF0` is a property of the potential. Tadah! 1.5.0 took the potential's and ignored the configuration's without a word.
- `[models][hpo][mlip][umbrella]` **The density form's OPTIM low-bound rules follow the basis**: under `NONNEG` an `SGRIDMB` box of a Gaussian density needs a low bound > 0, as DM_REAM requires, in `tadah hpo` (startup) and `tadah analysis check_bounds`. `OPTIM SGRIDMB (1) -0.5 2.0` on a Gaussian `NONNEG` seed passed startup, and every candidate with a width <= 0 then failed when its model was built.
- `[models][hpo][umbrella]` **WATOM is read with one number grammar** by the model and by the density checks and HPO density gates (the decimal grammar of potential files); the gates also read `RCUTMBMAX` that way. `0x1p-1` was a weight of 0 in the model and 0.5 in a gate; it is now an error on both paths.
- `[models][umbrella]` A DM_REAM `TYPEMB` basis code that is not an integer (or is absent) is an error instead of basis code 0 (Blip), or the integer prefix of `2.5` (closes the open issue "DM_REAM falls back to basis code 0"; only Contexts built in code could hit it).
- `[models][hpo][umbrella]` Non-negative least squares no longer reports convergence when it stopped only because every column with a dual above the tolerance was refused; `tadah refit`'s WARNING names the reason, and the compact (HPO-build) refit counts its coefficient solves that stopped before a Kuhn-Tucker point and reports them at the end of the ladder.
- `[lammps][umbrella]` `pair_coeff * * pot.tadah W` for an element the potential does not have stops LAMMPS through `error->all` with the reason (`ERROR: pair_coeff: failed to initialise Tadah potential 'pot.tadah': Symbol not in potential file: W`), so it reaches `log.lammps`; the exception escaped `pair_coeff` and left no log entry. A failed driver `load(Context)` no longer prints its reason to stderr as well (`pair_coeff * * EXTERN` reports it). *Quick Start* says so.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` `tadah hpo` refuses a `-c` configuration whose model does not build at startup, with the reason (a `DIMER` potential whose `TYPE2B` names element pairs, say); it printed a WARNING, or nothing, and every evaluation failed ("no evaluation produced a usable loss"). The bump-support gate names a channel whose grids cannot be read instead of skipping it.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` `tadah hpo` reads an active RESTART block's `METHOD WARM` seeds at startup: a missing or unreadable file, or one whose `RHOFORM` the run refuses, stops the run before the first evaluation, not at the first stagnation, and the density gates check restart seeds as they check INIT seeds.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` A second `tadah hpo` run in the same process no longer prints the first run's bound-occupancy table, and prints its own once-per-run notes (the DLIB:BFGS MAXEVAL preamble, the gradient diagnostics) and counts (FAILPROBE "total gated=N", pc-worker failures) from 1.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` `BUMPGATE`'s reject line prints the violation losslessly (`violation=0.4166666666666667`; below 5e-7 it read `0.000000`).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` The `--hotvar` reject line prints the hot value and its window losslessly (`rejecting (hot_a0=4.500000000000001 outside [2.5, 4.5], ...)`; a value just above the max read as the max itself).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` Compact refit under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve`: FUSE refines the radial bumps in their outer support edges bounded at the cutoff, as the energy-mode FUSE does, so it never ends worse than its start (the bump-support projection undid it: a test curve went from 0.0948 to 0.1020).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A negative tolerance in a `tadah refit` stage block is an error** at its key (`REFINE: GTOL must be a number >= 0, got '-1'`): `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `XTOL_REL` and `GTOL` are finite numbers >= 0 (0 switches the test off). A negative one was accepted, Ceres refused it and returned without iterating, and the refit logged that as the stage's stop term (`refine=FAILURE(0it, Invalid configuration...)`), so REFINE and FUSE did nothing and the run exited 0. The compact fitter's Ceres solves throw when Ceres refuses their options, before they change the fit (*Refitting*, the numbers paragraph).
- `[mlip]` **Non-orthogonal CIF and COD structures had a transposed cell** (the reader copied gemmi's column-vector matrix untransposed; hcp Mg got a 2.78 Å neighbour). The atoms were right; the cell is now too, in `tadah predict -s`, `tadah data print|write -s` and the exports.
- `[mlip]` The AFLOW reader places the atoms from `positions_fractional` in the cell it builds (the Cartesian positions are in AFLOW's own frame), and refuses an entry without `geometry` or `positions_fractional`, with mismatched species, composition and position counts, with a malformed site, or with angles no cell has.
- `[mlip]` POSCAR: Cartesian coordinates are scaled like the lattice, a negative scaling factor is the cell volume, three factors scale x, y and z, and the coordinate mode and Selective dynamics are read from the first character, as in VASP; an invalid scale line, a missing element-symbol line (VASP 4 layout) and a symbol/count mismatch are refused.
- `[mlip]` CASTEP `.cell`: block unit lines (`bohr`, `nm`, …) are applied, fractional positions are converted after the whole file is read (block order no longer matters), text after `!`, `#` or `;` is a comment, and a missing, incomplete or singular lattice, a missing positions block or an unknown unit is refused.
- `[mlip][hpo][umbrella]` **Neighbour lists missed periodic images** of cells thinner than the cutoff in some direction, of triclinic cells and of atoms outside the cell, so `tadah train`, `tadah predict`, `tadah analysis` and `tadah hpo` used wrong descriptors for such structures (a perfect bcc crystal got forces) and disagreed with `pair_style tadah`. `NNFinder` now finds every image of any cell (a cell list sized by the cell heights over a reduced lattice basis, each pair tested once), and its list is symmetric and ordered by (neighbour, shift). Thick cells with atoms inside the cell get the same pairs within the cutoff as before (a lattice shell exactly at the cutoff may now also be listed; consumers ignore it). A neighbour-cache hit after a cell change (the native relaxer, HPO's cohesive-energy observable) reused the image displacements of the old cell (E_coh off by 0.06–0.08 eV/atom in the tests' setups). `tadah analysis dataset_stats` counts neighbours strictly inside the cutoff. Tests compare `NNFinder` with an independent brute-force search on thin, tilted, sheared, skewed, unwrapped and 200 random cells, and `tadah predict` and the published Kr potentials with thick supercells.
- `[hpo]` A candidate outside the `OPTIM` box whose cutoff exceeded the neighbour lists (built once for the box) was scored with the short lists; no point outside the box is evaluated now (the BREAKING entry on points outside the `OPTIM` box, *Changed*).
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **A usable loss at or below `STOPVAL` that NLopt was not given ends the search `STOPVAL_REACHED`**: NLopt is given `+inf` for a point whose gradient `MAXEVAL` cut short or that is not finite, so it never tested that point's loss against `STOPVAL`, and the search went on past it (or ended with `STOPPED -- no usable gradient` at a local search's start). A usable loss (not a failure under `FAILSCORE`) at or below `STOPVAL` now ends the search there, and the journal says which loss and why (`NLopt finished: STOPVAL_REACHED -- … the loss 24.2 is at or below STOPVAL 100, and NLopt was not given it: its point has no usable gradient: …`). Docs: `nfp_configuration.rst` (*Points without a usable gradient*).
- `[umbrella]` Correct cutoff formula and normalization descriptions and clarify the permitted self-terminating NONE case; a test pins f(0) and f(r_cut) of every cutoff to the documented table (`Cut_Tanh`'s f(0) = tanh(1)^3).
- `[models][mlip]` **`refit --edit` of a potential with a smooth `RCTYPE2B`/`RCTYPEMB` edits the tapered curve**: its curve views and the `--remove` compensation include the cutoff and its product-rule derivatives (the compensation fitted the untapered basis, so under a smooth cutoff the residual it minimised and reported was not the curve's). *Refitting* (*Editing a refitted potential*).
- `[mlip]` **`tadah analysis bfunc`**: `--rescale --derivative` prints the product rule f_c g' + f_c' g (it printed f_c g'); a knot basis with mixed degrees (`KDEG2B`, `KDEGMB`) is analysed with its degrees and without widths; a DM_REAM density is printed in its declared form (the steps of a `MONOTONE` density).
- `[models]` **The bounds `tadah analysis suggest_bounds` suggests for the widths of `D2_Blip`, `D2_Blip5` and `DM_Blip` are inverse distances** ([1/s, 10/s] for the pair, [1/w, 6/w] for the density): they were Gaussian bounds, which scale as 1/length² (for the quick-start `D2_Blip`, `SGRID2B` 0.064 to 6.4).
- `[core][mlip]` **`tadah refit --rcut2b` and `--rcutmb` need `--retarget`**, and a refit configuration that sets an option of `--retarget` or `--edit` is refused (`tadah refit: the RETARGET_RCUT2B key requires --retarget <file.hpotarget>.`): on a refit of an EAM file they had no effect, without a message (also in 1.5.0). *Refitting*.
- `[umbrella]` **Docs: a long field name wraps.** The name column of a field list takes at most 30% of the list's width, and a longer name, such as "Template Parameters" on the API pages and on *Descriptors* and *Regressors*, wraps. On one line it pushed the field body out of the text column, and those pages scrolled sideways on narrow windows.
- `[umbrella]` **Docs: the *Configuration File* page says that `tadah hpo` reads the file.** It said that nested fitting does not use the configuration file and has a format of its own; `tadah hpo --config` takes the same training configuration and trains every potential of the search from it.

## [1.5.0] — 2026-09-10

### Breaking
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **BREAKING — `PARAM` value vocabulary is `ON` / `OFF` / `AUTO`.** Numeric booleans (`CORANA 1`) and `0`-means-disabled (`SWAP_WINDOW 0`) are gone across the whole OPTIMIZER block, and `BOUNDARY_MODE` became the enum `REFLECT | REDRAW`. `0` had drifted into meaning three different things on neighbouring keys. Old spellings are hard errors naming the replacement.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **BREAKING — every index a person reads is 1-based**, in the sidecar TSVs and `best_pot.tadah`'s `# HPO_SOURCE_SLOT` header as well as `hpo_run.log`. Internally they stay 0-based (they are cache and checkpoint identities), so the conversion happens at the I/O boundary. Scripts parsing a slot number must subtract one.
- `[hpo][umbrella]` **BREAKING — `ANNEAL_PT` ladder-scales the initial jitter by default** (`PARAM INIT_JITTER_LADDER AUTO`): slot *k* is displaced by `sigma*(T_k/T_HOT)^0.5` instead of every slot sharing one sigma, so a warm start survives at the cold end. Changes the starting population of existing PT runs at a fixed `SEED`; `OFF` restores the previous placement exactly.
